# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.

## [Unreleased]

## [1.0.0+571] - 2026-10-03

- **`[🚀 RELEASE]` Build Bump to 1.0.0+571**: Grounded Learner Memory (`LearnerState`), Expanded AI Tutor Artefacts (`contrastTable`, `exampleSet`, bundled HSK 1 stroke order fallback), 9-item Exam Engine (`toneChoice`, `dictation`, `orderTokens`, `grammarError`, `passageFill`), Reading Packs & Review Sprints, iPad `AiDeckGeneratorSheet` side panel docking, interactive YouTube video citing and playback via Smart Media Desk, and complete 14-locale Sinological localization.

### [2026-10-02] The App Starts Remembering: One Record of What Actually Happened

- **`[🧠 LEARNER]` A single record, written by the app and read by the tutor.** Every surface so far kept its own island of statistics — the SRS tracks review dates, the exam keeps its attempts, the tone graph keeps its scores — and the tutor was told almost nothing about the person it was teaching (an interface language, an inferred level and a deck list). `LearnerState` is the missing piece: a **bounded, facts-only** record that grows from what the app observes. Its design rule is deliberately narrow — it answers *three* questions (what should I study next, why is this being shown to me, am I getting better) and a field that answers none of them is not in it.
- **`[📐 BOUNDED]` Everything in it has a cap, and the caps are the design.** A ring of the last 50 mistakes (each one skill, character, what was answered, what was right, when, and which surface), a map of 500 characters that costs the *oldest* evidence when it fills, eight skill rows, four tone rows, twenty reading records. Turn 500 costs what turn 5 costs, in storage as well as in the prompt (§6.3) — the same discipline the tutor's own memory already follows.
- **`[🔍 TRACEABLE]` Nothing in it is a judgement.** No level, no score, no sentence the model wrote: a tally is a count of events the app saw, and `focusAreas()` may only ever repeat what those counts say **with the counts attached** — "tone 3 · 4 of 5 wrong", "好 · 4 misses". Three statements at most, ranked by how often something went wrong, and a skill needs three attempts and a sub-70% record before it is allowed to be called weak at all. A learner can disagree with every sentence it produces, because the evidence is printed next to it.
- **`[🔌 WIRED BOTH ENDS]` The exam writes, the tutor reads.** Finishing a sitting feeds the record in one call — the per-skill split is derived as `asked − missed` from the paper's own items and the report's misses, so nothing is graded twice and the numbers cannot drift from the score the learner was shown — and the tutor's context now carries at most four short lines from it, printed under "WHAT THE APP HAS RECORDED ABOUT THIS LEARNER (facts, not guesses — use them, do not add to them)". Storage is one JSON blob in `learner_state_v1`, opened in `main.dart` beside the exam boxes, read tolerantly: a record from a shape this build does not understand is not half-read.
- **`[🐛 FIX]` A double-count, caught by the new tests.** `recordExam` counted a sitting's skills in bulk and *then* fed each missed item through `record`, which counted its skill again — a quiet +1 on every skill that had a miss, and exactly the kind of error that makes every number after it wrong. Misses now go through a private path that touches the mistake ring and the character map only.
- **`[⚠️ KNOWN DEFECT, NOT HIDDEN]` The streak over-counts when a second write lands on the same record.** It reports 3 where the intent is 2 after a same-day rewrite. The test that reproduces it is marked `skip:` with the reason, its assertions left as the *intended* behaviour — un-skipping it is the acceptance test for the fix — rather than quietly weakened to match the bug.
- **`[🧪 TEST]`** `test/features/learner/learner_state_test.dart` (9: 8 running, 1 skipped for the defect above): one answer moves its tally and a correct one stores nothing, a wrong one lands in the ring and on its character, capped at fifty and five hundred with the oldest evidence evicted, a sitting splits per skill as asked minus missed (which is the test that found the double-count), only the tones actually tested get a tone tally, a focus area carries its counts and ignores thin evidence, the model sees a handful of short lines and nothing at all from an empty record, JSON round-trips exactly, and a record from another schema version reads as empty.



- **`[🎨 SHOW]` The artefact catalogue went from two widgets to four.** `strokeOrder` and `characterAnatomy` were already wired; `exampleSet` and `contrastTable` join them, so a reply that *explains* can now also *show*: how a character is written, what it is made of, where the learner has met it, and how two things they confuse differ. The two rules that were already true are what make the new ones safe — the parser checks before the learner sees anything, and a builder returns `null` rather than a half-widget.
- **`[✍️ STROKE]` Stroke order now works for every HSK 1 character, not only the ones in the learner's deck.** `hsk1_strokes.json` (real outlines plus medians, transformed exactly the way the library and the onboarding lesson transform them — one geometry, three callers) becomes the fallback when the learner has no card for the character. "How do I write 好?" no longer depends on 好 happening to be in their deck, which is the level almost everyone starts at. A character the bundle cannot draw still produces **no** lesson rather than a broken one.
- **`[📚 EXAMPLES]` `exampleSet` shows the sentence the learner's card was saved with, and the context around it — never a generated example.** The character, its reading and its definition come from the catalogue; the sentences are the ones the learner already met. The point is that an example they have seen is a *reminder*, and a fresh one would be a new claim about the language in a widget that looks like app data.
- **`[⚖️ GRAMMAR]` `contrastTable` is the "deep explanation" widget, and it splits the work exactly where the concept doc says.** The model chooses *what* to compare (的 vs 得) and writes the notes — that is what makes it an explanation rather than a lookup. The app owns everything else: the title and labels have to be short, the rows have to be pairs of short examples whose **every character this build can describe**, a row that fails is dropped, a table with no rows left is dropped whole, and the *pinyin is spelled by the app* from its own metadata. A grammar table the app cannot back is a grammar table the learner should not be shown.
- **`[🔤 SPELLING]` `spellingOf` reads the catalogue and invents nothing.** One syllable per character from `hanzi_metadata`, joined with spaces; a character with no reading is **omitted** rather than guessed, because an approximation of a pronunciation is worse than an absence of one.
- **`[🐛 FIX]` A real bug, caught by a new test before it shipped: the registry's character guard ran *before* the switch.** Any artefact without a `hanzi` — which is exactly what a grammar table is — returned `null` and would never have rendered, no matter how well the parser had checked it. The guard now belongs to the character-shaped builders only.
- **`[⚡ JANK]` The tutor's local data is decoded off the main thread.** `_loadLocalData` used to `json.decode` a megabyte of `hanzi_metadata.json` inline on the frame that first showed the tutor — the jank flagged two sessions ago and deliberately left alone then. It now goes through `compute()`, and the stroke bundle rides the same path.
- **`[🚫 NOT BUILT, WITH THE REASON]` `toneCurve`.** The catalogue lists a pitch-contour widget, but `ToneGraphCard` draws a *measured* recording against a target — it is a pronunciation-feedback widget, not a "here is what tone 2 looks like" chart. There is nothing honest to draw for a character the learner has not spoken, so the type is not accepted, and a test pins that a reply asking for one gets no block.
- **`[🌐 i18n]` Two new strings in all 14 locales** (`tutorInUse`, `tutorFromYourCards`), pinned in the parity test as must-not-be-English.
- **`[🧪 TEST]`** New `test/features/tutor/tutor_widgets_test.dart` (8): the parser's character check per row, the four-row ceiling, an unknown widget type dropped whole, stroke order built from the bundle for a character outside the deck, examples built from a card and **absent** without one, a checked table rendering while an empty one does not, and the spelling rule (a known reading, and silence for an unknown character). The prompt-budget test's ceiling moved from 3000 to 3600 characters **with the reason written down** — the rules now document four widget kinds, one with a payload schema; the assertion above it (the session adds nothing to the prompt) is unchanged. 68 tests pass across `test/features/tutor` and `flutter analyze` reports no issues.



- **`[📖 READING PACK]` A story at a level, plus questions asked about *its own words*.** `make` gained `readingPack`: the model chooses the **level** and nothing else — the topic comes from the app's own story catalogue, so no free text from a reply ever reaches a prompt. The story is written once and cached under the reading library's own id scheme (`<blueprint>_hsk<level>`), which means a pack the tutor makes opens instantly in the reader, and a story the learner has already read is never written twice. The questions are then derived by the app: the vocabulary pool is the bundled HSK list for that level **filtered to the words the story actually uses**, and the story's text is the *passage* the gap-fill items are asked about. A story whose words the app cannot key still makes a pack — it just makes one with no questions, because the alternative is easy questions.
- **`[📄 PASSAGE]` A paper's passage is no longer example sentences stitched together.** When a paper is built for a level the learner already has a story for, that story becomes the paper's passage. No story, no passage, and a paper without passage items — exactly as before. This closes the caveat the exam shipped with: the passage in a real paper is now something the learner can read.
- **`[🔁 REVIEW SPRINT]` Today's cards, in one folder — and the proposal cannot influence a single card.** `reviewSprint` takes **no arguments**: what is due is a fact the app owns (the SRS dates on the cards), so the model can only notice that a learner wants one. The cards are gathered across every deck, soonest-due first, capped at whatever the proposal asked for (a cap can only ever shrink it). "Nothing due" is its own status, worded as the good news it is, rather than an empty folder or a generic failure.
- **`[🗂️ YOUR PAPERS]` The exam store has held every paper since the first one was saved; now there is a surface for it.** The intro lists the learner's earlier papers with their best attempt — the sentence a retake already uses for comparison, so "best" means one thing in this app — and tapping one opens it again from the same frozen key.
- **`[🔑 PRACTISE WHAT YOU MISSED]` §5.4.6 asks a report to end in a teaching action, and "look at the answers again" is not one.** The report gains a second action that builds a short paper **over the words that were missed**, assembled by the same builder (`ExamWord.fromItem` recovers the word from the item itself, so nothing has to be remembered between the sitting and the retake). It appears only when there are enough words for a paper at all — five, the app's existing floor.
- **`[✍️ WRITING]` The characters you missed, on the writing bench that already exists.** The report's third action opens `WritingBenchScreen` — the same screen a deck offers for writing practice — with the missed characters the learner's own cards carry stroke data for. Nothing new was built for this, deliberately: the tutor does **not** `make` a worksheet, because a sheet is a study action rather than a durable object, and inventing a `make` kind for a screen would have muddied what `make` means.
- **`[🧹 NAMING]` `ExamFolderResult` became `TutorMakeResult`, and its statuses became the real outcomes** — `created`, `createdPaper`, `createdReadingPack`, `notEnoughCards`, `nothingDue`, `failed`. A single `created` status made the confirm card label *any* success "Done", which for a reading pack that could not be written, or a review sprint with nothing in it, would have been a card that lied. `ExamFolderBuilder` also grew `createFromCards`, so a folder can be built from gathered cards (due today) as well as sampled from one deck — one code path, two sources.
- **`[🌐 i18n]` Nine new strings in all 14 locales**, seven of them pinned in `l10n_arb_parity_test.dart` as must-not-be-English: the story link, the question count, the two honest-refusal sentences, the paper history, and the two report actions. The two names (`tutorReadingPack` with its level, `tutorReviewSprint`, which also names the folder) are translated too.
- **`[🧪 TEST]`** New `test/features/tutor/tutor_make_test.dart` (10): the parser's rules for the two newer sources (a level the app has no vocabulary for cannot become a pack; an absurd sprint cap is clamped), the **cache-before-write** rule with a source that counts how often it was asked to write, "questions only from the story's own words" exercised against the app's real HSK 1 bundle, the unkeyable story that still makes a pack, the refused level, the passage helper's three outcomes (this level, another level, a store that cannot answer), a mistake-paper that can only ask about words the learner missed, and `ExamWord.fromItem` recovering the word — sentence and all — from the item it was asked about. 93 tests pass across `test/features/exam`, `test/features/tutor` and the l10n parity suite, and `flutter analyze` reports no issues.



- **`[📝 EXAM]` The exam went from four item kinds to nine, and every new one is *derived* rather than written.** `ExamItemKind` gained `toneChoice`, `dictation`, `orderTokens`, `grammarError` and `passageFill`, so a paper now asks a learner to pick a tone, produce a pinyin, arrange a sentence's own words, spot the sentence the app broke, and fill a gap in text the app already had. The rule that made the first four safe is what makes these safe: the model never supplies a stem or a key, the app builds both from data it holds, and `ExamBuilder` drops a slot it cannot key rather than filling it with something weaker.
- **`[🎧 LISTENING]` `toneChoice` is a real minimal pair, and the app chose neither half of it.** The word's own syllable is stripped of its tone by a new `PinyinUtils.stripTone` (which also drops a numeric tone, so `hao3` and `hǎo` strip alike) and re-marked as `base+1`…`base+4` through `convertNumericToMarks` — so the four options are the same syllable in four tones and the key is the word's own tone. A word that states no tone (`ma`, or a neutral tone) yields **no item at all**: there is no tone question whose answer is "none", and `toneFromSyllable` is used deliberately in place of `getTone`, which reports every unmarked syllable as confident neutral tone — the same trap that once showed 划 as "Neutral (light)".
- **`[✍️ WRITING]` A writing section now exists, from HSK 3 where the real paper has one.** `orderTokens` puts a sentence's words back in order, and the words are the sentence's *own*: `ExamBuilder.segment` splits it longest-match against the source's vocabulary with single characters as the fallback, and punctuation is deliberately **not** a token (it stays in the sentence the learner reads the answer against). The key is the order the author wrote and the grading is a sequence comparison, so the right words in the wrong order is wrong. `dictation` says the word and asks for the pinyin: the key is the authored pinyin and both sides are folded through a new `PinyinUtils.normalizePinyin`, so `hao3`, `Hǎo` and `hǎo` are one answer — and a *missing* tone is not, because folding only merges the two ways of writing a tone.
- **`[🔍 READING]` Two more reading kinds, and the corruption rules are deliberately few.** `grammarError` shows four sentences: three the source authored and the app left alone, one the app **broke** by doubling 很, 不 or 的 — all categorically wrong, so the key cannot be argued with. 非常 is excluded on purpose (`非常非常` is legitimate emphasis, and a rule that could be defended would be a rule that could be answered for). `passageFill` blanks one of the passage's own words, and the passage is text the app already had: whatever the caller passes (a graded story one day), or the source's own example sentences joined. Nothing in either kind is invented at exam time.
- **`[🧱 BUILDER]` A section the source cannot carry is *absent*, not a shortfall.** A blueprint may ask for word order at a level whose bundle has no sentences, and the honest outcome is a paper without that section — not a paper claiming seven items it never had. `ExamBuilder` now decides section by section: nothing buildable at all → the section is not in the paper; buildable but thinner than asked → the shortfall is counted in `dropped` and shown, exactly as before. A slot also looks for a *usable* word instead of giving up on the first one it meets.
- **`[🛠️ FIX]` A deck paper is still sized to the deck.** Adding a writing section to the deck blueprint quietly broke the arithmetic that made `listening + reading` equal the card count — a nine-card deck asked for eleven items and reported four of them as dropped. The blueprint now splits three ways (`writing`, `listening`, then the rest as reading), so the sections add up to the vocabulary the deck actually has: a nine-card deck is a nine-item paper again, and a test pins it.
- **`[🌐 i18n]` Five new instructions, in all 14 locales.** `examPromptTone`, `examPromptDictation`, `examHintPinyin` (which says *both* acceptable ways to write a tone, so nobody is graded on a convention they were never told), `examPromptOrder` and `examPromptGrammar` — translated per locale and pinned in `l10n_arb_parity_test.dart` as must-not-be-English, because an instruction the learner cannot read is an item they cannot answer.
- **`[🚫 NOT BUILT, WITH THE REASON WRITTEN DOWN]` Speaking and picture items — and neither is deferred for lack of time.** Picture items cannot be built because the app ships no vocabulary images. Speaking needs a per-word reading from the learner's audio to key the item, and the app's only tone grader is fed by a **cloud** transcript (`gemini_service` → `LocalToneGrader.apply`), so the key could not be verified on device — and an unverifiable key does not belong in an exam. This corrects §5.3 of `docs/AI_TUTOR_CONCEPT.md`, which had claimed speaking would be cheap and "on device". `writePrompt` (draw the character) waits for a smaller reason: the stroke matcher exists, but the item needs drawn geometry plumbed through the answer model, which is its own slice of work.
- **`[🧪 TEST]`** `test/features/exam/exam_paper_test.dart` is **25** (six new): the tone minimal pair *and* the refusal to build one for a toneless word; dictation's folding rules asked one at a time (`hao3`/`hǎo`/`Hǎo` pass, `hao` and the wrong tone fail — driven off whichever word the builder drew, since it plants the pool rather than walking it in order); word order keyed to the authored sequence, with the same words in another order failing; the grammar key being the altered sentence while every untouched option is the pool's own; and the passage key being a word the passage contains. The deck-trust test now also holds the sentence-based kinds to the *character* rule — nothing shown that the deck did not contain, in a word or in its sentence. `hsk_exam_assets_test.dart` was updated for the writing section a real HSK 3 bundle can now carry. 679 of the suite's 681 pass; the two failures are in files this work never touches (`calligraphic_pitch`'s animation-frame timing and `motion_vocabulary`'s duration-literal baseline), and `flutter analyze` reports no issues.




### [2026-10-02] Generate Deck Docks to the Side on iPad

- **`[iPAD]` The deck generator opens as a side desk on a tablet.** *Generate* — the purple FAB on the dictionary screen — opened `AiDeckGeneratorSheet` as a blurred bottom sheet everywhere, so on an iPad a ten-field form stretched across the bottom of the display and **covered the very word it was being asked about**. Row 23 of `docs/IPAD_ADAPTIVE_PLAN.md`. `AiDeckGeneratorSheet` gained the **`embedded`** form the Scholar's Desk already uses, and its `show()` now branches on `ZenWindow.of(context).isExpanded`: an iPad gets the trailing-edge panel through the `zenSidePanel` helper (full height, width-capped at 420dp, sliding in from the right), while a phone **and a 700dp Split View half** keep the blurred sheet they already had.
- **`[🎨 CHROME]` The panel had to bring its own surface.** `zenSidePanel`'s barrier is transparent by design — the content is expected to *be* its own surface — but the generator had none, because `GlobalBlurredBottomSheet` had always supplied it. Embedded, it now wears the same Xuan-paper surface, rounded 28 on the edge it is docked to, and drops the sheet's 85%-of-height cap, which inside a full-height pane could only ever be a second, smaller ceiling.
- **`[🧪 TEST]`** `test/core/zen_side_panel_test.dart` gains 3 (**6** in the file): a guard that the generator goes through `zenSidePanel` *and* still keeps `GlobalBlurredBottomSheet.show` for phones, plus a behavioural one that the docked pane actually carries the trailing-rounded surface — the omission that would otherwise render a form on nothing.

### [2026-10-02] The Tutor Can Now Show What It Is Talking About

- **`[🎬 TUTOR]` A cited video is finally openable.** The tutor surface already promised this twice over — the envelope's `CITE` block has a `video` source described as *"played through the compliant YouTube path"*, the prompt tells the model it may cite a `VIDEO ID` and to give its `why`, and the chip even renders `l10n.openInYoutube`. But `tutor_screen` never handed the model any video ids, and `TutorEnvelopeParser` refuses a citation that is not in the set it was given — so **every video cite was dropped on arrival**. Even a survivor would have done nothing, because `_citeChip` marked only *deck* chips tappable.
- **`[🔎 GROUNDED]` The candidates are fetched before the model is asked.** `_send` now queries `YoutubeRepository.searchVideos(message, preferChineseCaptions: true)`, keeps at most six, and passes their ids as `videoIds:`, so a citation can only resolve to something the app itself fetched and can play. A YouTube failure is swallowed — the tutor still has its prose, widgets and decks — and the failure mode is *no chip*, never a broken one.
- **`[▶️ PLAYER]` Tapping the chip opens `SmartMediaDeskScreen`** through `SwipeBackPageRoute`: the app's own YouTube path, with the transcript, the briefing and `youtube_player_iframe`, rather than bouncing out to the YouTube app. The id resolves against the candidates of *that* turn (`_Turn.videos`), so an invented or stale id opens nothing.
- **`[🖇️ WIRING]` `TutorReplyView.onOpenVideo`** completes the callback set beside `onOpenDeck`/`onMake`/`onAskOption`, and the chip's tap is now chosen by `switch (cite.source)` — deck or video when the host can follow it, inert otherwise.
- **`[📝 PROMPT]`** Rule 3 now asks for a video when the learner wants to go deeper or to see something demonstrated, and restates the existing limit: the `why` is a claim about the video's **title**, never about contents the model cannot see.
- **`[🧪 TEST]`** `test/features/tutor/tutor_envelope_test.dart` gains 4 (**23** in the file): a video cite surviving when its id was handed over, an invented id dropped while the prose survives, and guards that the screen passes `videoIds:`/`searchVideos(`/`SmartMediaDeskScreen(video:` and that the chip calls `onOpenVideo`.
- **`[ℹ️ NOTE]` Stroke order needed no work — the widget already existed.** The tutor can emit `{"type":"strokeOrder","args":{"hanzi":"…"}}` and `TutorArtefactRegistry` builds it from the learner's own card strokes, dropping the block when the character has none. What it cannot yet do is reach the **bundled** `assets/data/hsk1_strokes.json` for a character outside the learner's library: that resolver (`_loadOfflineStrokes`) is private, and the registry is deliberately synchronous. Widening it is a bounded follow-up rather than a defect — a builder returning `null` is the documented outcome in `docs/AI_TUTOR_CONCEPT.md` §4.3.


## [1.0.0+570] - 2026-10-02

- **`[🚀 RELEASE]` Build Bump to 1.0.0+570**: Implemented grounded AI Tutor surface with structured reply stack (SAY, SHOW, CITE, MAKE, ASK), durable exam folder builder from verified user cards, AI Hub third-tab integration, persistent background audiobook transport via `NowPlayingHost` and `AudioRouteObserver` across all pushed routes, semantic accessibility for transport controls without Overlay dependency, and verified 0 analyzer issues repo-wide.

### [2026-10-02] AI Tutor Architecture: Durable Objects, Structured Reply Blocks & Exam Guardrails

- **`[🧠 TUTOR]` Comprehensive architecture and implementation for grounded, durable AI Tutor interactions.** Expanded `docs/AI_TUTOR_CONCEPT.md` (§11–§12) specifying durable object authoring (exam folders, paper packs, remedial sets, study plans), structured response block taxonomy (SAY, SHOW, CITE, MAKE, ASK), and library integrity guardrails preventing hallucinated vocabulary generation without dictionary validation. Implemented `TutorEnvelopeParser` (`tutor_envelope_parser.dart`), `ExamFolderBuilder` (`exam_folder_builder.dart`), `TutorScreen` (`tutor_screen.dart`), and `TutorArtefactRegistry` (`tutor_artefacts.dart`), wired as third tab in `AiHubScreen`. Verified 19/19 passing tests in `test/features/tutor/tutor_envelope_test.dart`.

### [2026-10-02] Leaving an Audiobook No Longer Strands It Playing

- **`[🐛 FIX]` The transport is mounted above the `Navigator` now, so no pushed route can bury it.** `AudiobookPlayerScreen.dispose` deliberately does **not** stop playback when it is playing — background listening is the feature — and hands the transport to the shell's `NowPlayingBar`. But that bar lives inside the *shell's* `Scaffold`, and the player is opened from `BookDetailScreen` and `BookReaderScreen`, so stepping out of the player landed on exactly the kind of pushed route that **covers** it: the audiobook kept playing, the only in-app control was behind a screen nobody could see, and the OS media notification was the sole surface left. New `NowPlayingHost` + `AudioRouteObserver` (`now_playing_host.dart`) mount the same bar in `MaterialApp.builder`, where it follows the learner onto every route, and draw it **only while the shell is buried** — so the shell keeps its own, above the tabs, exactly as before.
- **`[🛡️ ROBUST]` The observer is self-correcting, not a counter.** Every `NavigatorObserver` callback reports the route that is on top *afterwards* (`didPush` → the new route, `didPop`/`didRemove` → `previousRoute`, `didReplace` only when the replaced route was the top one), and "buried" is just `!top.isFirst`. A `pushAndRemoveUntil` or a `removeRoute` therefore cannot leave a depth drifting and silently strand playback again the way a counter would.
- **`[🎛️ UX]` The player keeps its own transport, and the host stands down there.** `playerOwnsTransportProvider` is raised in the player's `initState` and released in `dispose`; without it the host would duplicate the player's controls *and* — because tapping the bar reopens the player — offer to push a second one.
- **`[♿ A11Y]` The bar no longer requires an `Overlay`.** Above the Navigator there is none, and `IconButton(tooltip:)` builds a `Tooltip` that asserts without one. The two transport buttons carry their labels through `Semantics` instead, so screen readers announce play/pause and stop wherever the bar is mounted; the shell's hover tooltip was the only casualty.
- **`[🧪 TEST]`** `test/features/reading/now_playing_bar_test.dart` gains 5: the observer reporting a buried shell *and recovering on pop*, the bar **appearing on a pushed route with its stop control present** (the reported bug, reproduced), the host standing down for the player, plus source guards that the app wires the host into `builder:` alongside the observer and that the player claims and releases the flag. 13/13 pass, and `test/features/reading` is 151 green apart from the pre-existing `chapter_title_asset_completeness` asset-count failure.

## [1.0.0+569] - 2026-10-02

- **`[🚀 RELEASE]` Build Bump to 1.0.0+569**: Unified tone graph colour vocabulary and pitch lane geometry, resolved neutral tone misattribution on multi-pronunciation characters (including 划), localized network offline handling across 14 languages, high-definition YouTube thumbnail resolution ladder, poet biography enrichments across all 14 locales, iPad side panel docks for Scholar's Desk and Scenario Launcher with motion-safe animations, and verified repository hygiene.

### [2026-10-02] "No Network" Finally Says So, In Every Language

- **`[🌐 i18n]` The app stops leaking `ClientException: Failed host lookup…` and uses the sentence it already had.** `noInternetConnectionPleaseCheckYour` was **translated into all 14 locales and never referenced once** — dead in the catalog, as were `ourAiTutorsAreCurrentlyOfflinePleas` and `azureGradingTimedOutCheckYourIntern`. The sentence now has one owner, `lib/shared/widgets/network_notice.dart`, so a lost connection reads the same wherever it happens and rewording it is one line per locale rather than a hunt through call sites.
- **`[🔎 CLASSIFY]` `lib/core/utils/network_failure.dart` (new) tells a lost connection apart from every other failure**, because a false positive blames the learner's wifi for a server-side bug and a false negative shows them a stack trace. Types first — `http.ClientException` (`package:http` only throws this for connection-level problems, and its `IOClient` wraps a dead socket as a `ClientException` that *also* implements `SocketException`, so one check covers the radio, the DNS lookup and the socket), `TimeoutException`, and `FirebaseException` by its `unavailable` / `network-request-failed` codes — then message markers for the transports that wrap or stringify the original (`youtube_explode_dart` rethrows through a chain; `google_generative_ai`'s chat stream does `controller.addError(e, s)` from inside an SDK we do not control). It deliberately imports **neither `dart:io` nor any transport package**, since it is reached from screens that are otherwise web-compatible and `dart:io` alone would make every one of those importers unbuildable for web.
- **`[🚫 NOT-OFFLINE]` The classifier refuses the tempting false positives, and a test pins each one.** `youtube_explode_dart`'s `TransientFailureException` means **`statusCode >= 500` and "Channel page is broken"** — YouTube is unwell, the network is fine, and telling the learner to check wifi would be actively misleading — so it is *not* offline; neither is a 429 (the request arrived), an empty model response, a `VideoUnavailableException`, or our own `ClientException('Client is already closed.')`.
- **`[🔔 UX]` A fourth toast tone, `ZenToastTone.offline`** — the wifi glyph in Emperor's Gold with the same refusal haptic. "Nothing can work until you turn your network back on" is different advice from "that didn't work, try again"; a red error for "no wifi" teaches a learner to retry a request that cannot possibly succeed.
- **`[🔌 WIRED]` Every screen that cannot work offline now says so.** The two shapes are one-expression swaps, so nothing was restructured: a **load** that failed renders `NetworkNotice.describe(context, e, fallback: …)` in the error panel it already had, and a **tap** that failed asks `NetworkNotice.showIfOffline(context, e)` before its usual toast. Covered: YouTube search, category shelves and channel feeds (shelves gained an `offline` load state; the channel panel swaps to the wifi glyph); the story reader's generation panel and all five of `StoryController`'s load paths; the **AI tutor** chat; `web_browser`'s analysis / extraction / simplify / translate actions; Smart Media Desk; nuance compare; shadowing studio and the travel interpreter; universal scanner (both scan paths); the AI deck generator; custom and deck scenarios; course-path generation; shelf installs; **sign-in, purchase restore and account deletion**; and the RevenueCat paywall. Three sites that merely *look* network-bound were deliberately left alone — a malformed URL, local deck saves, and a drag-and-drop answer check.
- **`[🗣️ PROVIDERS]` The providers can speak the learner's language now.** `ConversationState.error` and `StoryState.error` are plain `String`s, so a controller with no `BuildContext` had been storing **hardcoded English** — including its own copy of "No internet connection…" that was correct in exactly one of fourteen locales, guarding a hand-rolled `contains('socket') || contains('network')` sniff. New `lib/core/providers/l10n_provider.dart` resolves `AppLocalizations` from the same `settingsProvider.locale` that `main.dart` hands to `MaterialApp`, and both controllers take a **lazy** `l10n` getter, read inside the `catch` and never watched into the notifier, so a locale change cannot discard a conversation or a story in progress. The classifier replaced the sniff, and `ourAiTutorsAreCurrentlyOfflinePleas` / `azureGradingTimedOutCheckYourIntern` — translated into all fourteen locales and never once referenced — are finally the fallbacks they were written to be.
- **`[📐 WHY NOT A BANNER]`** A global "you are offline" strip was rejected deliberately: the app is genuinely offline-first (decks in Hive, texts and covers in the bundle, on-device TTS and tone grading), so a permanent banner would be wrong while someone happily reviews cards on a plane. The notice appears only when a request that actually needed the network failed.
- **`[🧪 TEST]`** `test/unit_tests/network_failure_test.dart` (17) pins **both** directions of the verdict; `test/shared/network_notice_test.dart` (5) proves the notice renders the *German* catalogue's own sentence when German is in scope (not a hardcoded English string), stays silent for a 500 and raises the wifi toast for a dead socket; `zen_toast_test.dart` gains the offline-tone assertion; and the two `StoryController` fakes now pass a localizer, since the constructor takes one. Green: `test/shared` **105**, `echo_hall` **80**, `media` **160**, `reading` **146**, plus the flashcards and auth localization suites. `flutter analyze` reports **0 issues**.
- **`[📝 NOTE]` Four reds that are *not* from this work, each verified rather than assumed.** `chapter_title_asset_completeness_test` — `assets/data/books` is git-clean (101 files, 3,133 ids) but `chapter_titles_by_id_en.json` holds only **1,523** of them, and the guard's constant is stale at 3,199. `tome_manager_shelf_test` asserts the label *"Master Bookshelf Status"*, which exists **nowhere** in the repo or the catalog. And the four `cultural_context_book_parity` / `simplified_reader_prose` parity failures predate this work. A Dart change to error handling cannot move an asset count or resurrect a deleted label.

### [2026-10-02] YouTube Stills Are Fetched at Their Highest Rendition

- **`[🖼️ MEDIA]` Every fetched YouTube frame now asks for the 1280x720 `maxresdefault` still first, and steps down to the guaranteed 480x360 `hqdefault`.** The frame the fetch paths defaulted to is 480x360 — it pixelates the moment it is stretched across a full-bleed header — and `youtube_repository`'s channel feed was additionally serving the 320x180 `mqdefault` in its lower slot. New `lib/core/utils/youtube_thumbnail.dart` is the one place that knows the rendition ladder and the caveat that makes it awkward: `maxresdefault` is **404** for uploads never encoded above 480p, so it must be *attempted*, never assumed.
- **`[🖼️ MEDIA]` Both sources already say when an HD still exists, so we finally listen.** `youtube_explode`'s `maxResUrl` and the Data API's `thumbnails.maxres` are populated only for uploads that really have the 1280x720 frame, so `_videoToModel`, the channel-upload mapping and `YoutubeVideo.fromJson` now prefer them, falling back to `hqdefault` when absent. `daily_discovery_repository`'s two private builders now delegate to the shared helper instead of re-hardcoding the same URLs.
- **`[🔧 FIX]` A missing HD still degrades to a picture, not a grey tile.** `channel_videos_screen` and `media_search_screen` handed the 404 straight to their placeholder; both now step down to the 480x360 frame first, matching the two-step fallback `birthday_shelf.dart` already had.
- **`[🧪 TEST]` `test/unit_tests/youtube_thumbnail_test.dart` (new)** pins the URL ladder and the `fromJson` preference order — `maxres` → `high` → `medium` — with the lower slot always left on the frame every upload has. `birthday_playlist_test`, `daily_discovery_repository_test` and `youtube_discovery_test` (18) stay green; `flutter analyze` reports 0 issues.

### [2026-10-02] The OpenRouter Key Is Rotated

- **`[🔑 SECURITY]` The OpenRouter credential in `.env` was rotated to a freshly issued key.** The remedy in the hardcoded-key scrub below removed the literal from source, but removing a secret from a repository does **not** revoke it — the value stays live at the provider until it is rotated there. `.env` is gitignored, so the replacement never enters source control.
- **`[📝 NOTE]` `functions/.env`'s OpenRouter entry was deliberately left alone.** `openRouterProxyV2` in `functions/index.js` intercepts and forces every request onto Gemini 2.5 Flash, and is deployed with `secrets: [geminiApiKey]` only — so that key is vestigial, and rotating it would change nothing.

### [2026-10-02] The Phrase Tone Graph Now Shares One Axis (DTW)

- **`[FIX]` The two strokes finally share an x-axis.** The target is a plan of equal tone slots and the learner's contour is real time, so the two were drawn on two different clocks and a crossing point meant nothing - removing the overlay's false synchrony (two lanes) was only half the fix. The learner's contour is now warped **onto the target's slot axis** with the previously-unused `DtwAligner.alignPitch`, in `shadowing_studio_screen.dart`. `alignPitch` returns an array that is silent exactly where the target is silent, so both lanes are the same length and map to the same x - a vertical slice now compares like with like. DTW only re-times what was measured; the *verdict* still comes from `gradeAudio`, never from this warping.
- **`[TEST]` `test/unit_tests/dtw_aligner_test.dart` (new)** pins the property the graph depends on: the aligned contour has the reference's length, is silent exactly where the reference is silent, and never fabricates a value (values are drawn from the learner's own contour; an empty contour aligns to silence).

### [2026-10-02] The Roleplay Launcher Becomes a Side Desk, and the Two Modes Say What They Do

- **`[iPAD]` Choosing how to practise happens beside the catalogue now, not over it.** Tapping a scenario card in Echo Hall opened the briefing as a bottom sheet everywhere — a stretched phone on an iPad — and the F5 fallback (a centred form sheet) hid the very card being chosen about. New **`zenSidePanel`** in `lib/shared/widgets/zen_overlay.dart` (the fourth helper, beside `zenSheet`/`zenDialog`/`zenPicker`) docks `scenario_selection_screen`'s launcher to the trailing edge at `isExpanded` — full height, width-capped at 420dp, sliding in from the right — while a phone **and a 700dp Split View half** keep the bottom sheet (the same briefing, in the platform's own form).
- **`[UX]` "Voice Call" and "Text Chat" now say what they do.** The pair of 52dp buttons was an icon plus a label, so a learner who has never placed an AI voice call could not tell the two apart. Each mode is a full-width card carrying a one-line description ("Immersive roleplay with AI avatars" / "Practice in Roleplay"), behind `BouncingButton` so it presses like every other card in the app — and the pair is **stacked rather than sharing one row**, which is also what stops it overflowing at large text scales.
- **`[MOTION]` Animation, all of it motion-safe.** The launcher's content fades in behind the panel with a 2% lift (`_SheetEntrance`: one-shot, no timers, and it rests at the end state under Reduce Motion), and the persona portrait gains a soft breathing halo (`ZenMotion.ambient`) that pulses only when the OS has not asked to reduce motion. The drag handle is dropped on the panel (the scrim dismisses it) and kept on the sheet.
- **`[FIX]` `lib/shared/widgets/chat_motion.dart`** declared `library;` after its imports, which is a compile error for every library that reaches it — moved above them.
- **`[TEST]` Verified.** `test/core/zen_side_panel_test.dart` (4: the sheet on a phone **and** a Split View half, the trailing panel at expanded, and a guard that the launcher never falls back to a centred dialog or a raw sheet) plus `scenario_launcher_layout_test.dart` (3) pass; `flutter analyze` reports 0 issues.

### [2026-10-02] The Scholar's Desk Docks to the Side on iPad

- **`[iPAD]` The Scholar's Desk opens as a side panel on a tablet.** The AI-tutor chat (`character_chat_sheet.dart`) was a bottom sheet everywhere, which wastes an iPad - a chat wants height, not a sheet (row 23 of `docs/IPAD_ADAPTIVE_PLAN.md`). `CharacterChatSheet` gained an **`embedded`** form that fills a pane (bubbles size to the pane not the window, the sheet chrome is dropped, and it closes through a callback), and `character_detail_screen.dart` docks it to the trailing edge at `isExpanded`. The phone keeps the exact modal sheet it had; the FAB branches on the window class and withdraws while the panel is open.
- **`[MOTION]` More animation, all motion-safe.** The docked desk slides and fades in from the trailing edge (one-shot, and it rests at the end state under Reduce Motion), and the follow-up chips now rise in one beat apart behind their bubble instead of all landing at once - reusing the existing `_MessageEntrance` (fade + 10% lift) so Reduce Motion is honoured throughout.
- **`[TEST]`** `character_detail_ipad_layout_test`, `character_chat_localization_test` and `character_detail_localization_test` pass; `flutter analyze lib` reports 0 issues.

### [2026-10-02] Phrase Tone Graph: Two Lanes, So It Stops Imitating One Signal

- **`[UX]` The phrase tone graph no longer overlays two strokes on axes that mean different things.** The target is one equal slot per tone (a plan, with no time meaning) while the learner's voice is real elapsed time - drawn over one another they read as a single time-aligned signal, and a crossing point read as "there, I was off" when it was an accident of layout. `ToneGraphPainter` now draws the two strokes in **separate lanes** (your voice above, the target below) sharing one width and one pitch scale, with a hairline between them. `ToneGraphCard` keeps its legend, which now maps to the lanes by colour.
- **`[UX]` One colour vocabulary, defined once.** `PitchGraphPalette` (blue = your voice, grey = target) is now the single source for the phrase graph's legend, its strokes and the tone sheet's key, so the two surfaces cannot drift apart again.
- **`[TEST]`** `tone_graph_card_test`, `tone_comparison_sheet_test`, `tone_evaluator_test`, `pinyin_utils_test` and `speaking_tone_feedback_test` pass; `flutter analyze lib` reports 0 issues.

### [2026-10-02] Tone Graphs: One Colour Language, and a Tone That Is Never Invented

- **`[BUG]` A missing tone is no longer reported as the neutral tone.** `PinyinUtils.getTone` returns 5 for any syllable without a tone mark, so the tone sheet opened on 划 (in 计划 - a **fourth** tone, hua) announced "Neutral (light)". New `PinyinUtils.toneFromSyllable` returns **0 for "no tone information"**, and both the per-syllable tap and the "Compare 4 tones" button now resolve the tone from the phoneme, then the word's authored pinyin (`wordData['pinyin']`), then the tone the grader already stored (`expectedTone`) - instead of recomputing a neutral from a bare syllable.
- **`[UX]` The tone sheet's graph now speaks the same language as the phrase graph.** Every pitch graph uses one vocabulary - **solid blue = your voice, grey dashed = target** - defined once in `PitchGraphPalette`. `CalligraphicPitchContour` had the mapping inverted (blue was the target), which is why the same blue meant opposite things on the two screens; it now also carries the legend it was missing, and says when no pitch was measured rather than leaving a bare box.
- **`[UX]` The neutral tone is demonstrated when it is the target.** The comparison list rendered tones 1-4 only, so a neutral-tone target had no row to highlight - the sheet named a tone it never played. It now shows a fifth row whenever the neutral tone is in play.
- **`[BUG]` A measured trace fills its graph.** `ToneGraphPainter` mapped points by raw index, so any unvoiced lead-in left the blue line starting a third of the way in and ending short, which read as "your voice was late". It now maps the voiced span across the full width.
- **`[TEST]`** `tone_comparison_sheet_test`, `tone_graph_card_test`, `tone_evaluator_test`, `pinyin_utils_test` and `speaking_tone_feedback_test` pass; `flutter analyze` clean.

### [2026-10-02] Every Poet's Biography Rewritten and Re-Translated into All 14 Locales

- **`[READING]` The author card of every poetry collection now has something worth reading.** The 100 poet biographies behind `BundledAuthorBiographyService` were a line or two each with no era, courtesy name, style, representative works or place in the tradition (Li Bai read only as "a romantic poet of the High Tang"). Every biography was rewritten to carry that, keeping the original facts as the source of truth (no invented dates or deeds).
- **`[I18N]` All 100 biographies re-translated into the 13 content locales plus English.** `assets/data/poet_bios.json` (Simplified Chinese) and `assets/data/l10n/poet_bios_<locale>.json` for `en, ar, de, es, fr, hi, id, it, ja, ko, pt, ru, th, vi` were regenerated: 1,400 texts, verified to hold exactly 100 non-empty keys each.
- **`[PROVENANCE]` The collective name `佚名` (Anonymous) was preserved untouched.** It is a collection, not a person, so its hand-written `origin: collective` biography is never model-generated; `poetry_collection_test.dart` pins that, and the pipeline now skips collective entries so a re-run cannot clobber it.
- **`[TOOLING]` `scratch/poet_bios_i18n.py`** rewrites and translates in one pass through Gemini (`.env` key), resumable via a sidecar progress file and saving after every poet, so an interrupted run costs one call and a failed poet keeps its existing text everywhere rather than going blank.
- **`[TEST]` Verified.** `poetry_collection_test.dart` (36), `bundled_author_biography_service_test.dart`, `bundled_book_detail_localization_test.dart` and `content_localization_guard_test.dart` all pass.

## [1.0.0+568] - 2026-10-02

- **`[🚀 RELEASE]` Build Bump to 1.0.0+568**: Unified Character Detail Screen card identity across all entry points, fixed Shadowing Studio iPad session stage to non-scrollable viewport, localized classical Chinese poetry titles and book titles across all 14 languages with high Sinological scholarship, added complete author biographies and book synopses across 14 locales, enriched poetry collection covers with authentic portraits and seal stamps, and verified GitLab CI pipelines and analyzer hygiene.

### [2026-10-02] Character Detail Screen Card Identity Unification & Shadowing Studio iPad Fixed Layout

- **`[🎴 FLASHCARDS]` Unified Character Detail Screen Identity Across All QuickLook & Dictionary Entry Points.** Resolved an issue where opening `CharacterDetailScreen` from bottom-sheet quicklook or dictionary search presented a bare/fallback card rather than the rich character screen with animated strokes, authentic HSK badge, and library mastery status:
  - In `GlobalDictionaryRepository._mapRowToCard`, populated `hskLevel` from popularity rank calculations instead of hardcoding `0`.
  - In `CharacterDetailScreen`, enhanced `_getCurrentCard()` to match against library flashcards by either card ID or Hanzi (`c.id == widget.card.id || c.hanzi == widget.card.hanzi`), ensuring global dictionary lookups seamlessly inherit saved stroke paths, median vectors, user mastery mode stats, deck associations, and HSK level.
  - In `QuickLookSheet`, unified `initialCard` with `localCard` from library lookup so opening the full character screen preserves complete library identity and animated stroke data. In "Full card" navigation, captured `rootNavigator: true` before sheet dismissal.
  - In `DictionaryScreen.unifiedResults`, merged HSK levels between master card and library card so search results maintain accurate HSK badges.
  - All 182 tests in `test/features/flashcards/` pass cleanly.

- **`[🎙️ LIVE TRANSLATE]` Shadowing Studio iPad Fixed Viewport Layout (No-Scroll Mandate).** On iPad (`context.isTabletWindow`), the live shadowing session UI is now rendered as a fixed, non-scrollable layout:
  - Inside `_buildSessionUI`, on tablet viewports, the session content is laid out directly in a fixed `SizedBox(height: constraints.maxHeight, width: constraints.maxWidth)` where the phrase card area expands via `Expanded` to center its content, eliminating `SingleChildScrollView` entirely so learners neither need to nor can scroll down.
  - On mobile phones, `SingleChildScrollView` is preserved so compact viewports remain smoothly scrollable without clipping.
  - Responsive iPad spacing tuned across the session stage: grade card margin reduced to 16dp, padding reduced to 14dp, ToneGraphCard height tuned to 100dp, bottom action padding reduced to 16dp, and conditional spacing applied after grading.
  - Verified across iPad landscape (1024x768), iPad portrait (768x1024), and phone portrait (390x844) viewports. All 54 tests in `test/features/live_translate/` pass cleanly.

### [2026-10-02] Poetry Collection Covers: a Poet's Portrait, or an In-House Seal

- **`[READING]` Every poet's collection now has a cover that says who wrote it.** The 100 poetry collections (`poetry_author_<digest>`) were previously illustrated from the Cleveland Museum's CC0 collection, which holds no portraits of Chinese poets, so a collection of Li Bai's poems wore a mountain and Du Fu's wore a teapot. A hybrid pass now gives each collection either a **public-domain historical portrait** of the poet or a **project-owned ink seal card** bearing the poet's name, so the shelf reads as poets rather than as landscapes.
- **`[RIGHTS]` Public-domain portraits, verified, never assumed.** A portrait is embedded only when its source file reports a public-domain licence (`PD-old`, `PD-China`, `CC0`...). Modern illustrations of these poets (e.g. `刘禹锡像.JPG` CC BY-SA 3.0, `柳永和歌妓大理石像.jpg` CC BY-SA 4.0) were rejected and fell back to a seal. 32 of 100 collections carry a verified portrait: the major poets (李白, 杜甫, 苏轼, 白居易, 王维, 孟浩然, 柳宗元, 韩愈, 李清照, 陆游, 曹操...), plus 岑参, 刘禹锡, 岳飞 and 柳永 found through curated public-domain overrides; the remaining 68 carry the seal.
- **`[ASSETS]` `poetry_cover_manifest.json` is now schema 3 (hybrid), with full provenance per cover.** Each portrait records its Commons file page, creator, work date, licence and sha256; each seal records the project as rights holder. The generator is `scratch/poet_portrait_covers.py` (dry run by default, `--apply` to write), mirroring `scratch/book_covers.py`.
- **`[TEST]` Guard test rewritten for the hybrid policy.** `test/unit_tests/poetry_cover_manifest_test.dart` asserts every cover id is the digest of the author it names, every poet has a cover, portraits carry a public-domain licence and a source page, seals are project-owned, and all covers are 600x800 with matching sha256. Passes; `flutter analyze` clean.

### [2026-10-02] Canonical Sinological Poem Title Translations Across All 14 Locales

- **`[🌸 POETRY]` High-Scholarship Poem Titles in All 14 Locales.** Completed rigorous Sinological title localization for classical Chinese poetry across all 14 supported languages (`en`, `ar`, `de`, `es`, `fr`, `hi`, `id`, `it`, `ja`, `ko`, `pt`, `ru`, `th`, `vi`). Overhauled machine-translated and literal artifacts in the curated classical masterpieces, establishing canonical literary forms:
  - **Japanese (`ja`):** Traditional Kanji/Shinjitai titles (e.g. 《静夜思》, 《黄鶴楼送孟浩然之広陵》, 《早発白帝城》, 《将進酒》, 《春望》, 《長恨歌》, 《琵琶行》) replacing literal machine translations like "ワインを持ち込んでください" (BYOB) and "春の景色".
  - **Korean (`ko`):** Canonical Sino-Korean titles in Hangul (e.g. 《정야사》, 《황학루송맹호연지광릉》, 《조발백제성》, 《장진주》, 《춘망》, 《장한가》, 《비파행》) replacing awkward modern colloquialisms like "와인을 가져오세요" and "봄의 전망".
  - **Vietnamese (`vi`):** Authentic Hán-Việt titles in Latin script with standard diacritics (e.g. "Tĩnh Dạ Tứ", "Hoàng Hạc Lâu tống Mạnh Hạo Nhiên chi Quảng Lăng", "Tả Phát Bạch Đế Thành", "Tương Tiến Tửu", "Xuân Vọng", "Trường Hận Ca", "Tỳ Bà Hành") replacing literal phrases like "Mang rượu vào" and "Quang cảnh mùa xuân".
  - **Russian (`ru`):** Scholarly Sinological titles (e.g. "Думы тихой ночью", "Проводы Мэн Хаораня в Гуанлин у Башни Желтого Журавля", "Рано утром покидаю город Байди", "Приглашение к вину", "Весенний взор") replacing crude translations like "Принеси вино". Replaced all 132 Roman numeral artifacts (` I`, ` II`, ` III`) across the entire 4,237 poem corpus with Cyrillic part numbering `(Часть 1)`, `(Часть 2)` and fixed manuscript notation `(П. 3619)`.
  - **French (`fr`), German (`de`), Spanish (`es`), Italian (`it`), Portuguese (`pt`), Thai (`th`), Arabic (`ar`), Hindi (`hi`), Indonesian (`id`):** Eliminated all untranslated English and Latin leaks ("Yellow Crane Tower", "Hibiscus Tower", "River City Lied", "Jiande", "Qinhuai", "Shu", "Leyou", etc.) in favor of culturally authoritative Sinological titles.
- **`[🌐 I18N]` English Base Poetry Asset Created.** Generated `assets/data/l10n/poetry_en.json` containing all 4,237 poems with scholarly English titles and summaries, completing 14-locale asset parity across all content families.
- **`[🧪 TEST]` Full Suite Verification.** All tests passed in `test/features/reading/content_title_localization_test.dart` (6/6), `test/core/content_localization_guard_test.dart` (8/8), and `test/features/reading/poetry_collection_test.dart` (36/36). Total Hygiene State maintained with 0 analyzer issues in `flutter analyze lib`.

On iOS/iPadOS, native `WKWebView` platform views ignore Flutter's software `ClipRect` across transforms, causing the video frame to protrude 15px over the stage border, vertical divider, and into the right transcript/prep column.

- **`[🔧 FIX]` 68 Gutenberg imports showed PG's auto-generated placeholder cover, not a jacket.** Every Gutenberg-sourced book (`docs/BOOK_SOURCES.md`) shipped the image PG draws when a book has no jacket art — a flat colour field with a few random shapes and a "Project Gutenberg" stamp — and `CalligraphicBookCover` rendered it with `BoxFit.cover`, which cropped the Chinese title off the top and the stamp off the bottom and left a band of abstract colour blocks beside the hand-picked jackets and the CC0 museum plates. `CalligraphicBookCover` now draws its own calligraphic plate (`_buildCalligraphicFallback`) for those books; the file stays bundled, so the cover-manifest contract is unchanged and the art returns as soon as a book is given a real cover.
- **`[🔧 FIX]` The list is derived, not hand-guessed.** `poetry_story_id.dart` gains `gutenbergPlaceholderCoverIds` + `bookCoverIsPlaceholder` — exactly the `gutenberg.org` sources in `docs/BOOK_SOURCES.md` (68 ids). `scratch/_cover_placeholder_probe.py` prints and re-validates it. A file-size rule would have been wrong: the four sub-20 KB covers outside Gutenberg (`dao_de_jing`, `journey_to_the_west`, `records_grand_historian`, `the_art_of_war`) are real, hand-picked jackets.
- **`[🧪 TEST]` `test/unit_tests/book_cover_placeholder_test.dart` 5/5** (new) — the id set stays inside the catalog, every placeholder book still ships a file, the four jackets are never swept in, and a widget test proves a placeholder book paints the plate (no `Image`) while a real jacket is still a picture.
