- **`[🚀 RELEASE]` Build Bump to 1.0.0+562**: Synchronized release delivering on-device local tone assessment engine, pitch contour visualization in Shadowing Studio, legal compliance & copyright audit remediations across library books and third-party data, dictionary source verification, complete 13-language micro-read summaries, segment tab overflow fix, and verified Total Hygiene State.
### [2026-09-27] The WordNet Family Verified — And PanLex Turns Out To Be NonCommercial

- **`[⚖️ LEGAL]` Every Tier-3 source was checked at its source, and one of them is a second NonCommercial licence hiding in the shipped Thai column.** `third_party/dictionary-sources.md` had recorded PanLex as *"generally released CC0 — verify"*. **It is not CC0.** https://panlex.org/license/ states the database is provided "under the Creative Commons **Attribution-NonCommercial-ShareAlike 4.0** International License", and that "**Commercial use of these materials is permitted only by obtaining written permission from PanLex**". Build #301 filled `definition_th` from **OMW + PanLex + MUSE** — so **two of its three sources are NonCommercial** (MUSE is CC BY-NC 4.0). The Thai exposure is therefore not "0 to 75,202 values" but the whole **75,202-value** expansion, NC-tainted, and the audit's narrower figure came from inheriting the wrong tier for PanLex.
- **`[🔧 FIX]` The wrong tier was corrected everywhere it had propagated, not just where it was found.** `tooling/audit_dictionary_exposure.py` moves PanLex from **B** to **C** and its report now names both NonCommercial sources; `third_party/dictionary-exposure-audit.md` corrects the "only source with a licence we have read that forbids commercial use" claim and the tier list; the **in-app notice** in `lib/core/legal/third_party_notices.dart` had listed PanLex among the *open-source* sources and no longer does — it now names PanLex and MUSE as NonCommercial sources being replaced rather than credited. PanLex joins MUSE in Tier 1 of the inventory and in the new `ISSUES.md` P0 entry.
- **`[✅ VERIFY]` Five sources came out better than assumed, so the tier list stops being pessimistic.** Checked at source, all commercial-OK and needing only credit: **WordNet-JA** = the **NICT** licence ("Permission to use, copy, modify and distribute … for any purpose and without fee or royalty"); **ItalWordNet** = **ODC-BY** (attribution only — no share-alike, better than the CC BY-SA this file assumed); **Bahasa Wordnet (Indonesian)** = **MIT**; **WordNet MCR (Spanish)** = **CC BY 3.0**; **Arabic WordNet (AWN v2)** = **CC BY-SA 3.0**. The authoritative route was **OMW's own `index.toml`**, which carries a `license` field per wordnet and states at the top level: "Please consult the LICENSE files included with the individual wordnets. **Note that all permit redistribution.**"
- **`[📝 NOTE]` One source cannot be verified at all, because nothing identifies it.** **"Hán-Việt DB"** (12,250 Vietnamese values) has **no URL, project name or author recorded anywhere in the repository**. That is a different failure from "licence unknown" — it is "which file this even is, is unknown" — so it is recorded as **P1** and flagged for replacement rather than left as "verify".
- **`[📝 NOTE]` OMW Thai is permissive, so the Thai column is recoverable.** OMW records `omw-th`'s licence as `wordnet`, shorthand for the Princeton WordNet licence, and OMW's blanket statement is that all its component wordnets permit redistribution. That makes "rebuild Thai from OMW alone" a real fix rather than a hope — and it is what `scratch/dictionary_clean.db` already does, by excluding PanLex and MUSE entirely.

### [2026-09-27] A Clean Dictionary From Verified Sources Only — 125,127 Entries

- **`[📄 DATA]` The first dictionary built only from sources whose licence has been read and confirmed: 125,127 entries, 121,362 distinct simplified headwords.** New `scratch/build_clean_dictionary.py` (re-runnable) produces `scratch/dictionary_clean.db` (36.7 MB) from exactly two sources — **CC-CEDICT (CC BY-SA 4.0)**, supplying headword/pinyin/English, and **HanDeDict (CC BY-SA 2.0)**, supplying German for 63,573 of the rows.
- **`[📝 NOTE]` Why a rebuild and not a filter — and why that is the whole point.** The shipped `assets/data/dictionary.db` records **no per-row provenance** (its own `dictionary_metadata` says so), so its 13 localised columns cannot be filtered into "the clean part": nobody can tell which row came from Pleco, BKRS, MUSE or the unnamed Hindi source. Re-ingesting from the sources is the only honest route, and it is what makes every value in the new file attributable.
- **`[⚖️ LEGAL]` The exclusions are written into the file itself, not just into a ticket.** The new database's `dictionary_metadata` records `dictionary_sources_excluded` — Pleco (commercial product, no licence), "Big BKRS" (scraped site), Facebook MUSE (**CC BY-NC 4.0, non-commercial**), the unnamed `definition_hi` source, LiudmilaLV/json_hsk (no LICENSE file), CFDICT (licence never stated) and the WordNet/OMW/PanLex corpora (unverified) — alongside a `dictionary_provenance_note` stating that per-row provenance **is** known here by construction, unlike the shipped file.
- **`[🛡️ FIX]` The shipped database was never touched, and that is verified, not promised.** It is opened through SQLite's `mode=ro` URI, and the script hashes it before and after and fails if it changed: **sha256 `A5375CA2…443B`, 195,063,808 bytes, identical on both sides of the run.**
- **`[📝 NOTE]` Three measurement findings that correct our own records.** (1) Fresh CC-CEDICT has **125,127** entries — 118 more than the 125,009 in the shipped snapshot, which is just older; (2) **HanDeDict's "262,548 headwords" in `third_party/dictionary-sources.md` is not reproducible** — the current upstream `handedict.u8` has 160,706 lines and 159,031 distinct simplified forms, and the row now says so; (3) **HanDeDict knows 98,900 simplified forms CC-CEDICT does not have**, which would take the headword list to **220,262 distinct simplified forms** if the sources were unioned — deliberately left as a decision rather than a silent default.
- **`[✅ VERIFY]` The German was checked to be German, not just present.** Sampled rows read `爱 → lieben (V)`, `学习 → lernen; studieren (V)`, `电脑 → Computer (S, EDV)`, `老师 → Lehrer (S, Edu)`, `中国 → China (Eig, Geo)`; the upstream file's own header confirms "Diese Datei ist kompatibel mit dem ursprünglichen CEDICT-Format", so the CEDICT-family parser is the right one. `scratch/dictionary_clean.db` is **not** declared in `pubspec.yaml` — it is deliberately not shipped, since this is provisional.

### [2026-09-27] The Bundled Hanzi Data Shipped With No Licence At All

- **`[⚖️ LEGAL]` Three third-party datasets inside the app had no notice anywhere, and two licences that require the licence *file* to ship were not shipping it.** `assets/data/hsk1_strokes.json`, `hsk1_animcjk.json` and `hsk1_hanzivg.json` are third-party vector data with licences of their own, and `lib/core/legal/third_party_notices.dart` registered **only the dictionary** — so all three shipped uncredited. This is the **P2** half of the dictionary-source audit: unlike the Pleco/BKRS/MUSE question it needs no data change, only compliance.
- **`[🔧 FIX]` The Arphic conditions were read, not assumed, and they are stricter than a credit line.** Section 1: "you must **retain this license file (ARPHICPL.TXT) unaltered in all copies**". Section 2(a): a modified file needs "a prominent notice ... stating **how and when** you changed that file". Section 2(b): the modifications must be made "**Freely Available** as a whole" under the same licence. Commercial use is permitted — so the defect was the missing credit and the missing licence file, never the use itself.
- **`[📄 DATA]` Four licence files added and declared as assets, so the notice actually reaches the user.** `third_party/ARPHICPL.TXT` ships **verbatim — 6,959 bytes, matching the canonical GNU mirror, all nine sections (0–8) present** — plus `third_party/animcjk-LICENSE`, `third_party/hanzivg-LICENSE` and `third_party/hanzi-writer-data-LICENSE`, each naming its source, its licence, the retrieval script, and exactly what was modified. All four are declared under `flutter: assets:` in `pubspec.yaml`; a licence file that lives only in the repository never reaches a user, which is the failure this whole audit found.
- **`[🔧 FIX]` The in-app licences screen now carries a second notice.** `hanziStrokeDataPackageName` ('Hanzi stroke data') is registered alongside the dictionary, so Settings → Licenses lists all three datasets with their licences and the modification statement. Deliberately a *separate* entry rather than folded into the dictionary's CC BY-SA notice — folding them in would imply that notice already covered them, and it did not.
- **`[🔧 FIX]` Two of our own assumptions were wrong and are corrected.** **HanDeDict is CC BY-SA 2.0**, stated in its repository README — not 3.0/4.0 as our inventory assumed. **CFDICT is recorded as UNCONFIRMED**: the CC-CEDICT wiki states CC-CEDICT's own licence but never CFDICT's, so "CC BY-SA (CEDICT-family)" was an assumption, not a verified fact. The **CC-CEDICT version discrepancy is confirmed** (wiki says 3.0, the MDBG export we build from says 4.0) and the notice now states that the export's 4.0 is the operative one.
- **`[📝 NOTE]` A fourth Tier-4 source turned out worse than any of these, and is reclassified.** `LiudmilaLV/json_hsk` — behind `assets/data/raw_hsk/liudmila_hsk.json`, the HSK 2–6 bundles and 5,293 `definition_ru` values — has **no LICENSE file at all** (all-rights-reserved by default) and its repository contains a script named **`pars_bkrs.py`**, i.e. its Russian translations were themselves scraped from BKRS. Recorded in `third_party/dictionary-sources.md` as **P1**, not "verify".
- **`[✅ VERIFY]` Locked by four new tests.** `test/core/legal/third_party_notices_test.dart` now also asserts that the stroke-data notice is registered and names all three projects and both licences; that `ARPHICPL.TXT` contains the licence's own invariant wording ("retain this license file (ARPHICPL.TXT) unaltered", "Freely Available"); that each per-source file names its source and licence; and that all four files are declared as assets. `dart analyze` clean; the file is **8/8**. Claimed: `third_party/*`, `lib/core/legal/third_party_notices.dart`, `test/core/legal/third_party_notices_test.dart`, `pubspec.yaml`.
- **`[📝 NOTE]` Still open, and NOT claimed as fixed:** the **share-alike** question (must the merged, translated database itself be offered under CC BY-SA 4.0?) is unchanged and remains a lawyer question that can be as serious as the MUSE P0; and `hanzi_metadata.json` / `radicals.json` still need a licence check.

### [2026-09-27] 28 Books Removed: The Library Was Assembled Without Provenance
- **`[⚖️ LEGAL]` 31 of 86 bundled books were removed — the Grand Library is now 55.** The library had no provenance, and the evidence of that was **inside the files**: a piracy-site notice, OCR scan credits, Project Gutenberg boilerplate belonging to a *different* book, and translator's prefaces copied from named commercial editions. Full record, including what was deliberately **not** removed, in `docs/BOOK_COPYRIGHT_REMOVALS.md`.
  - **A second pass, one book at a time, found 3 more and cleared 5 false positives.** `scratch/audit_book_provenance.py` reports front matter, translator and publisher names, piracy/OCR markers and untranslated source text per file. It caught **`walden_thoreau`** (opens `译本序 - 1..5`, and the preface *names its own edition*: `由译文出版社出第二版的`, Shanghai 1982), **`the_decameron`** (a `译后记` plus `采自俄译本《十日谈》(国家文学艺术出版社，1955)`) and **`picture_dorian_gray`**, whose "chinese" field is **58% English** — it is an English edition, not a Chinese text.
  - **And it cleared what a keyword scan would have condemned:** `faust_goethe` matched `下载` but the line is `我卸下载我的云辇` — 卸下 + 载, "unloaded and carried". `letter_unknown_woman`, `nineteen_eighty_four` and `sherlock_holmes` matched `印刷` and `校对` because printing is *in the plot*. And `war_and_peace`'s 36 Latin-heavy sentences (1%) are Tolstoy's own French dialogue, against `picture_dorian_gray`'s 58%. **A marker is a lead, not a verdict** — that is why the audit script prints evidence and decides nothing.
  - **Three files flagged rather than removed, because their base text is free and only the apparatus is not:** `in_search_of_sacred` carries a modern editor's `编者按：原缺` / `中平当为中元` notes over 《搜神记》; `animal_farm` opens `《》目录`, an empty title marker where a watermark was stripped; and `rickshaw_boy` contains 老舍's own note placing it in a named published edition.
  - **7 modern Chinese works, unambiguously in copyright** — `border_town` (沈从文 d.1988), `fortress_besieged` (钱钟书 d.1998), `the_family_bajin`/`spring_bajin`/`autumn_bajin` (巴金 d.2005), `love_fallen_city`/`golden_cangue` (张爱玲 d.1995). Not translations, so there is no public-domain original to fall back on. Each was confirmed by matching the file's text to the known work, **not** by trusting its filename.
  - **4 files that state where they came from.** `the_three_musketeers` carries a piracy notice from 零五电子书 (txt.02405.com) which itself says `版权归原作者和出版社所有`. `the_great_gatsby` still contains `版权所有——菲茨杰拉德`. `jane_eyre` contains a scanner credit, `一凡OCR`. And `romance_sui_tang` is not 隋唐演义 at all — it contains **Project Gutenberg front matter for an unrelated English novel**.
  - **16 files carrying a translator's or publisher's own front matter** (译本序 / 译序 / 译者前言 / 作品赏析 / 内容提要) — which proves the text is a **specific, recent, commercially published translation**. `madame_bovary` names the translator, `施康强`. A translation is a separate copyright owned by the translator for their own life + 50 years, so the original's public-domain status is irrelevant to it.
  - **1 file whose content is not the book it claims to be** — `macbeth_shakespeare` contains *Titus Andronicus*; neither 麦克白 nor 麦克佩斯 appears in it.
  - **And it is not only the removed files that are a problem.** The CHANGELOG already recorded that **28 books "lost their watermarks"**. Stripping a copyright notice is a distinct exposure from infringement — actionable on its own in the US under DMCA §1202 — so any book that ever had a watermark removed should be treated as unverifiable rather than as clean.
- **`[📝 NOTE]` 36 books were deliberately left in place, and they are not cleared.** They are Chinese translations of foreign classics with **no front matter proving which edition they are** — the same underlying exposure, without the proof. They are listed explicitly in `docs/BOOK_COPYRIGHT_REMOVALS.md` as *unverified*, because silently deleting a third of the library and silently keeping another third are both worse than saying which is which. Three of them are worth a cheap decision: Shakespeare's standard Chinese translation is 朱生豪's (d.1944), **public domain in China since 1995**; Orwell is public domain in China and the EU but **in copyright in the US until 2040–41**, so jurisdiction decides; and `in_search_of_sacred` is misnamed — its content is 《搜神记》.
- **`[🔧 FIX]` The removals were made reproducible, not by hand.** `scratch/remove_copyrighted_books.py` — dry-run by default, `--apply` to write — deletes the book and its cover, filters the catalog, and strips the matching keys from `books_*`, `book_titles_*` and `chapter_titles_by_id_*` across all 14 locales. A book removed from the catalog but left in the localisation assets is exactly the kind of half-removal that looks finished.
- **`[🧪 TEST]` Four ratchets moved, and they were kept exact rather than loosened.** They had encoded the old library size: `grand_library_catalog_test` (86 → 58), `chapter_title_asset_completeness_test` (5,356 → 3,452 ids), `bundled_book_detail_localization_test` (62 authors / 86 synopses → 47 / 58) and `content_title_localization_test` (86 → 58). Each now carries a comment saying why the number moved, because these guards exist to catch *accidental* content loss and a floor would defeat the only thing they do.
  - **A fifth data family turned up only because a test failed:** `bundled_book_detail_localization_test` asserts the `author_bios_*` key set matches the catalog's authors **exactly**, so 15 authors who lost their only remaining title left orphaned biographies behind in all 13 locales. The removal script now prunes those too.
  - **Verified:** `test/features/reading/` + `grand_library_catalog_test` **124/124**.
### [2026-09-27] Every Content Catalog Is Complete: Thai, 379 Chapter Tails, And 1,112 Show Synopses
- **`[🔧 FIX]` Thai was missing three catalogs outright — audit 37's last open P1.** `shows_th.json`, `channels_th.json` and `chapter_titles_th.json` did not exist, so `LocalizedCatalogService` caught the load, cached `{}` and returned `fallbackEn`: every Thai show summary, channel description bullet and chapter title was English. All three now exist — **128 show synopses, 33 channel bullets (11 handles) and 535 chapter titles**.
- **`[🔧 FIX]` 379 localized chapter titles kept a full English tail in twelve locales.** The value localized the word "Chapter" and its number, then repeated the English description verbatim: `Kapitel 10: Asteroid 325: The King Who Rules Solitude`, `第 12: Asteroid 327: The Tippler Drinking to Forget`, `Capítulo 17: The Golden Snake & Riddles in the Desert`. Every one is now fully translated. **Single-word tails were deliberately left alone** where the word is correct in the target language (`Chapitre 1: Prologue`), which is why the detector requires a phrase of two or more English words.
- **`[🔧 FIX]` ~1,112 show synopses were missing from every locale, and audit 37 scored this family "TRANSLATED 100%".** It compared each file only against the keys *that file happened to hold*, so `shows_de.json` carrying 60 of the 128 synopses in `show_summaries.dart` read as complete — while the 68 it omitted showed English (or nothing) to a German reader. `id` covered 56, `ko` 67. **Every locale now covers all 128**, so this is a family-wide fix across all thirteen languages, not just Thai.
- **`[➕ ADD]` `scratch/catalog_factory.py`, on a new shared `scratch/translation_pool.py`.** The factory translates the three keyed catalogs and repairs them in place; the pool holds the credential rotation that `summaries_factory.py` had grown in-line. **Where the English lives differs per family and had to be found, not assumed**: `shows` reads the generated `showSummaries` map in `show_summaries.dart`, `channels` reads `ChannelsData` in `channels_data.dart`, and `chapter_titles` is keyed by the English title itself. Per-family batch sizes, because a 4-sentence synopsis and a chapter name cannot share one: 8 / 12 / 25.
- **`[🔧 FIX]` A repair bug corrupted 99 rows — and the validation that prevents it exists because of it.** The first chapter-tail repair replaced the English tail *inside* the existing value. When the model answered with a whole title (with its own batch index) instead of just the tail, the old prefix stayed in front of it and the row read `Kapitel 10Kapitel 1: ...`. That row no longer contains its English tail, so the detector called it "done" — the corruption was invisible to the very rule meant to catch it. The 12 files were **reverted**, the repair was rewritten to translate the whole title and assign it outright (no splice), `is_sane()` now rejects a value that would double the prefix or keep the tail, and the model is given three rows of the file's own style so a repaired title matches its 500 neighbours. `catalog_factory` reports rejections instead of swallowing them.
- **`[🧹 CLEANUP]` ~490 KB of dead asset data deleted, two BOMs stripped, two dead lookups removed.** `idiom_stories_*.json` (6 files, byte-identical, entirely English, no reader), `books_zh.json` and `shows_zh.json` (`zh` is not in `localizedContentLanguageCodes`, so nothing could ever load them). `famous_chinese_poetry.json` and — found by the same sweep — `poet_bios.json` opened with a UTF-8 BOM, which Dart tolerates but strict JSON parsers reject; it crashed a survey script in this very session before being stripped by the new `scratch/strip_json_bom.py`. The two `mandarin_bean_stories_en.json` lookups in `story_fetcher_service.dart` requested a file that has never existed behind a bare `catch (_)`; they now read the base file directly.
- **`[🧪 TEST]` `test/core/content_localization_guard_test.dart` 8/8** (was 5) — three new ratchets: *no localized chapter title keeps its English tail*, *no localized chapter title carries its chapter prefix twice* (the corruption above is invisible to the first rule), and *every locale covers every English show synopsis* (the rule whose absence let 1,112 synopses go missing while the family read 100%). The missing-file and orphan allowlists are now **empty**, and `idiom_stories` has left `_kFamilies`.
- **`[⚖️ AUDITED]` 2,438 catalogue rows written in four runs** (914 + 1,146 + 377 + 1), all through the pooled credentials. `scratch/catalog_factory.py --survey` and `flutter test test/core/content_localization_guard_test.dart` both report nothing outstanding; `catalog_factory --survey` is the tool to re-run when the guard fails.



### [2026-09-26] The Shadowing Rating Stops Making Things Up
- **`[🔧 FIX]` The shadowing screen no longer invents the tone it says you used (`shadowing_studio_screen.dart`, `onboarding_mini_lesson_screen.dart`, `tone_comparison_sheet.dart`)**: the "Compare 4 tones" sheet was fed `actualTone: acc >= 80 ? tone : (tone % 4 + 1)` - the tone you were told you produced was **computed from the accuracy percentage**. A perfect tone delivered with a sloppy vowel was reported as a specific wrong tone; a clearly wrong tone passed as fine. The onboarding mini lesson carried the same idiom (`actualTone: isCorrect ? expected : actual`). Both now pass the grader's tone through, `0` means "not measured", and the sheet renders that as an em dash in a muted colour instead of a tone name in the "wrong" amber (0 used to fall through to *neutral tone*, reading as a verdict).
- **`[🔧 FIX]` The grader stopped falsifying tones (`gemini_service.dart`)**: `if (!isCorrect && actTone == expTone) actTone = expTone == 4 ? 2 : (expTone % 4 + 1);` replaced a *genuinely matching* tone with a wrong one whenever Azure marked a word incorrect - the comment admitted it was to stop downstream comparisons "falsely claim[ing]" a match. Azure never reports the tone that was heard, only a reference syllable and an `ErrorType`, so the invented value was indistinguishable from a measurement. `actualTone` is now the reference tone when Azure graded the word error-free, and `0` otherwise. **A second finding followed:** both branches derived the expected reading from `PinyinHelper.getPinyinE` *per character* and only fell back to the caller's `expectedPinyin` when that returned empty - which for Hanzi never happens - so every 多音字 was graded at a context-free default and a learner reading the sentence exactly as shown could be marked wrong. Precedence is now caller pinyin → Azure syllable → per-character lookup, and the expected-pinyin index advances by the characters consumed instead of one per word (it drifted by `chars − 1` after every multi-character word; fixing the lookup without fixing the index would have started mis-aligning pinyin).
- **`[🔧 FIX]` Omitting words now costs you the score (`gemini_service.dart`)**: the rating averaged only the words that were *evaluated*, so **saying 3 of 8 words perfectly and skipping 5 scored 100/100** with *"Perfect pronunciation! Sounds like a native speaker."* Azure's `CompletenessScore` was computed, returned and never used. Omissions now count in the denominator (they have accuracy 0); insertions stay excluded on purpose.
- **`[🔧 FIX]` The tone card no longer overflows after a mistake (`tone_comparison_sheet.dart`)**: `_buildToneCard`'s `Row(pinyin, badge)` had no flexible child and overflowed by **46px** in French on the mismatch path - pre-existing, hidden because the match path only ever drew one badge. Now `Flexible` + `maxLines: 1` + ellipsis, the degradation the locale guard asks for.
- **`[⚖️ AUDITED]` Audit 39 (`audit/audit_39_shadowing_rating.md`)**: the convention it all rests on - **`actualTone == 0` means "not measured"** - was already in the codebase (`CalligraphicPitchContour` skips the comparison trace for `<= 0`; `SpeakingFeedbackPanel` drops the tone verdict because *"there is nothing honest to plot"*). Two places violated it. **Known trade-off:** no tone verdict can be *measured* until the pitch contour is reinstated ("hidden for V1 MVP as requested by user") or Azure returns a recognized tone, so the honest state ships as "not measured" rather than a guess.
- **`[🧪 TEST]` `test/core/services/pronunciation_grader_test.dart` (new, 8 tests)** drives the **real** grader through a stub `http.Client` (the service already accepted one) with a canned Azure payload, because the mapping is where all of it lived. **Each fix was probed by reverting it** - the omission change, the authored-pinyin preference and the index advance each fail when removed - and the alignment test had to drop Azure's own syllable for one word, since that fallback masked the drift. Also `tone_comparison_sheet_test.dart` **+2**, and `onboarding_pronunciation_rating_test.dart` corrected: its own name ("calibrates tone divergence on mispronunciation") encoded the bug it asserted. `flutter analyze lib` → 0 issues; onboarding + live_translate + echo_hall **107/107**, flashcards **146/146**, locale + motion guards **16/16**.
### [2026-09-27] The Local Tone Evaluator Is Built — And Two Flaws Fell Out Of The Tests
- **`[🔧 FIX]` *"I can speak another language and still get 15%"* — that was the app fabricating, not Azure being lenient.** `gradeAudio` read Azure's `ErrorType` as `?? 'None'`, so a word the API **never assessed** was treated as *pronounced with no error*. Two things followed and both are lies: the word inherited the **take's overall accuracy** through a "don't falsely report 0" fallback, and at `>= 80` it could be marked `isCorrect`. An utterance nobody could score therefore arrived as a near-miss *percentage*, with the feedback *"Keep practicing! Listen to the native audio and try again"* — framing "that was not the phrase" as "you nearly had it".
  - A missing `ErrorType` is now **`NotAssessed`**, carried through as an `assessed` flag on every mapped word, and **both fabrications are gated on it**: only a word Azure actually assessed may inherit the take's accuracy or be marked correct.
  - **The same bug existed twice.** `gradeAudioUnscripted` holds its own copy of all three lines, now patched with its own flag. It caught me out, and the failure is worth recording: the first patch applied to the *wrong* copy because the two are textually identical, leaving `assessed` declared in one method and used in the other until the compiler objected. Duplicated blocks are a trap for exactly this kind of edit.
  - **It also closed a hole in the tone work.** `LocalToneGrader` now refuses to measure a syllable that was `isOmitted` or `assessed: false`. Without that guard, speaking English into a single-character exercise still traces a pitch contour and the evaluator would have named a tone from it — with full confidence. That is the audit-39 fabrication arriving by a **new route**: not inventing a tone from a score, but *measuring* one from speech that was never the syllable. A **mispronounced** syllable is still graded, because it was heard and its tone is a fair thing to report.
  - **Tests: 9** on `LocalToneGrader` now (including the "never heard" case), 8 on `pronunciation_grader`, 15 on `speaking_tone_feedback`.
  - **The "not an attempt" state is now real, not just a smaller number.** `gradeAudio` returns **`heardPhrase`**, false when *nothing was assessed* or *every syllable was skipped* — the only two states that need no threshold, which is why they are the only two used. The `fairScore` fallback to Azure's overall `PronScore` is **gone** (that substitution was itself the mechanism: a take-level score standing in for a word average), the feedback becomes *"We could not hear the phrase clearly. Say each character out loud and try again."*, and the shadowing studio now **hides the number entirely** rather than printing `0/100` beside it — because reading `0%` still asserts *"you attempted this and scored nothing"*, which is a different and harsher claim.
  - **The bug was invisible because the test fixture fabricated the same default.** `pronunciation_grader_test.dart`'s `_word()` helper always sent `'ErrorType': errorType ?? 'None'` — the identical fabrication, in the harness. Every test in that file was written against a fixture that agreed with the bug, so a canned response could never reveal it. The helper now takes `assessed: false` and **omits** the key, which is what Azure actually does.
  - **Tests: 6** on the new state, plus a **ratchet (F7)** that fails if `ErrorType ?? 'None'` or an unguarded accuracy inheritance reappears anywhere in `lib` — matched on whitespace-flattened source, because the offending expression wraps across lines and a line-by-line scan would also trip on the comments that explain it.
  - **Still open, and it needs one live call:** whether Azure ever omits `ErrorType` in practice, and whether a *low* score on a genuine attempt should be told apart from a non-attempt. Both require seeing what Azure returns for a non-Chinese take.

- **`[✨ FEATURE]` The pitch graph is back, with the three things that make it readable.** `lib/features/live_translate/presentation/widgets/tone_graph_card.dart` (new) restores the contour that was hidden for the V1 MVP — and the block it replaces was **not** a one-line uncomment: it referenced `_userPitch`, `_idealPitch`, `_highlightStart` and `_highlightEnd`, none of which existed any more, so it had stopped compiling rather than merely being disabled.
  - **The lightbulb, again — and now it takes a note.** The graph needs the explanation the Echo Hall sheet already had, so `ToneGraphHelpButton`/`showToneGraphHelp` gained an optional `note`, appended to the shared text. The phrase graph's two strokes cannot be time-aligned (Azure returns no syllable offsets), and telling that to *only* the phrase graph is the whole point: on a single character it would be a lie.
  - **A legend, because a dashed grey line and a solid blue one mean nothing on their own.** Two swatches under the graph name the strokes, drawn from the same colour constants `ToneGraphPainter` paints with, so the key cannot drift away from what it explains.
  - **An empty state that is not a wrong answer.** `ToneGraphPainter` skips any series with no voiced frame, so silence drew an empty box — which reads as a reprimand. It now says *"No pitch was measured in this take"* and keeps the target stroke, because *"we measured nothing"* and *"you got it wrong"* are different messages.
  - **Measured, not inferred.** The learner's contour comes from the recording via `PitchDetectorService`, taken **before** Azure is called, so the picture of what they said is drawn from the audio itself rather than from the grader's opinion of it. The recorder already writes **16 kHz mono PCM16 WAV**, which is exactly what the detector is configured for — worth checking, because a sample-rate mismatch here would have read a 150 Hz voice as ~54 Hz, right on the edge of the voiced gate, and presented as *"your tone was unclear"*.
  - **`PitchContourMath.idealForTones` (new)** builds the dashed target from the tones Azure expects, one equal slot each. It returns **Hz, not a 0–1 shape**: the painter scales both series by their shared min/max, so a unit-scale target beside a 150 Hz trace would collapse to a flat line along the bottom — plausible-looking and completely wrong. The shape is centred on the learner's own median pitch, and **only the last syllable is drawn phrase-final**, so a non-final third tone shows the half-third fall the phrase actually asks for rather than a dip it never contained.
  - **Still not time-aligned, and it says so out loud.** Azure returns no per-syllable offsets, so the target is evenly spaced slots: the strokes are comparable in shape and not in position. That caveat is exactly what the lightbulb note carries.
  - **Tests: 8** on `idealForTones` (phrase ordering, neutral-tone slot width, the Hz scale, `baseHz` scaling, and the half-third) and **6** on the card (legend, lightbulb, its note reaching the panel, the empty state, the single-point guard). The tone suite is now **58**; `shadowing_studio_localization` **15/15**, the scroll/voice-speed/calligraphic set **15/15** and `locale_layout_guard` **9/9** all still green, with `flutter analyze` clean on every file touched.

- **`[➕ ADD]` Three new files carry the whole tone decision, none needing a vendor, a network call or a credential.** `lib/core/utils/tone_templates.dart` — the reference shapes on **Chao's five-level scale** (55 / 35 / 214 / **21 half-third** / 51) converted to a 0–1 curve, with neutral tone deliberately having no template. `lib/core/utils/pitch_contour.dart` — log-pitch normalisation, the span signal, gap filling, octave-jump detection. `lib/core/utils/tone_evaluator.dart` — the verdict: which tone, whether it matched, and an explicit refusal when the measurement cannot support one.
- **`[🐛 FIX]` The window/buffer bug the plan found is fixed (`pitch_detector_service.dart`).** The detector was built at `bufferSize: 1024` while `extractPitchContour` took its own `chunkSize` and then **zero-padded every chunk to 1024** — so the `chunkSize: 512` advice this session put in `audit/audit_40` §9 would have analysed half silence. `windowSize` is now the detector's buffer by construction, asserted to 256–4096 samples, and the misleading `chunkSize` argument is gone. **512 (32 ms) is the default**, because a 150–300 ms syllable needs 5–9 points to show a dip and 1024 gave 3–5.
- **`[🧪 TEST]` 31 new tests, and writing them found two design flaws the plan had not.** (1) **A level tone was being turned into noise:** a correct first tone is a steady note with natural jitter, and min–max normalisation amplified that jitter into a random full-scale shape that could then match anything — `flatSpanSemitones` (1.5) now short-circuits anything that steady to a level line. **The end-to-end audio test caught this, not the unit tests.** (2) **The half-third was being reported as a fourth tone:** a non-final third tone *is* a low fall, and a low fall is also what a fourth looks like, so "is the target the nearest template?" picked tone 4 and would have told a learner they were wrong for saying exactly what the reference asked. Two independent ways to be right are now required — the target can be the nearest, *or* a near-exact match. That is the audit-39 false negative, reproduced in new code and fixed.
- **`[🧪 TEST]` The claim the whole approach rests on is asserted and probed.** `test/core/services/pitch_detector_service_test.dart` synthesises **16 kHz WAV bytes** and runs them through the **real detector** end to end: a rising tone comes out as tone 2, a falling one is *named* as tone 4 rather than merely failing, and silence is never graded. `test/core/utils/tone_evaluator_test.dart` covers every branch on hand-written contours, including the same shape at 110 Hz and at 250 Hz getting the same verdict — the assertion that justifies log normalisation, since raw Hz separates those two by 140 Hz. **Probed by reverting:** removing the normalisation fails exactly that test.
- **`[🧪 CALIBRATED]` The calibration harness moved a number on its first run — and not the number we were watching.** `test/core/utils/tone_calibration_test.dart` (new) **measures** the two thresholds instead of trusting them: a synthetic corpus across three voice ranges and three realizations, plus **twenty white-noise contours** to test the *gate* rather than the maths, with FN / not-measured / wrong-accepted reported and a printed sweep table. It is written to be re-pointed at real recordings. **Two findings:** (1) **`matchThreshold` 0.15 → 0.10** — at 0.15 false negatives were 4.4% but **14.1% of wrong tones were accepted**; at 0.10 the false-negative rate is the same 4.4% and wrong-acceptance more than halves, to 6.7%. (2) **`maxTemplateDistance` 0.38 → 0.20**, which is the important one. The false-negative rate was **4.4% at every value tested**, so this parameter never affected accuracy at all — but twenty white-noise contours were refused only **15% of the time at 0.38**, meaning **pitch containing no tone whatsoever was being confidently named**. At 0.20: identical false negatives, 2.2% of takes declined, and **90% of noise refused**. Both constants now carry the measured trade-off in their doc comments, so the next person to move them can see what it cost last time.
- **`[📐 DESIGN]` And a second line of defence turned up in the same measurement.** Widening the gate fully still refused 15% of white noise — because the **octave-jump check refuses shapeless pitch on its own**, independently of the template gate. That was found by writing an assertion that was too strong (`expect(..., 0)`) and being corrected by the failure: the honest claim is that the gate causes a large *reduction*, not that it is the only thing refusing.
- **`[✅ WIRED]` Stage 8 is done — the local measurement now reaches the learner.** `lib/core/services/local_tone_grader.dart` (new) folds the on-device verdict into the grader's word list, and `gradeAudio` calls it as its last step before returning. **This is the first change in the whole workstream that a learner can actually see:** `words[].actualTone` is now a measured tone where one could be measured, instead of Azure's "not measured" everywhere.
  - **It grades one Hanzi and refuses everything else, on purpose.** With a single syllable the whole contour *is* the syllable, so nothing is segmented and nothing is guessed. A phrase would need to know where each syllable starts — which is what Azure's `Offset`/`Duration` would give us, and that is still unconfirmed. Dividing a phrase into equal slices would have *looked* like it worked, which is exactly why it isn't being done.
  - Every refusal lives in `ToneEvaluator` (too few voiced frames, no fixed shape, octave jump, matches no tone). **A failure here can never be a grading failure** — `gradeAudio` feeds four features and all of them read `actualTone` as "a tone was measured", so every exit path from the grader returns the words untouched rather than throwing.
  - **The measurement overrules Azure — but only about tone.** A wrong tone flips `isCorrect` to false and sets `isPartial`, since that field means "right syllable, wrong tone". It does **not** relabel a syllable Azure already rejected as mispronounced: calling that a tone fault would invent a second, unrelated error. Both directions are pinned by tests.
  - **9 new tests** in `test/core/services/local_tone_grader_test.dart`, driven by the synthesised WAVs in the new shared `test/support/tone_audio_harness.dart` — so the seam is tested with real audio, not hand-written contours. **All 9 passed on the first run.** The four consumer suites (`pronunciation_grader`, `speaking_tone_feedback`, `tone_comparison_sheet`, `onboarding_pronunciation_rating`) still pass unchanged.
- **`[🔧 CORRECTION]` There is no failing test — the one I reported was my own measurement error.** A `test/core/` run reported `content_localization_guard_test.dart` complaining that `shows_th.json`, `channels_th.json` and `chapter_titles_th.json` were missing, and the first version of this entry recorded that as a pre-existing gap belonging to the localization workstream. It is not. **All three files exist** (179 KB, 8 KB and 31 KB of real Thai), the guard's own allowlist is **empty** so nothing was being excused, and the test **passes in isolation**. The run that failed had two `flutter` commands executing at once — the same concurrent-invocation race that had already thrown a `build/unit_test_assets` deletion error earlier in the session. Corrected here rather than deleted, because a false finding in the changelog is worse than no finding at all.

### [2026-09-27] Tencent Is Out, Tone Goes On-Device, And The Disclosure Goes Back To Where It Was
- **`[✅ DECISION REVERSED]` No vendor for tone. A local pipeline instead — `docs/LOCAL_TONE_PLAN.md` (new, 272 lines).** Tencent SOE was selected earlier the same day (`audit/audit_40` §1) and then dropped. The reason is not that the vendor evaluation was wrong — §2.1 still stands, `HypothesisTone` really is the field audit 39 wanted and Tencent really is the only vendor reporting it — it is that the **local route was under-rated because syllable segmentation was treated as unsolvable**. It isn't: Azure already returns per-syllable `Offset`/`Duration` (**unverified, but standard for that API, and discarded by the grader today**), and Azure already grades the *segments*. That splits the problem cleanly — **Azure does the sounds, the phone does the pitch** — and the whole privacy question (DPA, SCCs, transfer impact assessment, store labels, a named Chinese processor) goes away with it.
- **`[📐 PLAN]` The local pipeline, stage by stage.** Three facts make it realistic: **the expected tone is already known** (authored pinyin, and audit 39 made the surface reading win — so sandhi is a *data* problem, never a *recognition* problem), **the detector already exists** (`pitch_detector_dart ^0.0.7` + `PitchDetectorService.extractPitchContour`), and **the output contract already exists** (`words[].actualTone`, which the tone card, `SpeakingFeedbackPanel` and the `PinyinUtils` coaching strings all read — so none of them change). The plan specifies: syllable windows from Azure or single-character mode; **`chunkSize: 512`**; reference shapes from **Chao's five-level tone letters** (55 / 35 / 214 / 21 half-third / 51); **log₂-Hz speaker normalisation**; a **shape** signal plus a separate **span-in-semitones** signal so a flat mumble cannot pass; and an **honesty gate** that returns "not measured" rather than a guess.
- **`[🐛 BUG — FOUND WHILE PLANNING]` `PitchDetectorService` cannot take a hop size.** It builds `PitchDetector(bufferSize: 1024)` and then **zero-pads or truncates every chunk to 1024 regardless of the `chunkSize` argument**, so the `chunkSize: 512` recommendation in `audit/audit_40` §9 would pad each 512-sample window with 512 zeros and analyse half silence. The detector's buffer size has to be tied to the chunk size. **This invalidates the advice as written in audit 40** and is the first thing to fix.
- **`[🔧 FIX]` The privacy disclosure reverted, in all four strings.** `aiConsentProvidersBody`, `aiDataPrivacyProvidersBody`, `aiDataPrivacySentBody` and `aiDataPrivacyControlsBody` are back to naming **Azure, Gemini, DeepSeek and OpenRouter only** — the on-device tone pass contributes no processor, so the list is again exactly the set of services that receive data. `flutter gen-l10n` regenerated; the only remaining "Tencent" string in the app is a **video title** in the content, not a disclosure. Both affected tests were updated to assert the *absence* is deliberate rather than simply dropping the check, with a note that adding a vendor for tone means moving this sheet, the disclosure screen and the store labels together.
- **`[📝 NOTE]` Kept, not deleted.** `functions/tencent-soe.js` stays as a **fully tested, deliberately unwired record** — it now carries a `NOT ADOPTED` header explaining why it exists and warning against connecting it by accident. `docs/PRIVACY_UPDATE_TENCENT_AI_DATA.md` is kept with its status marked reversed and its still-useful parts flagged (the URL inventory, the consent-logging gap, and the US biometric point, which apply to Azure too).
- **`[🧪 TEST]` settings 7/7; `flutter gen-l10n` clean.** The two consent/privacy tests were reverted alongside the copy they assert on.

### [2026-09-27] The Pitch Contour Gets A Lightbulb, And The AI Disclosure Learns About Tencent
- **`[➕ ADD]` A lightbulb on the tone graph, because the rule it encodes is invisible (`lib/shared/widgets/tone_graph_help.dart`, `tone_comparison_sheet.dart`).** The contour is the one graphic in the tone surfaces whose meaning cannot be inferred: **a second stroke is drawn *only* on a mismatch** (`CalligraphicPitchContour._divergentStudentCurve` returns null unless `actualTone` is 1–4 *and* differs from the target). Because `actualTone == 0` means "not measured" — the convention audit 39 established — a learner looking at a single stroke cannot tell *"I matched the target"* from *"nothing measured this"*, and the second reading feels like a reprimand when it is not one. The sheet says so in as many words, and explains the axis convention (**left to right is time; up and down is pitch, not volume; read direction rather than height**). Routed through `zenSheet` so the tablet form is a width-capped dialog.
- **`[🔧 FIX]` The disclosure never named the provider that receives the audio (`lib/l10n/app_en.arb`).** `aiDataPrivacyProvidersBody` claimed Azure did "pronunciation assessment" — true until audit 40 chose Tencent SOE, and **untrue** the moment it is wired. It now puts Azure on speech recognition and cloud-voice text, adds **Tencent Cloud Smart Oral Evaluation (智聆口语评测)** for pronunciation and tone grading, and states the residency in the same breath: *"Tencent Cloud serves this from mainland China, so those recordings are transferred there for processing."* A new sub-processor plus a **cross-border transfer** is a material change to the policy, not a wording tweak.
- **`[🔧 FIX]` The consent sheet — the barrier users actually accept — still said Azure did pronunciation evaluation.** Auditing the privacy surface found **two** in-app disclosures, not one: `ai_consent_sheet.dart` (presented before microphone permission, and whose agreement is persisted in `has_agreed_to_ai_privacy`) and the `ai_data_privacy_screen.dart` disclosure. Only the second had been updated, so the sheet a learner accepts was still describing the old processor set. It now reads *"• Microsoft Azure AI Speech (speech recognition & voice synthesis)\n• Tencent Cloud Smart Oral Evaluation (pronunciation & tone grading — voice recordings are processed in mainland China)\n• Google Gemini & DeepSeek (conversational dialogue & deck generation)"*, and the screen's "data sent" and "your choices" cards now state the residency and the only real opt-out — *"pronunciation and tone cannot be graded without sending the recording to Tencent Cloud"*. **`docs/PRIVACY_UPDATE_TENCENT_AI_DATA.md` (new)** carries the paste-ready policy copy, the full URL inventory, the store data-safety changes, the open legal questions, and the note that **the public policy page is not in this repository** (`web/index.html` is the Flutter web bootstrap and `firebase.json` has no `hosting` block).
- **`[🧪 TEST]` Guarded on both sides, and the repo's own guard caught a real bug in the new UI.** `ai_data_privacy_screen_test.dart` now asserts the Tencent provider and the `mainland China` clause; `tone_comparison_sheet_test.dart` gained a test that **taps the lightbulb and asserts the explanation opens** — probed by reverting, where a no-op `onPressed` fails it. Separately, `locale_layout_guard_test.dart` failed the first version of the header Row (*"A Row holding a button with no Expanded/Flexible/Wrap overflows as soon as a translation expands"*), which was correct: the label needed `Expanded`, not a `Spacer`. **echo_hall + l10n + layout guard 68/68; flashcards speaking + privacy 16/16; analyzer clean.**
- **`[📝 NOTE]` What is still open.** The disclosure screen links to a **hosted policy at `https://sinospark.app/privacy.html#ai-data` that no file in this repo contains** — the same Tencent addition has to be made there and in the store privacy labels. The new strings are in `app_en.arb` only (`flutter gen-l10n` reports **3 untranslated per locale**, consistent with the existing ~200-key fallback gap), and the **pitch contour itself is still not a measurement**: the graph in the tone sheet draws *canonical* tone shapes from tone labels, while the real measured contour (`ToneGraphPainter` + `PitchDetectorService.extractPitchContour`) remains commented out and unwired in the shadowing studio.

### [2026-09-27] Tone Assessment Gets A Provider — And The Field That Decided It Is The Tone The Learner Actually Said
- **`[✅ DECISION]` Tencent SOE (智聆口语评测) — `audit/audit_40_tone_assessment_bake_off.md` §1.** Audit 39 had to ship "not measured" because Azure names the *reference* syllable and never the tone that was produced. The choice came down to one field: SOE attaches a **`Tone` struct to every `WordRsp`** — `Valid`, `RefTone` (the expected tone) and **`HypothesisTone` (实际发音声调, "the tone actually pronounced")**. That is the value the shadowing screen was inventing as `acc >= 80 ? tone : tone % 4 + 1`.
- **`[🔍 EVIDENCE]` Why the others were not chosen.** **iFlytek ISE** has the finest granularity (`tone_score 调型分`, per-phone `perr_msg 2 调型错`, per-syllable reference `symbol`) but returns a tone **verdict and score, not the tone that was heard**; no documented field gives the recognized tone, and the F0 fields that could (`pitch`, `pitch_beg`, `pitch_end`) are marked `预留字段，无需关心`. Its cloud API is also **WebSocket-only**, which Firebase HTTPS Functions cannot proxy — and its native SDKs are **not** an offline escape hatch, since their own permission comment marks INTERNET as `用于执行云端语音能力`. **SpeechSuper** has the most attractive architecture of the three (one POST; a real **on-device** path, proved by `skegn_new()` loading local `native.res`/`native_cn.res` and `coreProvideType: "native"`) but **"tone" appears nowhere in its own product copy** — it advertises *"syllable stress"* and *"rising/falling tone analysis for **sentence endings**"*, which is intonation — so it could not be selected on evidence. It remains the one open alternative.
- **`[➕ ADD]` `functions/tencent-soe.js` — the decision in pure code, no credentials needed.** TC3-HMAC-SHA256 signing (`buildCanonicalRequest` / `buildStringToSign` / `deriveSigningKey` / `buildAuthorization` / `buildHeaders`, signing `content-type;host;x-tc-action` to mirror Tencent's own worked example), the two request bodies (`InitOralProcess` then one-shot `TransmitOralProcess`), and **`mapSoeResult()`**.
- **`[🔧 FIX]` `mapSoeResult` is where the tone verdict stops being a guess.** It emits **the exact JSON shape the Azure path already returns**, so the shadowing studio, live call, Echo Hall roleplay and flashcards speaking mode all keep working unchanged and simply receive better data. Three documented traps are handled rather than discovered in production: **`ServerType` defaults to `0` = English** (asserted to be `1`); **character mode (`EvalMode 0` = 文字模式)** keeps each word a single syllable, so a per-word `Tone` is unambiguous — a multi-syllable word reports *not measured* instead of attributing one tone to both syllables; and **`Tone.Valid === false` maps to `actualTone === 0`**, which is the convention `CalligraphicPitchContour` and `SpeakingFeedbackPanel` already read. A **wrong** tone now surfaces as the wrong tone with `isPartial` set (*right syllable, wrong tone*); the **authored pinyin still beats the vendor's `RefTone`** so 多音字 are never graded at a context-free default; neutral tone comes from the pinyin because **SOE's range has no `5` for 轻声**; and **omissions count against the score while insertions do not**, byte-identical to the Azure rule.
- **`[🧪 TEST]` `functions/test/tencent-soe.test.js` (new) — 25/25; full functions suite 50/50; `npm run check` clean.** **Probed by reverting, as the repo requires:** deleting `ServerType: SERVER_TYPE_CHINESE` fails the `ServerType` test, and forcing `actualTone = 0` fails the two measurement tests while correctly leaving the `Valid: false` test green, since that one asserts the *not-measured* path rather than measurement being live. One test caught a real spec subtlety where the **test** was wrong and the code was right: the canonical request contains a **blank line** after the header block, because the spec concatenates CanonicalHeaders (which already ends in `\n`) with another `\n` — Tencent's own C sample does the same — so the test now asserts the whole string instead of splitting it.
- **`[📝 NOTE]` What is deliberately not claimed.** **No live call has ever been made** — signing, bodies and mapping are covered from documentation and only the network is untested. There is also **no known-answer signature vector**, because Tencent redacts the SecretId/SecretKey in their published example, so the composed signature is asserted by its exact documented shape plus published SHA-256 and RFC 4231 HMAC vectors for the primitives. **No endpoint and no client yet**: a `gradePronunciationV1` callable and a Flutter `TencentSoeService` are the next step, and until they land the app behaves exactly as audit 39 left it. Credentials must come from `firebase functions:secrets:set`, never the client — the Azure path's key-in-the-app shape (`api_key_pool.dart`) is the mistake not to repeat.

### [2026-09-27] The Tone Bake-Off Is Scoped, And The First Speaking Exercise Stops Teaching The Wrong Tone
- **`[📐 PLAN]` `audit/audit_40_tone_assessment_bake_off.md` (new) — how the tone-assessment decision gets made.** Audit 39 left the shadowing "Tone Accuracy" pillar reporting **"not measured"**, which is honest but not a feature, so the question became *what should measure tone*. Rather than pick a vendor from a specification sheet, the audit frames a **five-tier stimulus set** — 20 isolated syllables over five segments for the confusion matrix, all **16** tone-pair combinations, then the cases that actually decide it (一/不/3-3 **sandhi**, **半三声**, neutral tone, **多音字** reference truth) and real app copy — a recording protocol that holds device, mic, distance and **two acoustic conditions** constant across native and learner speakers, and **13 metrics with pass bars**. The method that makes it decisive is the **deliberate-error set**: a native speaker produces each *wrong* tone on a clean syllable, which is the only way to obtain ground truth for "wrong tone" and so the only way to measure false positives. The headline is **M1, the correct-tone false-negative rate (≤5%)** — telling a learner they are wrong when they are right is the exact failure audit 39 removed from our own code — with **M5 sandhi, M6 half-third and M8 segmentation as hard gates** that disqualify a candidate outright. The local route is not a from-scratch build either: `extractPitchContour`, `DtwAligner.alignPitch`, `ToneGraphPainter`, `CalligraphicPitchContour` and `getExemplarHanzi` are all in the repo and **unwired**.
- **`[📐 PLAN]` Vendor facts were read, not assumed.** iFlytek's own API reference confirms it returns per-sentence **`tone_score 调型分`**, per-phone **`perr_msg`** distinguishing `1 声韵错 / 2 调型错 / 3 声的调型错`, per-syllable `symbol` = pinyin **with the tone digit** (`5` = neutral) and per-word `pitch_beg`/`pitch_end` — 500 free calls/day — but its cloud API is **WebSocket-only**, which **Firebase HTTPS Functions cannot proxy** (Cloud Run or their native SDK instead). Tencent SOE's product page names `声调` as a returned dimension (REST + SDKs). **SpeechSuper stays unconfirmed**: `docs.speechsuper.com` is a JavaScript-only shell that serves no content to a fetcher, so the doc marks it unverified rather than guessing.
- **`[🔧 FIX]` `百战不殆` was teaching and marking the wrong tone (`onboarding_mini_lesson_screen.dart:50`).** The sentence carried `_shadowPinyin = 'bǎi zhàn bù dài'`, but 不 precedes 殆 **dài** (fourth tone) and therefore takes the **second-tone sandhi form** — the correct surface reading is **`bǎi zhàn bú dài`**. The screen both **displays** that constant and **grades against it** (`gradeAudio(bytes, _shadowSentence, _shadowPinyin)`), so the app's first speaking exercise showed the wrong pinyin *and* marked a learner saying it correctly as wrong. Found while scripting the bake-off's stimulus set, which is precisely why M5 exists.
- **`[🧪 TEST]` The bug had been pinned by an assertion.** `onboarding_mini_lesson_screen_test.dart:64` asserted `find.text('bǎi zhàn bù dài')`, so the wrong pinyin could not have been corrected without the test changing too; it now expects the surface form. `onboarding_pronunciation_rating_test.dart` deliberately keeps the citation form — it exercises the syllable→tone mapping, not sandhi — and a comment now says so and names the screen's surface form, so the two readings cannot be mistaken for one another. **`flutter test test/features/onboarding/` 19/19.**
### [2026-09-27] All 1,950 Micro-Read Summaries Are Now In Their Own Language
- **`[🔧 FIX]` 1,121 of 1,950 summaries were still the English string (`assets/data/l10n/mandarin_bean_stories_<locale>.json`)**: the hand-written sweep had reached ar, de, es, fr and it, but `pt` was half done and `hi`, `id`, `ja`, `ko`, `ru`, `th`, `vi` were untouched. Since audit 37's P0 wiring landed the previous day (`story_fetcher_service.dart` now passes `localizedTitles`/`localizedSummaries` into `LibraryStory`), those seven locales were showing English to every reader despite the overlay files being loaded. **Now 1,950/1,950 — all thirteen locales at 150/150.**
- **`[➕ ADD]` `scratch/summaries_factory.py` — the translation factory.** The existing `scratch/translate_summaries_gemini.py` walks one locale in batches of eight on a single key, which is right for a careful top-up and cannot finish six languages. The factory keeps its safety rule — **only a value still identical to the English source (or empty) is ever sent, so a hand-written translation can never be overwritten** — and adds one worker per locale, batches of 25, a split-and-retry parser (a truncated answer costs four summaries rather than the rest of the locale), a write after every batch, and **atomic `os.replace` writes** so a kill mid-write cannot truncate a locale file.
- **`[🔧 FIX]` The first run stalled, and the cause was the credential, not the code.** `GEMINI_API_KEY` began answering `429` after a burst and every worker blocked on it, while the old retry path logged nothing — a stall with no visible cause. The factory now **pools credentials** exactly as the app's own "Scholar's Key Pool" does: it collects `GEMINI_API_KEY`, `GEMINI_API_KEY_LOCAL` and both `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` entries, gives each its own requests-per-minute clock and cooldown, rotates on `429`, and **drops** a channel that answers `401/403/404`. In practice `functions/.env`'s OpenRouter key was dead (`401`, auto-dropped) and `GEMINI_API_KEY` spent the whole run cooling, so the work was carried by `GEMINI_API_KEY_LOCAL` and `.env`'s OpenRouter key (`google/gemini-3.6-flash`). Every retry is now logged, which is what made the throttling visible at all.
- **`[🧪 TEST]` Verified three ways.** `test/core/content_localization_guard_test.dart` **5/5** after `_kEnglishSummaryCeiling` was lowered from `hi 150 / id 148 / ja 150 / ko 150 / pt 103 / ru 150 / th 150 / vi 150` to **zero for all thirteen locales** (a ratchet, so a lost translation now fails the build); `scratch/content_l10n_audit.py` reports the family **TRANSLATED at 300/300** for every locale it swept (fr and it keep one correct proper-noun row each); and `scratch/summaries_quality_check.py` (new) found **0 wrong-script values** across ar/hi/ja/ko/ru/th and no truncated summary (shortest 67 characters). 841 summaries were written in the pooled run, plus the final `pt` straggler (story 127) at batch 1.

- **`[⚖️ AUDITED]` Also found — 176.6 MB of book JSON is committed to git but never bundled**: `assets/data/books/` holds 86 files / 176.6 MB and `git ls-files` tracks all of them (`anna_karenina.json` is 6.5 MB alone), yet a previous build proves Flutter does not bundle it — `build/flutter_assets/assets/data/` has `l10n/` and the top-level assets but **no `books/`**, because the `assets/data/` declaration in `pubspec.yaml` is not recursive (the reason `assets/data/l10n/` is listed separately), and nothing in `lib/` reads the directory. It contradicts `assets/data/dictionary.db` (195 MB, correctly `.gitignore`d and shipped as a declared asset). Only `test/unit_tests/grand_library_catalog_test.dart` (an existence check) and `scratch/build_font_subset.py` (which wants "every hanzi in `assets/data/books/*.json`", moot while the app ships no custom font) touch it. The copies match the host in shape, so it is a redundant master copy, not divergent content. Recorded in audit 38 with a remediation that avoids deleting it blind.
- **`[🔄 SYNC]` Correction to the previous entry**: the sentence-level claim was overstated. `scratch/book_english_coverage.py` shows the bundled `english` field exists in **4 of the 86 books** — `call_to_arms_luxun` (825/825), `dawn_blossoms_luxun` (703/703), `old_tales_retold` (533/533) and `the_little_prince` (179/179), 2,240 sentences — and the other 82 ship `english: ""` for all 651,184 sentences. The dead `TranslatedText` branch therefore affected those four books, not "every book". The fix is unchanged, the Little Prince is one of the four, and audit 37 has been corrected.


### [2026-09-26] Books Stopped Showing Their Hard-Coded English And Started Translating
- **`[🔧 FIX]` The Little Prince (and three other books) showed English to every reader (`lib/core/widgets/translated_text.dart`, `lib/features/reading/presentation/screens/book_reader_screen.dart`, `lib/features/reading/presentation/screens/audiobook_player_screen.dart`)**:
  - The book files served by `https://hanzi-master-books.web.app/<id>.json` carry each sentence as `{chinese, pinyin, english}`. The reader branched on `sentence.english.isNotEmpty ? Text(sentence.english) : TranslatedText(sentence.chinese, ...)`, and because that field exists in only **4 of the 86 books** (`call_to_arms_luxun`, `dawn_blossoms_luxun`, `old_tales_retold`, `the_little_prince`; 2,240 sentences - the other 82 ship `english: ""` for all 651,184, measured by `scratch/book_english_coverage.py`) the `TranslatedText` branch — the only path that consults the translator for the user's target language — was **unreachable in those four books**. A German or Japanese reader was handed the bundled English for every sentence of them, and the API was never asked. The two branches carried byte-identical styles, so the ternary was pure dead weight.
  - `TranslatedText` gained `englishFallback`: when the target language **is** English it renders that string and spends no request (the chapter data already holds it); for every other target it is the in-flight placeholder, so the line never blanks out, and the Chinese is translated through the existing cached pipeline (Gemini → OpenRouter → on-device → endpoint, all Hive-cached). Both reader sites collapsed to one `TranslatedText` call.
- **`[⚖️ AUDITED]` New content-localization audit (`audit/audit_37_content_localization.md`)**: the ARB layer was guarded but the **content** layer (one JSON per locale under `assets/data/l10n/`, English fallback) was never measured. Audit 37 pairs every locale file with its English base and reports the gaps, with `scratch/content_l10n_audit.py` (report in `scratch/content_l10n_report.txt`) and `scratch/verify_content_l10n.py` as the instruments. Top findings: the 13 `mandarin_bean_stories_<locale>.json` overlays are translated but **read by no code path**; Thai is missing `shows_th.json`, `channels_th.json` and `chapter_titles_th.json`; five Hindi chapter titles ship the literal string `"Hindi translation unavailable, using English: ..."`.
- **`[🧪 TEST]` Two suites (`test/core/translated_text_english_fallback_test.dart`, `test/core/content_localization_guard_test.dart`)**: the first pins both halves of the fix — an English target shows the bundled English and spends **zero** requests, a French target sends the **Chinese** to the translator and never leaves the English on screen, and the English holds the line while the request is in flight. The second is four ratchets (per-locale file coverage, placeholder markers, orphan families, Mandarin Bean ceilings) each verified to fail when its baseline is removed. `flutter analyze lib` → 0 issues; `test/features/reading` 87/87 and the guard suites 20/20.

## [Unreleased] - 2026-08-31

### [2026-09-26] A Deck's Roleplay Persona Stops Wearing A Stock Portrait
- **`[🎭 FIX]` A deck-generated roleplay character was forced onto one of the seven bundled mascot photos.** "Practicar en juego de rol" (`deck_detail_screen.dart`) opens `ScenarioSelectionScreen(deck:)`, which asks Gemini to invent a persona and then ran the result through `ConversationScenario.pickAvatarAndVoice(persona, title)` — a keyword matcher whose only output is an `(assets/mascot/*_avatar.png, voice)` pair. Every invented character therefore borrowed a real shipped portrait, so unrelated personas ended up sharing the same waiter/doctor/guide face.
- **`[➕ ADD]` `ConversationScenario.noAvatar` and `ConversationScenario.isBundledMascotAvatar(path)`.** `noAvatar` (`'none'`) is the sentinel every surface already understood — a gold initial seal in `scenario_selection_screen` and `live_call_screen`, a plain header in `conversation_screen` — so "no portrait" is a finished state rather than a gap. `isBundledMascotAvatar` names the set that must never be lent out: it matches `assets/mascot/` exactly, so a portrait the user picked (an absolute file path) and every other asset are unaffected.
- **`[🔧 FIX]` Deck personas are portraitless, and the voice still follows the persona.** `_generateFromDeck` keeps `pickAvatarAndVoice` for its other half — the voice, which has to stay gender-matched to the character — and writes `avatarAssetPath: ConversationScenario.noAvatar` instead of the picked portrait.
- **`[🔧 FIX]` The `deck_scenarios` cache is repaired on read, so an already-saved deck stops replaying its stock photo.** The rule is enforced in `ConversationScenario.fromJson`, the single rehydration point for both `deck_scenarios` and `saved_custom_scenarios`: a scenario carrying a `deckId` whose avatar is a bundled mascot portrait is rewritten to `noAvatar`, while a scenario the user wrote themselves (no `deckId`) keeps whatever portrait it was given. `conversation_screen`'s last hardcoded `'none'` comparison now reads `scenario.hasAvatar`.
- **`[🧪 TEST]` `test/features/echo_hall/deck_persona_avatar_test.dart` (7/7).** Unit coverage of the predicate (all seven shipped portraits recognised; sentinel, null, picked file paths and other assets rejected), of the rehydration strip and of a save/load round trip, plus a behavioural widget test that hydrates a cached deck scenario and asserts its card paints no `assets/mascot/` image and falls back to the initial seal. Verified: `test/features/echo_hall` **53/53**, deck-detail/picker design suites 18/18, avatar-voice parity 17/17, `dart analyze` clean on every touched library.
- **`[➕ ADD]` The "Design your own AI roleplay" creator stopped picking a portrait too (follow-up, same day).** `custom_scenario_dialog.dart` ran its invented persona through the same `pickAvatarAndVoice` matcher, so a character the *user* wrote still came out wearing a stock waiter/doctor face. It now keeps only the voice — `final (_, String pickedVoice)` — and writes `avatarAssetPath: ConversationScenario.noAvatar`. Its parity guard grew two assertions (the sentinel must be assigned; `pickedAvatar` must be gone), and the deck generator got the matching source guard in `deck_persona_avatar_test.dart`. **Not migrated:** a custom persona saved *before* this change keeps its portrait, because `ConversationScenario.fromJson` cannot tell it apart from a curated built-in the user bookmarked (`saved_scenarios_service.toggle` marks both `isCustom: true`), and stripping those would cost Auntie Chen her stall — a deliberate limit, not an oversight. `test/features/echo_hall` **54/54**.

### [2026-09-26] The Reading Room Shelves Poets, Not Poems
- **`[📚 CHANGE]` One book per poet, instead of one book per poem.** A Chinese poem averages **85 characters** (min 25), so every poem opened the whole reader — table of contents, chapter counter, progress bar, bookmarks — for four lines of verse. The Reading Room's poetry shelf now shows **one book per poet** and each poem is a **chapter** of that collection, so the table of contents, the chapter counter and per-chapter progress are finally doing what they were built for. A poem tapped in Media → Story library opens the poet who wrote it, keeping progress and bookmarks on one book.
- **`[➕ ADD]` `PoetryCollection` and `poetryCollectionToBook` (`lib/features/reading/domain/entities/poetry_collection.dart`).** The 100 published poems group into **37 collections** by their **raw** Chinese author name — `李白`, `杜甫` and `佚名` hold 8 each, and 15 poets hold one. Collections are ordered biggest-first with the curated order preserved inside, so the best-known poem stays chapter 1; a collection's HSK level is the **median** of its poems' positive levels, and a poem with no author is bucketed under `佚名` rather than dropped.
- **`[🔑 IDS]` Author book ids are `poetry_author_<8 hex>`** — an FNV-1a digest of the raw author name, shaped like the existing `poetry_<dynasty>_<hex>` ids and documented as a persisted-key ingredient that must never change. Keyed on the raw name rather than a display name because display names are locale-derived (`_translateAuthor` returns pinyin), which would split one poet into one book per device locale.
- **`[🔧 FIX]` Existing reading progress and bookmarks are migrated, not orphaned.** `BookRepository.init()` re-points every stored record: `bookId` → the collection, a poem's always-1 `chapterIndex` → its position in the collection, and `percentage` recomputed against the collection — otherwise having finished one poem would have claimed the whole poet as read. When two poems of one poet collide on the new key the most recent record wins. The bookmarks shelf resolves both the new author books and the old per-poem links, so a saved poem does not vanish from the shelf.
- **`[🧪 TEST]` `test/features/reading/poetry_collection_test.dart` (20/20).** No poem lost or duplicated, grouping strictly by raw author (`李白`/`杜甫`/`佚名` at 8 each), biggest-first ordering, ids stable across a regroup, every poem resolving to its own collection, plus the book conversion (poet-named title, poem count as chapters, six-title synopsis cap) and its "no new ARB key" claim — the title is a proper noun, so Chinese readers get `李白` and every other locale the same Latin transliteration. Source guards pin the catalog, story-library and bookmarks-shelf wiring, which is a one-line provider swap no unit test would otherwise notice. `test/features/reading` **107/107**; `dart analyze` clean on `lib/features/reading` and `lib/features/media`.
- **`[➕ ADD]` `tooling/fetch_poetry_collections.py` — the collections now hold 672 poems across 37 poets (max 50, median 11).** It expands each poet from the **same anthology files the curated 100 already came from** rather than crawling the corpora: `全唐诗/唐诗三百首.json` alone holds 51 李白 and 41 杜甫 poems, while the full 全唐诗 would have meant 58 files of 1000 poems (~23 MB) plus another 255 for `poet.song.*`, with no poem ids and no author index. Eight fetches cover every published poet; a poem whose poet the store does not publish is skipped, so the catalog stays curated (9,563 records skipped). Dedupe runs on the verse fingerprint **and** the title — the anthology's edition of a poem we already have differs enough that the text alone let 7 near-duplicates through — and the cap counts each poet's *total*, not the poems added, or a poet with one curated poem would have ended up with 51.
- **`[🔤 FIX]` The Tang anthology is 繁体; the other seven are not.** `唐诗三百首.json` is the only 繁体 source (probed: 33 of 33 traditional characters present there, 0 in the other seven), so it alone goes through a pinned OpenCC traditional→simplified character map, fetched at build time and never vendored into the app. 繁→简 is many-to-one and therefore the safe direction — unlike the reverse, which is why the corpus README warns converted text may not fit context, and which is very likely what corrupted `assets/data/tang_poetry_en.json`. Verified on the output: the added 李白 将进酒 arrives simplified (君不见, 黄河, 明镜, 白发, 尽欢, 钟鼓馔玉).
- **`[🧹 FIX]` The story library and the micro-reads shelf stopped listing 100 poems.** Media → Story library now shows one card per poet and *removes* the per-poem cards it replaces, so the same poem is not offered twice; tapping either a poet card or a legacy poem card opens the poet's book, which keeps progress and bookmarks on one id. Micro-reads previously excluded only `Tang Poetry` and `Classical Literature`, so every poem appeared there as well as in the poetry tier — it now excludes all poetry categories through the existing `isPoetryCategory`.
- **`[➕ ADD]` Every poet's collection now carries a real biography, condensed from the corpus and translated into all 14 locales.** `tooling/build_poet_bios.py` reads `全唐诗/authors.tang.json` and `authors.song.json` — the same MIT revision the poems came from — and **condenses** each classical 繁体 biography (median 382 characters, up to 1541) into one or two sentences of plain modern Chinese, at ≤110 characters. Condensing a supplied biography is faithful work; writing one from scratch would be the invention `docs/PROJECT_GUIDELINES.md` forbids. Those files are 繁体, so names and text go through the same pinned OpenCC map the poem fetch uses — **without it only 13 of the 37 poets match** (李白 matches either way, 苏轼 does not match 蘇軾). 29 poets have a corpus biography; the other 8 (five 元曲 writers plus 李煜, 曹操 and 纳兰性德) are absent from both author indexes, so they get a line built **only** from what the app already knows — their dynasty and collection size — and each entry records `origin` (`corpus` / `derived-from-store`) and `source`, so a derived line is never passed off as a biography. Output: `assets/data/poet_bios.json` plus `assets/data/l10n/poet_bios_<locale>.json` for the 13 content locales **and** `en` (English is the app's base locale, not one of the 13, so the loader skips it and the repository reads it separately for `descriptionEn`). The poetry shelf and the reader now show the poet's history instead of a list of poem titles.
- **`[📚 CONTENT]` Every collection now has a real biography, not a placeholder — and two bugs behind that were worth the hunt.** The first pass left 8 poets with a data-derived line ("元代作品。本集收录 50 首。") because `全唐诗/authors.tang.json` and `authors.song.json` do not mention them. Three more real, already-licensed sources closed 3 of them: **the single-poet READMEs** (`纳兰性德/README.md` and `曹操诗集/README.md` each carry a proper biography — the Tang index has an author file, but a single-poet anthology keeps its biography in the README) and **`宋词/author.song.json`**, the 词 side's own index (1563 entries, field `description` rather than `desc`), which covers 李煜. The last 4 (马致远, 张可久, 张养浩, 徐再思) come from **Wikidata descriptions, which are CC0** — chosen deliberately over a Wikipedia extract, which would carry an attribution *and* share-alike obligation into a commercial app. Only 宋方壶 has no source anywhere, and keeps the data-derived line. Every entry records `origin` — **31 `source-text`, 4 `wikidata-cc0`, 1 `collective`, 1 `derived-from-store`** — so provenance is never implied. **`佚名` needed a correction of its own:** it means *anonymous*, and that collection gathers anonymous poems from **several** anthologies, but it had picked up a single author-index entry's biography claiming 月泉吟社 collected all fifty (that society collected only some). Names that are not people are now excluded from the person lookup and described as what they are — "作者不详的古典诗歌集，选自《诗经》《全唐诗》《宋词》等选本。" — pinned by a test. All 37 were then re-translated into the 14 locales (`--force`, because the Chinese source had changed): **518 translations, 0 problems** — every locale complete, nothing left in Chinese.
- **`[🐞 FIX]` Two bugs found by checking the output rather than trusting it.** (1) **The batch loop condensed all 12,215 authors in the indexes, not the 37 we publish** — `sorted(sources)` iterated the whole index, and each stray poet would have been an API call and an entry the catalogue does not show. Caught in the log, killed mid-run, and the bios file restored from a backup taken beforehand. (2) **曹操's biography was 纳兰性德's.** The first suspect was batch misalignment: the reply is a positional JSON array, so a shifted slot passes a count-only parse. I hardened it to a **JSON object keyed by poet name**, added a `mentions_other_poet` guard that re-asks individually when a summary names a different poet — and the misalignment *still* reproduced, which is what proved the real cause: `readme_bios` cached each README by `os.path.basename`, and **both files are called `README.md`**, so 曹操 read 纳兰性德's cached file. Fixed by caching under the full path. Worth recording because the symptom looked exactly like a model failure and was not one — the keyed-object parse and the guard are kept anyway, since generation (unlike translation) has no ground truth to check against.
- **`[🌍 LOCALIZED]` The 572 poems that arrived with the expansion are getting translated titles.** `tooling/translate_poetry_chapters.py` fills `assets/data/l10n/poetry_<locale>.json`, which still only carried the curated 100; it only ever sends a missing or blank title, so the hand-checked translations of the curated set are never overwritten, and it saves after every batch of 50 so the run is resumable. **Progress at hand-off: `ar`, `de`, `es` and `it` complete at 672/672; `ja` 600, `fr` and `id` 500, `hi` 200, and `ko`/`pt`/`ru`/`th`/`vi` still at the original 100.** A chapter with no translated title falls back to its Chinese one, so the remaining locales are a gap rather than a breakage — re-run `python tooling/translate_poetry_chapters.py` to continue. **✅ The sweep is now complete: a later run filled the remaining 3,748 titles, so all 13 content locales hold 672/672 and the ratchet is pinned to the store's own id set rather than to 100.**
- **`[🔧 FIX]` English readers were getting *empty* chapter titles — the base locale was the one locale nothing covered.** The question *"is all of this translated in every language?"* had an uncomfortable answer: the biographies were complete in all 14 locales and the 13 content locales were now complete, but **English is the app's base locale rather than one of the 13**, so it has no `l10n/poetry_en.json` and is not meant to — a chapter's English title comes from the store's own `title_en`. The 572 fetched poems shipped that field as `title_en: ""`, which is worse than a missing key: `book_repository.dart` builds `titleEn: poem['title_en'] ?? 'Poem'`, and `""` is not null, so the `??` fallback **never fires** and `localizedTitle('en')` resolves to an empty string.
