Every one of those poems therefore rendered a **blank** chapter title in the table of contents and the reader. `tooling/build_poem_titles_en.py` (new) fills them: it reuses the 5 curated English titles the anthology re-supplied under a 乐府 form label ("鼓吹曲辞 将进酒" → "Bring in the Wine", matched by stripping the form prefix) rather than re-inventing them, translates the other 567 in batches of 40, and validates each answer — non-blank, no Han characters, not identical to the Chinese, ≤60 characters — so an unconverted answer is rejected instead of saved. **The validator earned its place immediately:** it caught the model answering `Remembering Qin娥` for three 纳兰性德 词 titles (it romanised half the tune name and kept 娥), and a stricter single-title retry pass then fixed all three. Titles are translated, never authored: `Pure Peace Melody II`, `Hard Journey III`, `Midnight Song: Autumn`, `To Meng Haoran`, `Farewell at Xie Tiao Tower`. Store written back with BOM, CRLF (19,568 lines) and key order byte-identical — a diff of all 672 entries reports **`title_en` as the only changed field** — and `legacyLinks` 50/50 preserved.
- **`[🧪 TEST]` The ratchets became invariants.** `test/features/reading/content_title_localization_test.dart` used to permit a partial sweep (`greaterThanOrEqualTo(100)`, commented *"the fetched poems do not yet"*); it now asserts every poem is titled in **every** content locale, that the l10n key set **equals** the store's id set, and — as a new test — that every poem has a non-blank English title **with no Chinese characters left in it**, which is the exact defect above made impossible to reintroduce. `test/features/reading` **119/119**; `flutter analyze` clean.
- **`[📝 NOTE]` One poetry field is still untranslated, and it is not displayed.** The per-poem `summary`/`summary_en` is empty for the same 572 poems (and the l10n `summary` sits at 100/672 in all 13 locales). It is left alone deliberately: the collection UI does not read it — a `BookChapter` carries no summary and a book's description is the **poet's biography**, which is complete — its only real consumers are the legacy per-poem story path and the offline EN dictionary seeded in `local_translation_service.dart`. Unlike a title, a poem summary cannot be *translated* from a source that does not exist: the curated 100 summarise a hand-written `themes` list (`["moon", "homesickness"]`) and the fetched 572 arrived with `themes: []`, `form: ""` and `hskLevel: 0`, so filling them means interpreting each poem and choosing its themes — authoring, not translating, and a decision to take deliberately rather than as a side effect of a localization sweep.
- **`[🌍 LOCALIZED]` ✅ The 100-book translation sweep is complete, and a second bug was only visible by measuring the output.** All 13 content locales now hold **4,237/4,237** chapter titles and all 14 hold **100/100** poet biographies, with the base locale's 4,237 English titles complete. The remaining locales were run **in parallel, one process per locale** (`--only <code>`) rather than serially — each writes only its own file, so there is no shared-write hazard and an hour of wall clock became minutes. **Then the check that mattered:** completeness is not correctness. Measuring every title for Han characters found **130 titles in 12 locales that were never translated** — proper nouns and 词 tune names the model had quietly left alone (`谢朓楼`, `蓬莱`, `桃花源`, `宜春`) plus **three Thai titles that were entirely Chinese** (`念奴娇` and nothing else). Every existing assertion passed on all of them, because each was non-blank and in the right file. New `--repair` mode re-translates exactly those with the requirement stated explicitly and a hard no-Han check (Japanese excluded — kanji *is* Japanese), two passes took it to **0 problems across 4,237 × 13**, and the whole thing is now pinned by a `no translated poem title is left in Chinese` test.
- **`[🔧 FIX]` A bookmark saved today did not reach the poet's shelf until the next launch (`book_repository.dart`).** Widening `book_repository_bookmark_migration_test.dart` to the collection era exposed it: `init()` migrated a legacy `tang_poetry_静夜思` bookmark all the way to 李白's collection, but `saveBookmark` only canonicalized to the **bare poem id** — `_canonicalBookId` called `canonicalPoetryId` and stopped there. So a bookmark written in the current session was stored under the poem, `getBookmarks(<collection>)` could not see it, and the shelf only caught up on the next start, when the migration ran. `_canonicalBookId` now resolves title-as-id → poem id → owning collection, which is the same destination the migration rewrites to, so writes, reads, the shelf (`getAllBookmarksAcrossBooks` was grouping by poem too) and the migration all agree. Test updated to the contract the app actually has, via `poetryAuthorBookId('李白')` rather than a hard-coded hex.
- **`[⚖️ LICENCE]` The bundled dictionary now carries the attribution its licence requires — and its share-alike question is now tracked instead of unnoticed.** `assets/data/dictionary.db` (186 MB, 125,009 entries) is derived from **CC-CEDICT**, which is **CC BY-SA 4.0**: commercial use is allowed, but attribution is *required* and modifications must be shared under the same licence. The app shipped it with the string "CEDICT" appearing **only in internal code comments** — no licence file, no in-app credit, no notices screen (the app had no licences screen at all), and nothing inside the file itself saying where it came from. Verified against both official sources: the CC-CEDICT wiki states CC BY-SA, and the MDBG export `tooling/build_dictionary.dart` downloads states **4.0 International** and spells out the two obligations ("mention where you got the data from" and "share these changes under the same license"). The attribution is now placed in **three** places so it travels with the data: `third_party/cc-cedict-LICENSE` (new, alongside `chinese-poetry-LICENSE`), seven `dictionary_metadata` rows inside the database itself (`cc_cedict_attribution`, `license`, `license_url`, `source_url`, `source_export`, `is_modified`, `modifications`), and the app's licences screen — `lib/core/legal/third_party_notices.dart` registers the notice through `LicenseRegistry`, and Settings gained a tile that opens Flutter's `showLicensePage`. That route needs **no new ARB key**, because the page's own chrome is localized by `GlobalMaterialLocalizations` in all 14 locales, so the credit is readable in every language the app speaks. `test/core/legal/third_party_notices_test.dart` (new, 3 tests) pins it: the notice is registered and contains the source URL, the licence file states the licence and lists the modifications, and the **shipped 186 MB database itself** is scanned in 4 MB chunks for the attribution string. **The share-alike half is deliberately left open** — see ISSUES.md — because it is a decision, not a code change: the file is unquestionably derived (13 translated `definition_*` columns and a 1,369,632-row quality table keyed to a hash of the English source definition), and the way this project handled the same obligation elsewhere was to *avoid* it (Wikidata **CC0** was chosen over a CC BY-SA Wikipedia extract for exactly this reason), so choosing it now should be deliberate.
- **`[❌ CORRECTION]` The bullet above is wrong on its central fact: the dictionary is NOT CC-CEDICT-only — it is a merge of many dictionaries, and two of them are not known to be redistributable.** The claim "there is exactly one upstream source" came from reading the two build scripts, which genuinely do download only the MDBG CC-CEDICT export — but it missed the ingestion work recorded in this very file. Builds **#296–#298** document ingesting **CFDICT** (French, 73,557), **HanDeDict** (German, 99,124), **WordNet-JA**, **OpenWN-PT**, **Hán-Việt DB**, **ItalWordNet**, **Bahasa WordNet**, **WordNet MCR**, **Arabic WordNet**, **BKRS/Liudmila HSK**, and then the **"Multilingual Pleco Database" (286,898 words)** and **"Big BKRS" (300,000 words)**. The shipped database confirms it: its per-language counts match the #298 record exactly (`definition_ru` 124,268, `definition_fr` 102,057, `definition_vi` 99,084, `definition_ja` 97,256, `definition_es` 96,976, `definition_ko` 96,777). **The severity ordering changes with it.** CC-CEDICT's share-alike is a copyleft question — using it is *permitted*. **Pleco is a commercial paid product** (which also licenses Oxford/ABC titles) and **BKRS is a scraped dictionary website**: those are "you may not distribute this at all" problems, and **no licence notice fixes them**. Crediting CC-CEDICT alone was therefore insufficient on two counts — it omitted the other open sources' attribution *and* it framed the file as a derivative of one dictionary when it is a merge. Fixed in the same change: `third_party/dictionary-sources.md` (new inventory, Pleco/BKRS flagged **unverified**), the in-app notice rewritten to name the real sources and say **merged**, four new `dictionary_metadata` rows, and the legal test now requires every source to be listed and the unverified two to stay flagged. **The lesson worth keeping:** the build scripts are not the provenance record — the changelog was, and reading only the code produced a confident wrong answer.
- **`[📝 NOTE]` Three suites were already red before this work and are still red, for reasons outside it.** `poetry_cover_manifest_test` now gets past its stale `expect(poems, hasLength(100))` — the store stopped being 100 entries the moment it became one collection per poet, so the assertion became "every cover points at a poem that exists" — and then fails on a genuine content defect it was always right to flag: **two of the 100 covers are perceptually near-identical** (dHash distance 5 where the guard wants more than 5). `cultural_context_book_parity_test` (3) and `simplified_reader_prose_test` (1) are another agent's files; `stroke_matcher_test` (2) and `tome_manager_shelf_test` (1) are untouched by this work. **Full suite: 1,055 passing / 9 failing, and the poetry area is green.**

- **`[➕ ADD]` The shelf went from 37 books to **100**, and from 672 poems to **4,237**.** Asking *"why are they only 37 books, I want 100 books"* was the right question, because 37 was never a target — it was simply how many poets the curated 100 poems happened to contain, and `merge()` refused to publish anyone else (`if author not in existing_counts: skipped`). The measurement that decided the design: the eight anthology files hold **741 poems spread over 170 poets**, so a hundred books taken from them alone would end with poets holding a *single* poem each — the exact complaint the collection model exists to fix. Two things were therefore needed. (1) **`tooling/select_poetry_poets.py` (new)** picks the hundred, and its rule is reviewable: keep every poet already published; rank the rest by presence in `唐诗三百首`, `宋词三百首`, `花间集` and 南唐词 (those anthologies *are* the fame signal, so no hand-made list of favourites is needed); drop names that are not people (`张` is a truncation, `不详`/无名氏 mean *unknown*, and a 元曲 "author" like `高明《蔡伯喈琵琶记》` is a play) — 11 rejected; and require at least 12 poems in the full corpora — 180 rejected as too thin, which is what stops the 100th book being another single-poem stub. (2) **`fetch_poetry_collections.py` gained `--deep` and `--publish-file`**: the full `全唐诗/poet.tang.*.json` (58 files, 56,468 usable poems) and `宋词/ci.song.*.json` (22 files, 20,590) now top each poet up to the 50-poem cap, cached on disk because they are 34 MB. The 繁体 split repeats itself and is probed rather than assumed — `poet.tang` is 繁体 (2,783 traditional to 37 simplified characters in one file), `ci.song` is 简体 (0 to 4,433) — so the same pinned OpenCC map applies to the Tang side only, and the output carries **zero** traditional characters. **Result: 100 collections, median 50 poems each (max 50), only one under five poems** (岳飞, 3), curated order and the 50 `legacyLinks` untouched, BOM + CRLF (119,388 lines) intact, 60,693 records skipped because their poet is not published.
- **`[🔧 FIX]` Two silent failures the expansion exposed, both fixed before they could reach a reader.** (1) **A truncated translation reply cost a whole locale.** Arabic returned 50 titles as an array cut off mid-string; `parse_array` rejected it, and the sweep's `break` abandoned **all 3,565** Arabic titles for the run. Fixed by batching 25 instead of 50 and, more importantly, by **salvaging** the complete leading strings of a truncated array and resuming from a cursor rather than a fixed offset — a cut-off reply now loses nothing, since only the answers actually received are consumed. The same hardening went into `build_poem_titles_en.py`. (2) **Poets with no Latin name read as Chinese in 13 locales.** `poetryCollectionToBook` sets `titleEn`/`authorEn` from the first poem's `author_en` and falls back to the **Chinese** name, so the 63 poets added to reach 100 would have shown 王维 to a French reader instead of "Wang Wei" — silently, because nothing is blank and nothing throws. New `tooling/build_poet_names_en.py` fills it: 63 names, validated as a Latin proper name with no Han left in it (韦应物 → "Wei Yingwu", 韩愈 → "Han Yu", 高适 → "Gao Shi"), written across 2,660 poems. `build_poet_bios.py` also learned to **keep biographies that already exist** (with `--force` for when the Chinese genuinely changes) — without it, regenerating would have returned different words for the 37 reviewed biographies and silently invalidated their 298 translations.
- **`[🌍 LOCALIZED]` In flight at hand-off: the 100-book content sweeps.** Biographies for the new poets are built (**94 from corpus source text, 4 Wikidata CC0, 1 derived**, the 37 existing ones preserved) and their **14-locale** translation is running (63 × 14 = 882 strings). Chapter titles are being filled for the new poems across the 13 content locales (~36,000 strings) and English titles for the base locale (3,135) — all three are resumable and idempotent, so **re-running the same command continues where each stopped**: `python tooling/build_poet_bios.py --translate`, `python tooling/translate_poetry_chapters.py`, `python tooling/build_poem_titles_en.py`. Until they finish, `content_title_localization_test` fails exactly where a reader would notice: 3 assertions that every poem carries a title in every locale and a non-blank English title. **Note for review:** the store is now **5.0 MB**, loaded and parsed on the poetry path at startup, which is the one thing about this expansion worth a second look on a low-end device.


- **`[🔑 KEYS]` Bulk translation had to move off the app's own Gemini key.** `gemini-3.6-flash` — the model the app uses — is **quota-exhausted** (`HTTP 429`), and `gemini-flash-latest` rate-limits this kind of bulk job; worse, the 429 path in the retry loop was **silent** and surfaced as an "empty reply", which quietly cost whole batches with no explanation. The tool now logs every backoff, and routes through **OpenRouter** (`google/gemini-2.5-flash`, ~1 s per call) when `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` is present, falling back to Gemini. A batched JSON-array prompt also returned empty on `gemini-flash-latest` while the single-item prompt worked, so the bio build falls back to one call per poet rather than losing a batch.


### [2026-09-26] Eliminate Segment Tab RenderFlex Overflow Across Locales in AI Hub and Explore Screens
- **`[🔧 FIX]` RenderFlex Overflow in Segmented Tabs (`lib/features/chat/presentation/screens/ai_hub_screen.dart`, `lib/features/explore/presentation/screens/explore_screen.dart`)**:
  - Addressed user-reported `A RenderFlex overflowed by 34 pixels on the right` bug on Spanish ("Shadowing (Imitación auditiva)") and long-locale tab labels in the segmented control.
  - Wrapped segment tab labels in `Flexible(child: FittedBox(fit: BoxFit.scaleDown, child: Text(..., maxLines: 1, overflow: TextOverflow.ellipsis)))` with `mainAxisSize: MainAxisSize.min` and horizontal padding `6dp` to guarantee zero RenderFlex overflow across narrow viewports (down to 320dp) and aggressive accessibility text scaling (up to 1.5x/2.0x).
- **`[🌐 I18N]` Clean Verbose ARB Segment Tab Strings**:
  - Replaced verbose parenthetical strings with concise standard action nouns across supported locales: Spanish (`es`: `"shadowing": "Shadowing"`), Russian (`ru`: `"shadowing": "Shadowing"`), Arabic (`ar`: `"shadowing": "المحاكاة الصوتية"`), Thai (`th`: `"shadowing": "ฝึกพูดตาม"`), Italian (`it`: `"roleplay": "Gioco di ruolo"`), Portuguese (`pt`: `"roleplay": "Roleplay"`), and French (`fr`: `"shadowing": "Shadowing"`). Regenerated localizations via `flutter gen-l10n`.
- **`[🧪 TEST]` New Tab Layout Test Suite (`test/features/chat/ai_hub_tab_layout_test.dart`)**:
  - Added 16 contract tests tagged `@Tags(['locale-sweep'])` verifying the segment tab layout never overflows at 320dp width and under 1.5x accessibility text scale across all critical expansion locales (`es`, `en`, `fr`, `ru`, `ar`, `th`, `pt`, `it`).


### [2026-09-26] Eliminate Startup Race Condition On Firebase App Initialization & Subscription Gate Dead-End
- **`[🔧 FIX]` Crash on startup `[core/no-app] No Firebase App '[DEFAULT]' has been created`**: `_initializeServices` in `lib/main.dart` previously instantiated `AudioService` (via Riverpod container) before invoking `Firebase.initializeApp(...)`. `AudioService` instantiated `SpeechCacheService`, which eagerly queried `FirebaseFunctions.instance` and `FirebaseAuth.instance` in its constructor, throwing `[core/no-app]` and kicking the app to the `StartupErrorScreen`.
- **`[🛡️ RESILIENCE]` Lazy Firebase Service Access**: Refactored `SpeechCacheService` (`lib/core/services/speech_cache_service.dart`) and `DictionaryExpansionService` (`lib/features/flashcards/data/services/dictionary_expansion_service.dart`) to lazily resolve `FirebaseFunctions.instance` and `FirebaseAuth.instance` via getters wrapped in safe try/catch blocks. Services can now be safely instantiated in unit tests or offline environments without requiring Firebase to be pre-initialized.
- **`[🚀 STARTUP]` Hoisted Firebase Initialization**: Moved `Firebase.initializeApp(options: DefaultFirebaseOptions.currentPlatform)` and `FirebaseAppCheck.instance.activate(...)` to Step 0 of `_initializeServices` in `lib/main.dart`, guaranteeing that any subsequent provider or service instantiation finds Firebase properly initialized.
- **`[🔓 UX/DX]` Unblock Subscription Gate Dead-End**: `_SubscriptionGate` in `lib/main.dart` previously trapped users in an inescapable error screen ("We could not check your subscription. Please try again.") whenever RevenueCat timed out or errored (standard on local simulators/devices without StoreKit sandbox). Updated `_SubscriptionGate` to gracefully fall back to `CustomPaywallScreen` on check error/timeout without trapping the user. Added a `[Dev Bypass]` button visible exclusively in `kDebugMode` on `CustomPaywallScreen` allowing 1-tap persistent unlock of developer mode (`demo_account_unlocked`) for rapid development and testing.
- **`[🧪 TEST]` Verified Total Hygiene**: Confirmed `dart analyze lib` reports 0 issues. Verified `flutter test test/startup/bootstrap_app_test.dart` and `test/features/premium/custom_paywall_screen_test.dart` pass (14/14).

### [2026-09-26] Reading Mode Wears The Book-Screen Vocabulary, And The Grade Legend Stops Rendering As Tofu
- **`[🔧 FIX]` The "Swipe to Grade" legend was four empty boxes.** `reading_mode.dart` built it as a single hardcoded `Text`: `'⬅️ ${again} ➡️ ${good} ⬆️ ${easy} ⬇️ ${hard}'`. Those arrows are emoji (U+2B05 / U+27A1 / U+2B06 / U+2B07) and rendered as the four blue tofu boxes in the reported screenshot wherever the emoji font is missing. Being one unbreakable string, the names also could not wrap, could not be translated independently of the arrows, and could not break at 320dp.
- **`[➕ ADD]` `lib/shared/widgets/swipe_to_grade_hint.dart`**: four chips, each taking its colour from the Hanko seal `SwipeableFlashcard` actually stamps for that direction (`HankoSealData.again/good/easy/hard`, so the legend cannot drift from the gesture it explains), a Material direction icon, the localized grade name, and the book screen's chip geometry (a 0.08 wash inside a 0.35 hairline, radius 12). A `Wrap` — never a `Row` — breaks the line at 320dp, and each label is `Flexible` so a long name like "Schwierig" wraps inside its own chip.
- **`[🎨 UI/UX]` Reading Mode now sits on the app palette.** The file had **zero** `AppTheme` references: the card was `Colors.white` / `Colors.black12`, its text `black87` / `black45` / `black54`, the reveal prompt `Colors.grey`, so none of it followed the other brightness. It now uses `AppTheme.surfaceOf` for the scaffold (so the transparent app bar and the body are one sheet of Xuan paper), `AppTheme.cardBgOf`, `AppTheme.carbonInkLight/Dark`, the same card border and shadow geometry the account and book screens use, and the Emperor's Gold `#D4AF37` hairline for the rule between the character and its reading. The card keeps radius 32 deliberately: `ZenFlipCard` paints its paper-shimmer highlight at 32, so any other corner would show the highlight's corners through the card mid-flip.
- **`[🔧 FIX]` Three real layout defects, found by the new sweep test rather than by eye.** (1) The answer block (pinyin + definition) overflowed the card by **135px** on a 320x568 screen — pre-existing, because a fixed-size block sat inside `Expanded(flex: 2)`; it now wraps at the card's width inside a `LayoutBuilder` + `FittedBox(scaleDown)` so it scales to its share instead of overflowing. (2) A long German grade name overflowed its chip by **20px** at 2x text. (3) The legend grew to **373** of the 464 available pixels at 2x text and squeezed the character itself into **59px**; the legend is a gesture hint rather than content, so its text scaling is clamped to 1.3 while the card keeps the learner's full scale.
- **`[🧪 TEST]` `test/features/flashcards/reading_mode_book_parity_test.dart`** (10 tests, tagged `locale-sweep` so the blocking CI gate runs it): the scaffold surface, the card fill asserted *per brightness* (the dark token can only pass if the palette is genuinely consulted), the four chips present only once the card is revealed, no emoji arrow in any label, the 320x568 x 2x-text matrix in de/ru/th, plus source ratchets for the forbidden Material vocabulary and a check that every chip colour comes from a `HankoSealData`.

### [2026-09-26] iPad Is No Longer A Stretched Phone: Phase 0 + Phase 1 Landed
- **`[🚀 FEATURE]` The app can multitask on iPad.** `UIRequiresFullScreen` was `true` in `ios/Runner/Info.plist`, which blocks Split View, Slide Over and Stage Manager outright; it is now `false`. Split View is the most-used iPad study pattern ("reader + notes"), and it is now possible.
- **`[🔧 FIX]` The global portrait lock is gone.** `lib/main.dart` forced portrait on every device, so a 12.9" iPad was a phone-shaped column even though the plist declared all four iPad orientations. Phones still lock portrait; tablets rotate. Two features that re-locked portrait on their own (`universal_scanner_screen`, `smart_media_desk_screen`) are now gated on the device class and hand orientation back in `dispose` instead of leaving the lock on for the session.
- **`[➕ ADD]` The adaptive layout kit** (`lib/core/layout/`): `ZenWindow`/`ZenBreakpoints` (Material-3 window classes: compact <600, medium 600-839, expanded 840-1199, large 1200-1599, extraLarge ≥1600), `ZenContentPane` (per-surface measure caps: reading 680, article 720, form 560, dialog 640, onboarding 480), `ZenGrid` (density-driven grid delegates; `mainAxisExtent` also removes `childAspectRatio`'s vertical clipping), `ZenNavigationRail`, `ZenTwoPaneScaffold` (degrades to one pane when the detail would fall below 320dp) and `ZenDevice`/`ZenOrientationLock`.
- **`[➕ ADD]` The shell is adaptive**: `main_navigation_screen` renders a `NavigationRail` beside the content at ≥600dp and keeps the phone's bottom bar byte-identical below it (still asserted by `now_playing_bar_test`).
- **`[➕ ADD]` One overlay entry point** (`lib/shared/widgets/zen_overlay.dart`): `zenSheet` stays a bottom sheet on phones and becomes a width-capped dialog (≤640dp, ≤85 % of window height) on tablets; `zenPicker` uses a 420dp chooser; `zenDialog`/`ZenOverlayFrame` are the shared frame. The 42 existing call sites can migrate one line at a time.
- **`[🚦 GUARD]` `test/core/adaptive_layout_guard_test.dart`** — four ratchets, now at their floor: `crossAxisCount` **10 → 0**, raw `MediaQuery.of(context).size` **29 → 0**, direct `showModalBottomSheet` **42 → 0** outside an allow-list (zen_overlay itself, the motion-tokenised `GlobalBlurredBottomSheet`, and the pointer-anchored `quick_look_sheet`), plus "every orientation lock must be device-class aware". Coverage tests keep the iPad/landscape viewports and the documented breakpoints honest, and `test/core/adaptive_layout_test.dart` pins the phone-vs-tablet contract of every primitive (14 tests).
- **`[➕ ADD]` The mechanical adoption ran as scripts, not hand edits** (158 files were dirty from other work in flight): `scratch/ipad_adoption.py` converted **29** size reads and **39** sheet calls in 23 files (a paren/quote/comment-aware parser, skips anything it cannot express verbatim and reports it), and `scratch/ipad_grids.py` converted **10** fixed-column grids keeping their original proportions. `dart analyze lib` is clean and the compiler is the safety net.
- **`[🐛 FIX]` `zenSheet` now accepts everything the existing calls pass** (`useSafeArea`, `enableDrag`, `showDragHandle`, `useRootNavigator`, `constraints`) so the conversion was a rename rather than a rewrite; the sheet→dialog switch moved to *expanded* (≥840dp) instead of medium, because 600-839dp is a large phone or a Split View half where the sheet is still the platform idiom.
- **`[🐛 FIX]` Two things the bulk edit disturbed, repaired:** the whitespace reformat turned a one-line `if` in `conversation_screen.dart` into a lint violation (now braced), and `deck_selection_sheet_design_test.dart` asserted the old `MediaQuery.of(context).size` source string (updated to `sizeOf`).
- **`[🐛 FIX]` The locale sweep was never testing its viewports.** `tester.binding.setSurfaceSize()` no longer reaches `MediaQuery` on Flutter 3.38 — a probe that sets 390x844 still reports `Size(800, 600)` — so every previous "sweep green" only proved the 800x600 default. The harness now sets `tester.view.physicalSize`/`devicePixelRatio` (with reset teardowns), which makes 320dp and 2× text scale real. The `locale-sweep` suite passes on the honest matrix (**55 tests**).
- **`[🧪 TEST]` New `ipad-sweep` tag** (`test/core/ipad_layout_sweep_test.dart`): the adaptive kit across `kIpadViewports` + landscape phones × de/ru/th × 1×/2×. It immediately caught a real bug — the rail overflowed by 44px in a 320dp-tall window at 2× text scale — fixed with `NavigationRail(scrollable: true, trailingAtBottom: true)`.
- **`[🚧 PHASE 2]` The first iPad screen treatment landed: `deck_detail_screen`.** At ≥840dp the deck's numbers (total / due / new / learning + the daily goals) move into an **insight rail beside the card list** instead of a header you scroll past. The wide branch is additive — `if (!context.zenWindow.isExpanded) return deckBody;` — so the iPhone layout is byte-identical, and a 700dp Split View slice also keeps the phone layout (asserted). Verified by `test/features/flashcards/deck_detail_ipad_layout_test.dart` (tagged `ipad-sweep`): rail present at 1024×1366, absent at 390×844 **and** at 700×1000.
- **`[🔍 FINDING]` The plan's "six two-pane screens" were based on a wrong assumption, now corrected in writing.** Only *some* of those screens push a detail; `book_catalog` and `scenario_selection` have **no `Navigator.push` at all** (covers use a hero route, scenario details open as sheets), and `dictionary_screen` only pushes *other features* — its word details are inline cards. So the iPad treatment differs per screen (width shaping, a rail, a preview pane, or — for `character_detail` — replacing the `PageView` with columns), and `scenario_selection` was already fixed by the `zenSheet` conversion at extended width. The corrected per-screen table is in `docs/IPAD_ADAPTIVE_PLAN.md` (Phase 2), together with the additive-branch recipe and the test pattern to copy.
- **`[🚧 PHASE 2]` Three more screens adopted, and the adoption is now enforced by a ledger.** `settings_screen` gets an unconditional `ZenContentPane(maxWidth: 760, padding: zero)` (a no-op on any phone, a centred form on an iPad); `dictionary_screen`'s hub is capped at 1100 so its shelf rows stop stretching across a 1366dp screen; `book_catalog_screen` gains a **real preview pane** — at ≥840dp a cover tap sets a selection instead of pushing, and the pane hosts the *same* `BookDetailScreen` in a new `embedded: true` mode that renders **no back button** (that button would have popped the catalogue itself). `test/core/adaptive_layout_guard_test.dart` now carries an **iPad adoption ledger** (5 screens, each pinning its gate + wide marker) plus a rule that no adopted screen may branch on a raw width comparison — the guard suite is 14 tests. Phones are untouched in all three: the caps are no-ops below 760/1100dp and the catalogue keeps `Navigator.push` below 840dp. Guarded by 48 passing tests across `book_catalog_localization`, `book_detail_localization`, `bundled_book_detail_localization`, `dictionary_result_ui_regression`, `dictionary_expansion_panel` and `notification_settings_localization`.
- **`[🚦 CI]` The iPad/Pencil work is now enforced by CI, not just by local runs.** Both `.gitlab-ci.yml` (`flutter_test_ipad`) and `.github/workflows/locale_guard.yml` (`ipad-guard`) gained the counterpart of the locale gate: the adaptive ratchets + adoption ledger and the primitive contracts first, then every suite tagged `ipad-sweep` (the adaptive kit across `kIpadViewports`, the deck screen's wide branch, and the Apple-Pencil input tests) — same tag-derived suite list pattern as `locale-sweep`, so tagging a suite is all it takes to bring it into the gate. Verified by running exactly the job's commands locally: **30/30** ratchets + contracts, **10/10** sweep.
- **`[🚧 PHASE 2b — PENCIL]` Step 1 landed: the writing canvas is Apple-Pencil aware, and phones are bit-for-bit unchanged.** `DrawingCanvas` gained `stylusInput`, `palmRejection` and `hoverPreview` (all default on, all inert without a stylus): **pressure tapers the live stroke** (`_drawTaperedStroke` — 0.5 pressure is exactly the previous finger width, so the brush only grows/thins around it), a **palm landing while the Pencil is down never draws**, **Pencil hover** shows a landing ring *and* peeks at the guide stroke even when the streak setting has hidden it, and `drawing_step`'s writing surface grows from 300dp to 460dp on an iPad. Implementation note: raw `Listener` + `MouseRegion` observe pressure and pointer *kind* (drag details don't expose pressure), while the `GestureDetector` still owns the gesture arena — so scrolling parents and the smooth-quadratic touch rendering are untouched. Verified by `test/features/flashcards/drawing_canvas_stylus_test.dart` (synthesised `PointerDeviceKind.stylus` and palm events, 4/4) and pinned by two new adoption-ledger rows; `test/core` plus the canvas's consumers are 151/151.
- **`[🚧 PHASE 2b — PENCIL]` Steps 2 + 3 landed: the writing bench is a screen.** `writing_bench_screen.dart` puts the capped (560dp) writing surface beside a persistent context panel — the character with its stroke-order animation, pinyin, definition, session progress, the per-stroke verdicts as bars using the canvas's own heatmap thresholds, and a completion badge. It is a **session** (Previous / Next / Clear / Skip stroke, plus **Replay** of your own attempt through the canvas's existing `readOnly` + `initialUserStrokes`), and the same screen collapses to one column on a phone. Reached from the iPad deck rail's new **Practice Writing** button. Tested by `test/features/flashcards/writing_bench_test.dart` (4/4) + a ledger row; `test/core` + `test/features/flashcards` are **283/283**, and the CI-gated `ipad-sweep` sweep is 4 suites / 14 tests. Test-infrastructure detail worth keeping: the panel's looping stroke-order animation schedules an uncancellable 2s `Future.delayed`, so the test disposes the tree and then *fires* that orphaned timer (its callback checks `mounted`) — that is what makes `!timersPending` pass.
- **`[📖🎧]` Listen-and-read: the audiobook and the book, side by side (the iPad study desk).** `listen_and_read_screen.dart` puts the transport (400dp) beside the matching text and makes both hosts **embedded panes** — the player already had no `AppBar`, and the reader gained `embedded`, which drops its `AppBar` *and* its "Resumed: Ch.x" toast (a chapter-following remount would otherwise announce one every chapter). Two rules make it work as a study device: **the audio is the clock** (a location event that changes the chapter re-keys the reader pane onto that chapter; the spoken *sentence* moves the text only on a chapter change or on an explicit "follow the audio" press, because re-keying per sentence would reset the reader's scroll) and **nothing stops the audiobook** — the phone path `stop()`s playback and `pushReplacement`s the reader, so the desk is the `isExpanded` branch of that same `_switchToReadingMode` button and the phone path stays byte-for-byte intact below it. The player gained exactly two host seams (`onLocationChanged`, `onOpenTextPane`) and the transport's read button became "put the text where the audio is". Zero new ARB strings (the existing `chapterXOfY` plus the book's own title). Asserted by `test/features/reading/listen_and_read_desk_test.dart` (7 tests, tagged `ipad-sweep` — including one that pins the *source order* of the phone branch vs the iPad branch) and 3 rows in the adoption ledger. One repair was needed: `book_reader_toolbar_density_test.dart` anchored on the literal `appBar: AppBar(`, which the conditional changed — it now matches the argument via regex, because `dart format` decides whether that ternary is one line or three.
- **`[📺 PHASE 3 · SLICE 1]` Video subtitles now scale with the viewing distance.** `premium_subtitles_overlay.dart` pinned 36 / 22 / 16pt at every window size; on a tablet that is a phone's subtitle size read from twice as far away. All three lines now ramp through `zenValue`: hanzi **36 → 40 → 44**, pinyin **22 → 24 → 26**, English **16 → 17 → 18** at compact → medium → expanded, with a phone unchanged. Unlike the heavy media screens, this widget takes plain data (no providers, no plugins), so the change is covered **behaviourally** rather than by source assertions: `test/features/media/premium_subtitles_typography_test.dart` pumps the real overlay at three window sizes and reads the resolved `TextStyle`s out of the tree, including a monotonicity assertion so the subtitle can never shrink as the window grows (3/3, tagged `ipad-sweep`, plus an adoption-ledger row). Its siblings from the same plan row — a user-facing size *control*, the transcript rail, and `mainAxisExtent` in place of fixed heights — are deliberately still open, and so are the phase's big restructures (two-page spread, scanner two-pane, media-desk split, browser tabs): each is a multi-edit change inside a 2,000+ line screen, and this slice was the piece that could be finished *and* verified rather than half-landed.
- **`[📺 PHASE 3 · SLICE 2]` The transcript line's reading text scales with the window too.** `premium_transcript_line.dart` — the phrase you actually read while watching, as opposed to the full-screen subtitle — pinned its hanzi at 22pt on every device; it now ramps through `zenValue` (22 → 24 → 26). Analyzer clean, guarded by an adoption-ledger row. Two honest gaps, recorded rather than papered over: the secondary pinyin/translation lines are still a fixed 14pt (the two remaining constants sit inside `const TextStyle`s whose exact whitespace defeated three anchor attempts, and stopping was better than churning the file), and the widget did not get its own behavioural test the way the subtitle overlay did. The media desk's **landscape split** (row #44's P0) is consequently still the top of the queue: the desk's `OrientationBuilder` body is one 185-line `Column(video, Expanded(content))`, so splitting it means extracting that body into two panes — one edit larger than the editor accepts, i.e. a chained 2-3 step edit over the region where two mishaps already cost repairs today.
- **`[🌐 PHASE 3 · SLICE 3]` The browser's extracted words become a pane, not a sheet.** Extracting words from a page used to cover the page with a modal review. At expanded the same review opens as a **380dp pane beside the live page**, so you can keep reading the article while choosing what to save. The sheet gained optional `onConfirm`/`onDismiss` — both default to null, so the modal path is byte-for-byte and **every phone is unchanged** — plus `_dismissExtracted` at its three exit points, because a pane owns its own dismissal and a stray `Navigator.pop` would have closed the browser. The add-to-deck step is now one shared `_addSelectedWordsToDeck`, so sheet and pane cannot drift. One existing guard was repaired rather than deleted: `deck_picker_dismissal_test` pinned the picker call, its branch and its `mounted` guard inside the loop, all of which moved into that helper — it now asserts the same invariants at their new home. Verified: analyzer clean; `test/features/media` + `test/features/reading` + the guard suite = **204 passing / 2 failing**, the two being the long-standing `cultural_context_book_parity` pair; an adoption-ledger row pins the pane.
- **`[📷 PHASE 3 · SLICE 4]` The scanner splits in landscape: live camera left, results right.** On an iPad in landscape the scanner used to look exactly as it does on a phone — a full-bleed camera preview with the OCR results painted over it. Now the preview keeps the left pane (`widthFactor: 0.45`) and the content column takes the right (`wideSplit ? 0.55 : 1.0`), so the results and the live frame are readable at the same time. The subtle part is *why* it works: the camera was extracted into `_buildCameraPreview()`, so the aspect-fit math inside it runs against the **pane s** constraints rather than the window s — otherwise the preview would be scaled for the full screen and clipped inside its half. The gate is `context.zenWindow.isExpanded` plus the **`1.0` fallback**, which leaves every phone and medium window byte-identical, and `wideSplit` deliberately **excludes** the interactive-image step: `ScannerOverlayPainter` still paints from `MediaQuery.sizeOf(context)`, so splitting there would draw the OCR boxes at window coordinates over a pane. Scoping that painter is the follow-up, and the guard test asserts the painter is still window-measured so the exclusion cannot quietly rot. Verified: analyzer clean; scanner guards **5/5** (new, tagged `ipad-sweep`) + the premium suite + the guard suite = **40/40**; adoption-ledger row added (and the file passes the ledger no-raw-width scan).
- **`[🎬 PHASE 3 · SLICE 5]` The media desk splits in landscape: the video keeps the left pane, the transcript sits beside it.** The desk used to stack the player above a scrolling transcript, so tapping a line to seek meant the video had scrolled away — and on a tablet in landscape most of the width sat empty. Now `split = context.zenWindow.isExpanded` puts the **video and its transport in the left pane** and the **AI prep + transcript in the right**, so a tap can seek a video that is still on screen. The phone layout is expressed exactly once (`if (!split)` returns the original `Column(videoPane, Expanded(contentPane), controlPane)`), and both the video and the content were extracted into `_buildVideoPane()` / `contentPane` — which the guard checks by **counting `YoutubePlayer(` occurrences and requiring exactly one**, because a duplicated player is the obvious way this kind of split rots. Verified: analyzer clean; media desk guards **4/4** (new, tagged `ipad-sweep`) + media suite + guard suite = **123 passing / 2 failing**, the two being the long-standing `cultural_context_book_parity` pair; adoption-ledger row added. **PiP is deliberately not included, and it is not layout work:** an iframe cannot enter picture-in-picture, so it needs native `AVPictureInPictureController` support plus a player that provides it — the honest blocker, recorded in the plan rather than promised.
- **`[📐 PHASE 2 COMPLETE]` The character reference: window-class gate + side-by-side sections at expanded.**
 The plan called this "the one screen where the structure itself must change"; the audit said otherwise, in a useful way. The screen **already had** a two-column wide layout (`_buildLandscapeLayout`: character card left, details right) and its `PageView` was **not** a character carousel — it pages a character's anatomy/radical components inside one section. So the landed work is the *correct* part: (a) the gate moved from `constraints.maxWidth > 600` to `context.zenWindow.isAtLeastMedium && constraints.maxWidth > ZenBreakpoints.compactMax`, so a 700dp **Split View slice can no longer be handed the iPad layout** because a raw pixel count crossed a magic number — that was the file's only raw-width comparison, which also lets it join the adoption ledger's scan; (b) at **expanded** the detail sections are arranged as **side-by-side columns** (`_buildDetailColumns`, two `Expanded` columns, even/odd split) and the pill bar is not built at all (its only job was jumping between stacked sections — a dead control above content already on screen), while phones and medium windows keep the pill-navigated stack byte-for-byte; (c) the anatomy `PageView` becomes a row of component cards at expanded — a swipe carousel on a wide screen hides content and fights two-finger trackpad scrolling, exactly the caveat the plan raises against `PageView`. Both arrangements are built from one `_detailSectionWidgets` list, so they cannot drift. Asserted by `test/features/flashcards/character_detail_ipad_layout_test.dart` (7 tests, tagged `ipad-sweep`) + an adoption-ledger row. Two guards needed repair (`common_words_tab_test`'s section-mounting anchor matched the *pill* guard after the refactor; `book_reader_toolbar_density_test` from the previous step) — both now match semantics, not formatting. Still open from that row: the Pencil practice pane and hover tooltips on hanzi.

- **`[🖱️ HOVER]` Hover-to-look-up in the shared hanzi text (the 19-file change).**
 `quick_look_sheet.dart` gained `showQuickLookPeek` + `QuickLookPeekHandle`: the **same body as the tap path, but in a non-modal overlay entry** that is deliberately `IgnorePointer`, so the page underneath stays fully interactive and a tap still opens the real sheet with its actions (no stacked modal routes, no barrier). `TappableHanziText` shows it on hover after a **320ms hover-intent** delay (a pointer crossing a sentence must not flash a card per character), keeps it while the hovered character does not change, and dismisses it after a **520ms grace period** on leave. The hit-test is exact: the `RenderParagraph` maps the pointer to a text offset, so the character *under the pointer* is the one peeked. Gated to ≥600dp and to pointer kinds (mouse / stylus / inverted stylus / trackpad), and opt-out per call site with `hoverPeek: false`. `TappableMarkdownHanziText` is deliberately excluded for now — its rendered text is pre-processed, so paragraph offsets would point at the wrong character (documented follow-up). Verified by `test/shared/tappable_hanzi_hover_test.dart` (2/2: peek appears after intent, dismisses after the grace period, and a phone never peeks) plus the two existing quick-look suites (14/14).
- **`[♿ A11Y]` The writing canvas is no longer invisible to screen readers.** `DrawingCanvas` gained `semanticsLabel`/`semanticsValue` and wraps itself in a `Semantics` node when either is set — the painters draw pixels, not text, so before this the entire writing area was silent to VoiceOver and Switch Control (an iPad is a common accessibility device). The bench announces `'Practice Writing: 难'` plus the live `'3 / 7 Strokes'`, and the panel's stroke-order animation is labelled with the existing `strokeOrderChip` string. Verified by a semantics test in `writing_bench_test.dart`. Still to do: the same two arguments from `calligraphy_canvas_sheet`, the scanner markup and the pitch contour.
- **`[⌨️ KEYBOARD]` A reusable hardware-keyboard layer, wired into the bench first.** `lib/core/layout/zen_shortcuts.dart` takes `activator → callback` pairs and **autofocuses** (a `Shortcuts` widget sees nothing until focus is inside it — the detail that makes keyboard support actually work rather than merely compile), with `ZenShortcuts.primary(key, cb)` binding both `⌘key` and `Ctrl+key` so an iPad Magic Keyboard and an Android tablet both work. The writing bench now drives its session from the keys: ⌘←/⌘→ between cards, ⌘R to clear, tested with synthesised modifier+key events.

### [2026-09-26] Two Live Credentials Were Committed and Shipped — Removed, Guarded, Rotation Required
- **`[🔴 SECURITY]` A metered Azure Speech key and an OpenRouter secret were committed to the repository and compiled into every build.** Found while starting W0 (routing AI traffic through the proxy): after cleaning `lib/`, a single `git grep` located the **Azure key in seven tracked files** — `lib/core/services/api_key_pool.dart` (as a "fallback" literal), `codemagic.yaml` (**the CI release injection point**), `web_audiobook/index.html` (**a web page that handed the key to every visitor**), its generator, and four dev scripts. `build_release.sh` additionally injects `GEMINI_API_KEY` / `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` / `AZURE_SPEECH_KEY` at compile time via `--dart-define`, and the only usage limit (`_checkUsageLimit()`) runs **client-side** — trivially bypassed by anyone holding a key.
- **`[🔧 FIX]` Seven instances scrubbed, verified from disk.** `scratch/scrub_leaked_azure_key.py` (dry-run first, post-condition asserted) rewrote them contextually: `codemagic.yaml` now uses its **secret reference** `$AZURE_SPEECH_KEY` — the correct Codemagic pattern, so the build keeps working — and every other site carries `MISSING_KEY_SEE_ISSUES`, which fails loudly rather than quietly spending a leaked credential. `api_key_pool.dart` now returns `'MISSING_KEY'` for both keys, and its class doc states that no credential may ever live in that file.
- **`[🛡️ GUARD]` `test/core/secret_guard_test.dart` — 3/3.** Refuses `sk-…`-shaped secrets anywhere in `lib/`, any `AIzaSy…` key outside the *generated, deliberately public* `firebase_options.dart`, and any 28+-character literal inside `api_key_pool.dart`. Each exclusion (import paths and the publishable RevenueCat `appl_`/`goog_` keys) carries its reason in the code, so the rules cannot be quietly loosened later.
- **`[🐛 FOUND WHILE FIXING]` The guard's own first draft was wrong.** `[^']{28,}` happily spans unrelated code between two innocent apostrophes and manufactured offenders — it flagged `package:flutter_riverpod/flutter_riverpod.dart` as a secret. It is now single-line and path-aware, with the reasoning written down.
- **`[❗ ACTION REQUIRED — cannot be done from this repository]` The keys remain valid, and they are in git history.** Deleting a literal does not un-leak it: **rotate the Azure Speech key and the OpenRouter key**, then review every value in `build_release.sh`, because `--dart-define` values are extractable from a release binary. Logged as URGENT in `ISSUES.md`.
- **`[✅ NOT A LEAK, recorded so it is not "fixed"]`** The `AIzaSy…` values in `firebase_options.dart` are Firebase *project identifiers*, published by design; the guard deliberately permits them there. That makes the **missing `firestore.rules`** (defect D6 in the roadmap) more consequential, not less.
- **`[📋 REMAINING]`** The guard scans `lib/` only, so the seven extra sites were cleaned by a **manual script** rather than prevented by a test: extending the rule repo-wide (CI configs, `tool/`, `web_audiobook/`) is queued so the next leak is caught automatically.

### [2026-09-26] The AI Cost Surface, Mapped: 24 Outbound Calls and What Should Be Cached
- **`[📚 DOCS]` New `docs/AI_CACHING_ROADMAP.md`.** Answers *"is a book's AI translation cached server-side so other users reuse it, and we don't have to call the AI again?"* — by tabulating **every** outbound call in the app with a cacheability verdict, the recommended cache (phone or Firebase) and a P1/P2 priority, then turning it into seven workstreams with acceptance criteria. Indexed in `docs/PROJECT_MAP.md`; tracked as a P1 entry in `ISSUES.md`.
- **`[🔍 FINDING]` The answer is no, with exactly one exception.** A book's AI work is cached **per device** (`Hive.box('ai_cache')`, key `story_$deckId`) and the shared `stories` collection is **read-only from the client** (one `.get()`, zero writes), so it holds raw text rather than AI output — meaning **every device re-runs the AI parse for the same story**, and the translation into the user's language is a separate on-device ML Kit job, also per device. The one genuinely shared cache is **dictionary expansion**.
- **`[✅ FINDING]` The pattern already exists in this codebase, and it is good.** `getDictionaryExpansionV1` → `dictionaryExpansionCache` uses content identity (`wordId` + `languageCode` + `sourceDefinitionHash`), an API that **rejects any other field** so the key cannot be poisoned, `MODEL_VERSION` / `PROMPT_VERSION` for safe invalidation, a `SUPPORTED_LANGUAGES` allowlist, a `ready` / `generating`+**lease** / `failed`+`retryAfter` state machine so concurrent misses pay **once**, and per-uid quota. Every workstream in the roadmap copies this shape instead of inventing a new one.
- **`[⚠️ GAP]` Both AI proxies are stateless**, so the other ~20 operations in `gemini_service.dart` (3503 lines) re-invoke the model for every user, language and device: `generateContentProxyV2` / `openRouterProxyV2` read `{model, body}`, `fetch`, and return — no key, no de-dup, no cache. Meanwhile **TTS is cached per device for audio that is byte-identical across users** (`tts_cache/` + a deliberate `_hashText`, single characters only) while a **4-hour weekly fair-use quota** already exists; **4047** `i.ytimg.com` thumbnail URL constructions meet **3** cached-image widgets; source article HTML and the immutable, URL-versioned `hanzi-writer-data` stroke JSON are fetched with no cache at all; and the local keys (`def_$word`, `story_$deckId`, `detailed_summary_$title`) carry **no language, no version and no TTL** — the one correct key in the file is `_nuanceCacheKey(sortedWords)`.
- **`[🚧 RISK]` Two dependencies that should not be production dependencies, found while auditing.** `translate_a/single?client=gtx` is an **undocumented, unauthenticated** Google endpoint (no contract, per-IP throttling), and **`firestore.rules` is not in the repository at all** while the client reads `stories` directly and both proxies are `invoker: "public"` with `cors: true` and no visible auth check (the dictionary callable even sets `enforceAppCheck: false` explicitly).
- **`[📏 SCOPE]` Docs only — no code changed.** The roadmap states what it does *not* claim: no bill was measured, the deployed collections were not inspected, and rules/IAM/App Check live outside this repo, so the relay risk is inferred from the handler bodies. The priority order (bundled-story parsing first) is plausible but **unmeasured** — server-side telemetry should confirm which operations dominate spend before choosing between W1 and W2.

### [2026-09-26] The Typeface Was Imaginary: Noto Serif SC Is Finally Bundled, With a Guard That Proves It
- **`[🔴 FIX]` The app's calligraphic identity never shipped.** `lib/` asked for `'NotoSerifSC'` in **85** places, and `AppTheme` asked for `'NotoSansSC'` in every role from `displayLarge` (120px - the big hanzi) down to `labelSmall`, while `pubspec.yaml`'s `fonts:` block was **commented out** and `assets/fonts/` did not exist. Flutter falls back silently for an unknown family, so **every one of those sites** has rendered in the platform font for the life of the project: Latin as Roboto on Android and San Francisco on iOS, and **the hanzi themselves - the product - in whatever sans-serif CJK face the device happened to have.** It went unnoticed because on Linux/ChromeOS the host often *does* ship Noto Serif CJK, which is where the design was previewed.
- **`[✨ FEATURE]` `NotoSerifSC` is real now: two SIL OFL subsets, 14.7 MB.** Built by `scratch/build_font_subset.py` from the 24 MB upstream variable font (which is deliberately **not** kept in the repo, and whose licence ships as `assets/fonts/OFL.txt` because the OFL requires it to travel with the font) into static Regular + Bold: instanced with `fontTools.varLib.instancer`, then subset. Coverage is **measured, not assumed**: `scratch/verify_font_coverage.py` reports **0 taught characters missing and 0 hanzi missing** against the app's own data - `hanzi_metadata.json` (**9,565** hanzi), `dictionary.db` (190 MB, +4,997) and the reading room's books (+517) - plus ASCII, Latin-1, **Vietnamese**, CJK punctuation, and **pinyin tone vowels, all 34 of which were explicitly verified present** because pinyin falling back to a second face is exactly the inconsistency this change exists to remove.
- **`[📐 DESIGN]` The type roles now say what they mean.** `display`/`headline`/`title` name the bundled serif, so large glyphs are one consistent face on both platforms; `body`/`label` and the component themes (app bars, buttons, dialogs) declare **no** family, i.e. platform-native. That split is deliberate twice over: it keeps UI text idiomatic per platform, and it leaves body/label **metrics untouched**, so the measured locale-overflow budget in `locale_layout_guard_test.dart` stays valid. The old `_fontFamily = 'NotoSansSC'` constant - referenced by 22 component-theme sites - is now an explicit `null` with a comment saying so; those sites never resolved that family anyway, so their rendering is byte-for-byte unchanged.
- **`[🧹 CLEANUP]` 31 sites lost a phantom family, via `scratch/pin_serif_family.py`.** `'Serif'` ×19 - a platform-generic name that resolves on Android and **falls back to the sans-serif system face on iOS**, so those sites were inconsistent between platforms *by construction* - plus `'serif'` ×5, `l10n.serif` ×3 and `l10n.notoserifsc` ×3 (a font family routed through the **translation files**, where a translator editing a string could change the typography) and `'NotoSansSC'` ×1. The codemod asserts a **post-condition** (none survive) instead of a pre-count, because concurrent edits moved the `'Serif'` total from 24 to 19 mid-flight; its first run therefore **correctly refused to apply**.
- **`[🛡️ GUARD]` `test/core/typography_guard_test.dart` - 3/3.** Every `fontFamily: '<literal>'` in `lib/` must be declared under `fonts:` in `pubspec.yaml`; every declared font asset must exist **and be non-trivial in size** (a placeholder file falls back exactly like a missing one); and the family must ship in two weights with its licence. This bug is invisible at runtime - it is a silent fallback, not an error - which is precisely why nothing caught it for the life of the project and why a guard is the only thing that would have.
- **`[📋 REMAINING - a size decision, not an oversight]`** Body/label text stays platform-native rather than honouring the theme's original "NotoSansSC Mandate" for UI copy. Satisfying that means bundling a **second** subset (~+7.5 MB, ~22 MB of fonts in total), which is a judgement about app size weighed against a 190 MB dictionary - so it is queued as a decision rather than taken here. Also untouched: `FontFeature.tabularFigures` (2 uses), so counters, streaks and scores still wobble as digits change.
- **`[⚠️ HONEST LIMIT]`** Glyph *coverage* is proven by script; glyph *rendering* is not. I cannot see the app, so "the serif now appears on iOS and Android" is an inference from Flutter's documented fallback behaviour plus a correct declaration - not an observation from a device.

### [2026-09-26] The Last Three Silent Outcomes: a Session, a Deletion, a Swipe
- **`[🔧 FIX]` `session_summary_screen` had 0 haptics.** The app's biggest reward screen already rang a bell in `initState` and was silent to the touch — the loudest and least feelable moment in the app. It now fires `milestone()`, because a completed session is the achievement that screen exists to mark.
- **`[✨ FEATURE]` `destructive()` — an eighth primitive, and the first that is not a taptic tap.** Deleting an account is the most destructive action in the app and it was completely silent: the refusal pass gave `auth` its error paths, but its **destructive confirmation** stayed bare, so the app's most irreversible gesture felt exactly like a slightly firmer press. `HapticFeedback.vibrate()` maps to the iOS system vibration (`kSystemSoundID_Vibrate`) and to `HapticFeedbackConstants.LONG_PRESS` on Android, where the taptic styles share a constant — so it is a genuinely different tier rather than a louder `heavy()`. Deliberately **one** buzz, never a pattern: it must not read as the refusal's triple, and it silences anything still in flight.
- **`[🔧 FIX]` Swiping a card out of a deck.** `deck_detail_screen`'s `Dismissible.onDismissed` committed the removal with no touch feedback, while the file's only haptic sat in a different handler **71 lines away**. It now fires `medium()` — a state change worth noticing.
- **`[🧹 DEAD CODE]` `onLongPress: () {}` deleted** (`smart_media_desk_screen.dart`). An **empty callback** that swallowed long-presses and did nothing is worse than no handler, and it was the only one of the two long-press sites that was not a real interaction. Deleting it beat giving it a buzz.
- **`[📏 STILL DELIBERATELY SILENT]`** Tab changes were **already** ticking (`main_navigation_screen`), so the earlier "tabs stay quiet" note was really about **rows**. `ZenToast.success` (**16** sites) and `ZenToast.info` (**27**) stay silent on purpose: they confirm an action the user just took, which the ink bleed already acknowledges — ticking 43 confirmations would drown the **24** refusals that actually carry information.
- **`[🧪 TESTED]`** `test/core/feedback_guard_test.dart` **10/10**, alongside the three zero-baseline source guards. The new test pins the destructive tier's *shape*: exactly **one** impact, and **not** a re-labelled taptic style — `HapticFeedback.vibrate()` is the only call in the vocabulary that carries no style argument, which is what makes it a different tier rather than a louder tap.
- **`[⚠️ DEVICE CHECK]`** The felt strength of `vibrate()` — and of everything else in this campaign — cannot be verified in CI: haptics are a no-op in the iOS Simulator. Every claim here is that the *message is sent*, never that it *feels right*.
- **`[📋 NOTE]`** `dart format --set-exit-if-changed lib` reports **102 of 345** files, including many this campaign never touched (`character_loader.dart`, `firebase_options.dart`), so that is a repo-wide formatting/line-ending condition and was **not** used as a cleanliness signal here. Instead each hand-inserted block was checked back against the raw indentation of its neighbours, and the analyzer reports **No issues found!** on every touched file.

### [2026-09-26] The App Learns To Feel: 22 Refusals, a Card Flip, a Milestone Crescendo
- **`[📊 AUDIT FIRST]` Measured before touching anything.** Primitive usage across `lib` was: `light` **79**, `selection` **30**, `medium` **25**, `success` **17**, `heavy` **9**, `error` **1**. Two of the six primitives existed and were effectively unused, while the app *told* the learner "no" from **22** `ZenToast.error` call sites and **2** `ZenShake` sites and *felt* like "no" **once**.
- **`[🔧 FIX]` The refusal is heard 24 times over, from two places.** `ZenToast.error` now fires `HapticsManager.error()`, so all 22 "no" call sites gained a refusal without one of them changing — the toast is the app's single funnel for refusal, which is exactly why the call-site approach had failed. `ZenShake` fires its own for the same reason and to a stronger effect: the widget *is* the rejection, so it can no longer be forgotten. That required **de-duplicating** the two sites which had their own buzz (`quiz_step`, `radical_lesson_screen`), because a widget that owns its moment makes a call-site pairing a double buzz.
- **`[✨ FEATURE]` `milestone()`, a seventh primitive.** A **rising** crescendo (light → medium → heavy) for achievements — deliberately the opposite shape to the refusal, which is one weight repeated three times. Wired to the genuine milestones: a **mastery seal landing** (on the arrival's `onEnd`, so the thud lands *with* the stamp) and **crossing a `ScholarRank`** in the Ink Stone, where ordinary points keep the `success` tick.
- **`[✨ FEATURE]` The card flip finally has a touch.** `ZenFlipCard` already played a paper-flip *sound*, but turning a card over — the signature gesture of a flashcard app — was otherwise silent. It now fires `medium()`, the vocabulary's "reveal", in both directions.
- **`[✨ FEATURE]` The reward surfaces answer back.** `StreakFlameBadge` ticks `success()` when the streak grows, and `InkStoneWidget` celebrates points or a rank. All three previously had **0** haptics *and* **0** sound, so the app's emotional peaks were the quietest part of it.
- **`[🔧 FIX]` Two blind-spot features.** `auth` sat at **0** haptics: its two refusal points (terms not accepted, and the entire `FirebaseAuthException` credential map) now refuse. `settings` also sat at **0**: the Haptic and Sound **switches** now tick, and the switch settles *first*, so turning haptics **off** is silent while turning it back on ticks — which is how a user finds out the switch does what it says.
- **`[🔧 FIX]` All 7 `Slider` widgets tick.** Discrete sliders (`divisions` set) tick per detent in `onChanged`; continuous ones tick **once** in `onChangeEnd`. Ticking a continuous slider in `onChanged` would have been a continuous buzz — 9 sliders exist and only 4 have `divisions`, so the distinction is not academic.
- **`[🧪 TESTED]`** `test/core/feedback_guard_test.dart` **9/9**, with two new tests that pin *semantics* rather than wiring: a milestone must arrive as light → medium → heavy **in that order** (`reason: 'the rise is the meaning'`), and `ZenShake` must send the full triple refusal on a rising trigger with silence at rest. Analyzer: **No issues found!** across all 15 touched libraries.
- **`[🔍 CORRECTION]`** An earlier count in this campaign said "30 error-toned toasts and 4 shake sites". The exact figures are **22** `ZenToast.error(...)` **call sites** (the other 8 matches were `ZenToastTone.error` references inside the toast itself) and **2** `ZenShake` call sites (2 matches were the widget's own definition). A fine-grained grep inflated both; the code comments, the guard's doc comment and this entry now all carry the corrected numbers.
- **`[📏 DELIBERATELY NOT DONE]`** Navigation rows and tabs get **no** haptic — the ink bleed already confirms a tap, and the discipline is that haptics mark outcomes, not taps. Untouched for the same reason or for lack of a decision: scroll snapping, `Dismissible`, `PageView` and `TabBar`. The audit also found two capabilities the app has **never** used — `SystemSound.play(SystemSoundType.click)` (an asset-free tick) and `HapticFeedback.vibrate()` (a categorically stronger buzz than any taptic impact; neither needs the Android `VIBRATE` permission). Both are logged in `ISSUES.md` rather than invented into the product.
- **`[⚠️ HONEST LIMIT]`** Haptics are a **no-op in the iOS Simulator**. The guard proves the messages are **sent** — never that they are **felt**. Every addition here is a device check that no test in this repo can perform.

### [2026-09-26] The Feedback Promises Are Enforced: One Beat, No Buzzsaws, Three Zero-Baselines
- **`[🔧 FIX]` A burst of wrong answers no longer queues a buzzsaw.** `HapticsManager.success()` / `error()` were un-cancellable `Future.delayed` chains with no re-entrancy guard, so three cards answered wrong in quick succession in `quiz_step` queued **nine** heavy impacts instead of three clean triplets. Every pattern now runs through one private `_pattern(...)` that takes a **generation token** on entry — the same shape as `audio_service.dart`'s `_playbackGeneration` — and re-checks it before every impact: the newest outcome retires whatever is still in flight. Measured, the burst now produces **5** impacts (1 + 1 + 3; the already-sent leads cannot be recalled) where it used to produce 9.
- **`[🔧 FIX]` Switching haptics off is now immediate.** `setEnabled(false)` bumps the same token, so a pattern already in flight stops rather than finishing "because it was already buzzing": previously the last two impacts of an `error()` chain still arrived over the following 200ms.
- **`[🧱 DEBT PAID]` `ZenMotion.beat` finally has a consumer.** The gap between two impacts was three separate `Duration(milliseconds: 100)` literals; it is now the shared `ZenMotion.beat`. § Motion had recorded that token as *"no consumer at all (0 references). Use it or delete it, but do not count it as shipped"* — and this is what it was for: § 5 asks motion and haptics to feel like one thing, so they share one beat instead of each carrying its own literal. The standards entry was re-stamped accordingly, and the spacing is now **asserted to the millisecond**, so re-timing `beat` fails loudly instead of drifting. Side effect: `haptics_manager.dart` now holds **0** raw duration literals, down from 3.
- **`[🛡️ GUARD]` New `test/core/feedback_guard_test.dart` — three zero-baseline ratchets.** (1) No raw `HapticFeedback.*` outside the manager (the check that would have caught the paywall's six); (2) no direct `AssetSource('audio/sfx_...')` / `AssetSource('audio/zen_...')` outside `ZenSoundService` (the check that would have caught the quiz grading out loud with sound effects switched off); (3) a source assertion that `settings_controller.dart` still calls **both** `HapticsManager.setEnabled` and `ZenSoundService.instance.setEnabled`, so neither Settings switch can silently drift off its gate. Each prints its offender list on failure.
- **`[🧪 TESTED]` The guard is behavioural, not a source scan.** `HapticFeedback.*` is a platform-channel message, so the new tests **intercept `SystemChannels.platform` and count the impacts that really arrive**: the burst must end at **5** (this assertion fails against the old implementation), the double tick must land **exactly** one `ZenMotion.beat` apart — checked one millisecond either side — a disable mid-pattern must stop the remaining impacts, and with haptics off **none** of the six primitives may send anything. **7/7**.
- **`[📋 REMAINING]`** No `cancel()` was added: nothing would call it, and this repo deliberately tracks unused API as debt, so the token plus the disable-bump is the whole fix. The residual is stated rather than hidden — a fire-and-forget chain can still outlive the screen by at most one beat (100ms).
- **`[📏 RATCHETS]`** `test/core` passed clean, so all seven motion ratchets plus the three new feedback baselines hold. **Deliberately not quoted here:** the *global* counters, because concurrent agents are editing the same files — the measured duration total moved 135 → 132 and `ZenMotion.` 290 → 293 during this change, only part of which is this work. The verifiable claim is the local one: **3 → 0** raw duration literals in `haptics_manager.dart`.

### [2026-09-26] The Feedback Layer Gets One Gate: Sound Obeys the Setting, Haptics Reach Core
- **`[🔧 FIX]` The "Sound effects" toggle only silenced *some* of the app.** There were two sound paths. `ZenSoundService` (4 organic sounds, its own player, a tuned 0.16-0.26 ceiling) honours `enableSoundEffects`. But `AudioService.playCorrectSfx` / `playWrongSfx` - the quiz's graded accents - played straight to **`_player`, the speech player, and never consulted the setting at all**: a learner who turned sound effects off still heard the quiz grade every answer. The four `AudioService.play*Sfx` methods are deleted, and `ZenSoundService` gains `playCorrect()` / `playWrong()` on **its own player**, behind the same `_enabled` gate. Correct/wrong is now the sixth and seventh sound in the one suite that the Settings screen actually controls.
- **`[📐 DESIGN]` The shared player was NOT the bug - recorded so nobody "fixes" it.** Reading `_player` alone says the SFX and the spoken pronunciation share a channel and must truncate each other. They do not: `sfx_correct.wav` is **26,504 bytes = ~0.30s**, and `quiz_step` schedules `playCharacter` at exactly **300ms**. The sequence is hand-choreographed to the clip length, so it is now **documented in `ZenSoundService`'s header** ("nothing here may ever play on the speech player") rather than churned. Moving the accents to their own player preserves that timing and additionally lets the accent finish naturally, since the word no longer replaces it as a *source* on the same player.
- **`[🧱 REFACTOR]` `HapticsManager` moved to `lib/core/services/` (45 import sites).** It lived in `features/flashcards/presentation/utils/` while being imported by `core`, `shared` and ten features - so `shared/widgets/*` reached *into* a feature, and `core` was about to as well. It now sits beside `zen_sound_service.dart`, which is the same concern on the audio side. That move was a prerequisite, not a nice-to-have: routing the two `core/presentation/widgets` call sites through a feature path would have made the inversion **worse**, which is why it was promoted out of the backlog into this change.
- **`[🔧 FIX]` 13 raw `HapticFeedback.*` calls now go through the manager.** They were the reason haptics-off did not fully work: `custom_paywall_screen` (**6**, including on tap-to-purchase), `shadowing_studio_screen` (**5**), `hanzi_text_field` and `zen_search_bar` (**1** each) buzzed regardless of the user's choice. **Raw `HapticFeedback.` calls in `lib/` outside the manager are now zero.**
- **`[🧹 CLEANUP]` 106KB of never-played audio removed.** `playCompleteSfx` and `playStreakSfx` had **zero callers** (only their own definitions ever matched), so `sfx_complete.wav` (52,964b) and `sfx_streak.wav` (52,964b) shipped in every build for nothing. Session completion is already covered by `playBellChime()` in `session_summary_screen` and `lesson_screen`; `sfx_streak` never had a caller at all. Both files are git-tracked, so restoring either is one `git checkout`. The 4 imports of `package:flutter/services.dart` that became unnecessary once `HapticFeedback` left those files were removed with them.
- **`[⚙️ TOOLING]`** The sweep ran as a content-matched codemod, `scratch/wire_feedback.py`, dry-run first - it asserts **45** import sites and **13** calls before writing, so a concurrent edit fails loudly instead of mis-applying. It matched by content, never by line number.
- **`[📋 REMAINING]` Three follow-ups are queued in `ISSUES.md`**, not done: `HapticsManager.success()` / `error()` are un-cancellable `Future.delayed` chains with no re-entrancy guard, so a burst of wrong answers stacks into 6/9/12 heavy impacts and a buzz can land after you have left the screen; correct/wrong is still only wired into the **course quiz**, not flashcard review; and a shrink-only ratchet for "raw `HapticFeedback.` outside the manager" should lock this in, which is now a **0-baseline** guard rather than an aspiration.
- **`[🧪 TESTED]` Full `flutter test`: 780 passing, 6 failing - and all six were already failing before this change, none in its blast radius.** They are: **2** in `test/unit_tests/stroke_matcher_test.dart` and **1** in `poetry_cover_manifest_test.dart` (both families already logged OPEN as P1/P2 in `ISSUES.md`), **2** in `test/features/media/cultural_context_book_parity_test.dart` (`Bad state: No element` - a finder matching no widget, in an in-progress parity-test family; verified by dumping the exception, which rules out a colour cause and therefore rules out this change's `splashColor`), and **1** in `test/features/flashcards/reading_mode_book_parity_test.dart` (a legend overflowing 135px at the 320px viewport, in an *untracked* file another agent is still writing). **`test/core` passed clean, so all seven motion ratchets hold.** The earlier targeted run over core/shared/course/premium/live_translate/flashcards was 326/1 with only the reading-mode failure. The analyzer's only new findings were the 4 `unnecessary_import` infos this change *created* in the files whose last use of `services.dart` was `HapticFeedback` - all 4 fixed, and those files plus the new manager now report **No issues found!**

### [2026-09-26] Three Animations That Teach: the Tone, the Card, and the Character
- **`[✨ FEATURE]` The pitch contour draws itself.** `CalligraphicPitchContour` is the app's tone graph (used by `ToneComparisonSheet` and every Echo Hall pronunciation report, and therefore everywhere a learner is told their tone was wrong). It used to appear fully formed; the brush now lays it down left to right over `ZenMotion.quick` on `ZenMotion.enter`. The painter takes a `reveal` (0-1, in `shouldRepaint`) and inks only that fraction, walking 96 even arc-length stations and **lerping the head** between the two samples that straddle the cut-off, so the travelling tip is smooth rather than stepped. The reveal duration is a widget parameter defaulting to the token, so a screen can lengthen it without inventing a constant.
- **`[🐛 FIXED]` The obvious version of that idea would have flickered.** The widget is fed *live* contours while the learner is still speaking (see `didUpdateWidget`), so replaying the reveal on every update would strobe the stroke. Replay is therefore gated on a **genuinely new contour** - and a mid-flight update lets the current stroke finish instead of restarting it. Both behaviours are pinned by tests, not by hope.
- **`[✨ FEATURE]` A card now flies into the deck.** Adding a card in `deck_card_picker_screen` used to just make the row vanish. The card now travels from the tapped row to the target over `ZenMotion.quick` on `ZenMotion.enter`, shrinking as it lands, via `ZenFlight.to()` in `lib/shared/widgets/zen_flight.dart` (one `OverlayEntry`, so it can cross widget boundaries). `word_detail_dialog` was **rejected as the site** during reconnaissance: its deck target is not on screen, so there is nothing honest to fly *to*. The flight never blocks the write: the save proceeds independently, `onArrived` fires even when the animation is skipped, and a test asserts the word is still saved **exactly once** with the flight in play.
- **`[✨ FEATURE]` The forge now shows how a character is built.** Forging a radical used to recolour a border. `ZenAssembly` (`lib/shared/widgets/zen_assembly.dart`) now flies the character's parts in from their own offsets with a slight rotation, `ZenMotion.stagger` apart, and lands them on `ZenMotion.arrival` - a teaching animation, which is the one thing in this app allowed to overshoot.
- **`[📊 DATA HONESTY]` The assembly reads real decomposition, not a decoration.** `hanzi_metadata.json` already stores an **IDS** decomposition per character (`⿰日月` for `明`), so the parts are the radical the learner actually placed plus the components the metadata says are left over, in reading order. When no decomposition exists for a character, or it does not mention the placed radical, the code **falls back to the honest two-element form** (radical + character) rather than fabricating components; a guard caps the part count so the glyphs stay legible in a 140px tile.
- **`[♿ A11Y]` All three honour Reduce Motion.** The contour is painted complete on the first frame with no ticker; the radical is shown composed; and the flight is skipped entirely while still reporting arrival, so the add flow is byte-for-byte unchanged for a user who asked for less motion.
- **`[🧪 TESTED]`** Four new/extended suites, **29/29** together: `test/core/zen_ink_splash_test.dart` (10), `test/features/live_translate/calligraphic_pitch_contour_test.dart` (8, an existing file extended), `test/shared/zen_flight_test.dart` (5) and `test/shared/zen_assembly_test.dart` (6). Each pins the reduce-motion contract as *no ticker at all*, not merely "less travel". Ratchets **unmoved**: all three interfaces source every duration and curve from `ZenMotion`, `lib` was measured at **4** raw `Duration` literals in `radical_lesson_screen` **before and after** its edit, and `motion_vocabulary` + `motion_guard` still pass 16/16.
- **`[📋 REMAINING]` Four more "cool but optional" animations are queued** with reasoning in `ISSUES.md` - scroll unroll, book cover swing, deck riffle, ink drop for Ink Points and a brush-stroke audiobook progress bar - of which two need a standards decision first because they change a token the vocabulary already ships.

### [2026-09-26] The Tap Is Ink Now: One Theme Line Rewrites All 35 Taps
- **`[✨ FEATURE]` Every tap in the app bleeds cinnabar ink instead of flashing Flutter's grey disc.** `ThemeData.splashFactory` had never been set anywhere (measured: **0** occurrences in `lib/`), so all **35** `InkWell`s plus the one `InkResponse` painted the stock grey ripple while every other surface in the app spoke ink and paper — the last place the app still spoke in Material's voice. One new file, `lib/core/theme/zen_ink_splash.dart`, now backs `splashFactory:` in **both** themes, so the upgrade costs one line per theme and reaches every row, chip, tile and button without touching any of them.
- **`[🎨 DESIGN]` It is a drop of ink, not a circle.** `ZenInkSplash` paints a feathered, slightly irregular blob (the radius is modulated by two slow sine lobes) filled with a radial gradient so the edge thins as it soaks outward, spreading over `ZenMotion.quick` on `ZenMotion.enter` and dissolving with `ZenMotion.breathe`. The tint is `splashColor` thinned by the factory to **10%**, so themes keep a *full-strength* accent — cinnabar on paper, amber on ink (cinnabar would be invisible on the dark surface). Deliberately faint: the bleed sits under text and must never compete with it.
- **`[🔧 FIX]` The clipping is Flutter's, not a guess.** The first draft hand-rolled the clip and did not compile (`Rect.fromRectAndRadius` does not exist); another agent found it mid-edit, correctly refused to guess at someone else's geometry, and raised it as a build blocker. The real defect was a **design** error, not a typo, so the repair was made against the SDK source: `ZenInkSplashFactory.clipOf(box, containedInkWell, rectCallback)` is Flutter's private `_getClipCallback`, and `paintFeature` clips exactly the way `InkRipple`/`InkSplash` do (`customBorder.getOuterPath(rect, textDirection:)`, else `borderRadius.toRRect(rect)`, else `clipRect`) over `canvas.transform(transform.storage)` when the ink layer's transform is not a pure translation. The dead helper was deleted. **That P2 blocker is now closed** with this entry as its evidence.
- **`[♿ A11Y]` Reduce Motion puts the ink down without a ticker.** The gesture still gets feedback — the bleed appears instantly at a fixed tint — but nothing travels, and `confirm()` resolves it in a single frame, so no ticker ever starts. It remains a *response to a touch*, so it does not violate "nothing animates as decoration".
