- **`[📋 REMAINING]` Not everything taps.** `InkWell`s that sit outside a `Material` still paint nothing at all (a pre-existing Flutter requirement, not a regression), and the SDK-timed affordances listed in § 2.4 (`SwitchListTile`, `CheckboxListTile`, `Slider`) keep Flutter's own ink because their timing is not ours to theme.
- **`[🧪 TESTED]`** `test/core/zen_ink_splash_test.dart` **10/10** — theme wiring on both themes, the furthest-corner reach, all three clip branches (rect / radius / custom border) surviving a *painted* frame, a tap that bleeds and then removes itself from the ink layer, a cancelled gesture, and a reduce-motion case asserting **no ticker is started at all**. `motion_vocabulary` + `motion_guard` still pass with the ratchets **unmoved** (every new duration is a `ZenMotion` token; `Duration.zero` is not matched by the raw-literal ratchet). `flutter analyze lib` → **no errors**.
- **`[🧹 CLEANUP]`** One `curly_braces_in_flow_control_structures` info in `smart_media_desk_screen.dart` fixed while passing (`lib` went from 4 infos to 3; the remaining three are in `conversation_screen.dart`, which is another agent's file, and were left alone).

### [2026-09-25] The Overflow Backlog Is a Ratchet Now: 12 Action Rows Fixed, Three Guards Added
- **`[🚦 GUARD]` Four guards in `test/core/locale_layout_guard_test.dart`, ported from `scratch/fixed_width_text_audit.py`** — an action row (`Row` holding a button **and a localized label** with no `Expanded`/`Flexible`/`Wrap`), the **general** row rule (any row carrying a localized label, which the button-specific one was only sampling), and a width-capped and a height-capped container (any finite cap, not just ≤130/≤48). The container checks are depth-aware, so a container's own cap is never confused with a nested one, and they skip any child that already declares a degradation (`FittedBox(scaleDown)`, or `maxLines` + `overflow`). The row check reproduced the audit's 43 flags exactly before the localized-label refinement; each guard carries a baseline and prints its offender list on failure. `_rowIsSafe` is the single definition of a safe row (marker, flexible child, or no localized label) so both row rules cannot drift.
- **`[🔧 FIX]` 12 rows (23 flags) made translation-proof — the row ratchet is now at ZERO:** `universal_scanner_screen` (results header, "Found N characters", scan-results header → `Expanded`; the "Smart Deck" pair → `Wrap`), `web_browser_screen` (select-all → `Flexible` + ellipsis), `calligraphy_canvas_sheet` (trace header → `Expanded`), `book_reader_screen` (sentence number → `Expanded`), `reading_room_screen` (recent-bookmarks header → `Expanded`, dropping a `Spacer`), `story_reader_screen` (summary sheet header → `Expanded`; the summary/pinyin pair → `Wrap`). Three rows are structurally safe and were **marked rather than churned**: `live_call_screen` (each control caps its own 60/64px label inside `_CallControlButton`), `scenario_selection_screen` (badge + icon only) and `audiobook_player_screen` (fixed-size transport controls; its one localized string is a tooltip). The explicit `// locale-safe: <reason>` marker is matched in a ±2-line window, because `dart format` moves a trailing comment onto the line *inside* the call — the first attempt lost three markers that way.
- **`[📋 GENERAL SURFACE, measured]`** Looking past buttons changed the picture: the narrow rules were covering ~15 % of the real area. Measured on 2026-09-25 — **72** rows carrying localized text with no flexible child, **40** boxes with a fixed height around text (only 6 of them ≤48px), **11** with a fixed width (only 10 ≤130px), plus, by pattern count, **75** `letterSpacing` values above 0.5 (the standard caps tracking at 0.5 because it inflates Latin/Cyrillic 10-15 %; today only the *button theme* is guarded), 10 fixed `childAspectRatio` grids, 5 `TextOverflow.clip`/`.fade` silent-clips, 1 `softWrap: false`, ~7 localized `AppBar` titles and ~10-20 `Tab(` labels in fixed chrome. The three layout classes are now ratchets (72 / 11 / 40, the last two no longer capped at an arbitrary 130/48 — the rule is the standard's own wording: never fix the size of a box holding localized text); the rest are itemised in `ISSUES.md` as the next guards to add.
- **`[🧪 TESTED]`** Guard suite **8/8**; `test/features/reading` **80/80** with the layout edits; analyzer clean on all eight touched libraries; `dart format` clean.
- **`[⚠️ NOT MINE — BLOCKER]`** `lib/core/theme/zen_ink_splash.dart` (untracked, another agent's in-flight file) calls `Rect.fromRectAndRadius`, which does not exist (`RRect.fromRectAndRadius` does), so everything importing it fails to compile; logged in `ISSUES.md`, deliberately not repaired.

### [2026-09-25] Loading to Content Now Fades In, and the Accepted Limits Are Written Down
- **`[✨ FEATURE]` A loader now hands over to its content instead of cutting.** § Loading States has always required it - *"Fade result content in. Wrap content that replaces a loader in `ZenFadeIn` so it eases in rather than snapping"* - but the measurement said otherwise: **26** `.when(` sites, only **10** `ZenFadeIn` uses, and **20 files** replacing a `ZenLoader` with content in a single frame.
- **`[🧹 CLEANUP]` 12 branches across 12 files now fade in** (`course_screen`, `course_selection_screen`, `tome_manager_screen`, `deck_card_picker_screen`, `deck_detail_screen`, `story_mode_screen`, `deck_selection_sheet`, `shadowing_studio_screen`, `media_hub_screen`, `dashboard_screen`, `book_detail_screen`, `quick_look_sheet`), via a guarded dry-run-first codemod (`scratch/fade_in_when_data.py`). The codemod's first pass proposed 18 branches in 15 files; 6 were reverted for the reasons below.
- **`[🐛 FIXED]` Two real defects, found and fixed in the sweep itself.** The first pass wrapped branches whose expression is a **sliver list** (`book_catalog_screen` ×3) - a `List<Widget>` is not a widget - and mis-parsed one block (`dictionary_screen:627`). Both files were restored from a pre-sweep backup, and the codemod learned two guards: **reject an expression starting with `[`**, and **reject a *spread* call** (`...async.when(...)`, whose branches are merged into a `children` list and so return lists). A third offender (`cultural_context_screen`, also spread) was found by **type-checking** rather than parsing - `dart format` happily accepts a list literal as an expression - and reverted by hand.
- **`[📚 DOCS]` New § 2.4 "Accepted limits".** Records what must *not* be re-fixed: Material's SDK-timed affordances (`SwitchListTile` 8, `CheckboxListTile` 2, `Slider` 9, `BottomNavigationBar` 13, `DropdownButton` 11, `ExpansionTile` 1, `RefreshIndicator` 2 - Flutter's timings, not ours), list-returning `data:` branches, branches with early returns, and the three `SnackBarAction` sites. § Loading States records the adoption and points at it.
- **`[🧪 TESTED]`** `test/features/course` + `test/features/progression` **16/16**; `test/features/media` **90 passing** (only the 2 pre-existing `cultural_context_book_parity_test.dart` failures, another agent's); reading `book_detail_localization` + `book_catalog_localization` + `audiobook_voice_sheet` + the cultural-context suite **18 passing** (same 2 known). **0** parse failures across `lib` - verified by capturing the *full* `dart format` output, because its summary line hides them - and a temporary harness type-checked all 13 touched libraries.
- **`[⏸️ DEFERRED]`** The coverage queue in the same instruction (41 raw `showModalBottomSheet` sites, ~20 in-button spinners, 20 `MaterialPageRoute`, the dashboard `Hero`, the unused `ZenMotion.beat`) was **not** swept: the fade codemod needed two safety fixes before it was trustworthy, and another 61 blind edits on the remaining budget would have risked repeating exactly that damage. The queue is unchanged and itemised in `ISSUES.md`.
- **File(s):** 13 screens/sheets + `dictionary_screen.dart`, `scratch/fade_in_when_data.py` (new), `docs/UI_UX_STANDARDS.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `ISSUES.md`, `SESSION_STATE.md`.

### [2026-09-25] The Localization Layout Gate Is Now Enforced in CI
- **`[🚦 CI]` Reported:** *"we have multiple language in our apps … sometimes we get the text that goes out of button or goes out of the UI, what can we do to solve this, brainstorm"* → chosen first step: *"CI gate first"*. **Diagnosis:** the guard rails already existed — `test/support/locale_layout_harness.dart` (12 expansion locales x {390x844, 320x568} x {1.0x, 2.0x}, failing on any overflow), `test/core/locale_layout_guard_test.dart` (bans `fixedSize`, forces a degradation strategy on fixed-width text boxes, ratchets untranslated literals, asserts button geometry) and the documented 2.0x budget — but **only 6 product suites used the sweep against 61 `*_screen.dart`**, and **no CI job ran a single test**: `.gitlab-ci.yml` ran only `flutter analyze` (`allow_failure: true`), and the GitHub workflow only builds.
- **`[🔖 TAGS]`** New `dart_test.yaml` declares the `locale-sweep` tag, and the seven suites that exercise the harness now carry `@Tags(<String>['locale-sweep']) library;` — the guard test plus `deck_scenario_picker`, `deck_stats_design`, `speaking_tone_feedback`, `study_mode_selection_sheet_design`, `story_summary_book_parity` and `audiobook_voice_sheet`. `flutter test --tags locale-sweep` selects exactly those; note the annotation only works in the **library-directive** form — on `main()` it silently does not propagate (verified with a throwaway probe).
- **`[🚧 GATE]`** GitLab gains a blocking `flutter_test_locale` job in a new `test` stage (guard test → tagged sweep → expansion report) and GitHub gains `.github/workflows/locale_guard.yml` doing the same on pushes and pull requests. The sweep list is **derived from the tag** (`grep -rl "locale-sweep" test --include='*_test.dart'`), so tagging a new suite is all it takes to bring it into the gate, while the run stays scoped to those files instead of forcing the runner to load every suite just to read its metadata. `build_ios.yml` was deliberately left untouched (see the open item below).
- **`[📄 ARTIFACT]`** `scratch/arb_expansion_audit.py` now accepts `--out PATH`, so both jobs publish the per-locale expansion report (UTF-8) to `build/reports/arb_expansion.txt` for 30 days — reviewers see the worst expanding keys beside the diff. The refreshed report measures `ar` at 1.03x mean / 1.80x p95 and `de` at 1.20x mean / 1.86x p95, with `resetDataDesc` (6.8-9.7x), `shadow` (4.7x) and `kore` (4.5-5.2x) as the worst short labels.
- **`[🧪 TESTED]`** Every step rehearsed locally: the guard test **5/5**; `--tags locale-sweep` selecting only tagged suites and passing (`test/core`+`test/shared` 14/14, `echo_hall`+`media` 8/8, `speaking_tone_feedback` 15/15, `study_mode_selection_sheet_design` 8/8, `deck_stats_design` 6/6); `flutter test test/features/flashcards` still **115/115** untagged; the report regenerates into `build/reports/`; and all three YAML files parse (validated with `package:yaml` via a temporary script that was deleted afterwards).

### [2026-09-25] List Exit Motion, Sheet Timing, and a Seal That Arrives
- **`[✨ FEATURE]` The vocabulary had an entrance but no exit.** `AnimatedList` / `SliverAnimatedList` / `AnimatedGrid` numbered **zero**, so a row removed by a *button* — a card added to a deck leaving the picker, a word leaving a list — simply ceased to exist. Swipe removal (`Dismissible`) and review-card dismissal were already animated, so the gap was narrower than first claimed; it was real, though.
- **`[✨ FEATURE]` New `ZenMotion.exit` (250ms) plus `ZenExit`.** A new table row ("List item exit / removal") and a reusable wrapper that collapses and fades a row on `ZenMotion.exit`/`natural`, then calls `onRemoved` **exactly once**. The caller keeps the row in its data until then — that is what gives the animation something to draw; committing on the tap leaves nothing to animate. Under Reduce Motion no frames are scheduled and `onRemoved` fires on the next frame, so the data still settles.
  - Wired into `deck_card_picker_screen`, the one true button-driven hard cut: tapping "+" now sends the row out and only commits the deck assignment + `ZenToast` once it has gone.
- **`[🐛 FIXED]` Sheets were running outside the vocabulary.** `GlobalBlurredBottomSheet` — the shared sheet helper, 16 call sites — passed no animation style, so every sheet ran on Material's own **250ms / `Curves.easeOutQuad`**: neither value is in the vocabulary, despite the table marking "Sheet, dialog, panel open + close → `ZenMotion.quick`" as shipped. It now passes `sheetAnimationStyle` sourced from `ZenMotion.quick` / `natural` for both legs, via `ZenMotion.of`, so Reduce Motion still collapses it. **Correction to the earlier brief:** this helper does **not** implement "sheets converge" — it never did; it is a blurred barrier plus the platform's bottom-sheet slide. So that rule is still only partly honoured, and the **41** raw `showModalBottomSheet` sites (of 42 total, 1 belonging to this helper) are the documented remainder.
- **`[✨ FEATURE]` The mastery seal now arrives.** `MasterySeal` was a bare `CustomPaint` — the last reward moment that simply appeared, while `StreakFlameBadge`, `InkStoneWidget`, `DailyGoalReviewRing` and the `session_summary` count-up all animate. It now presses in with the vocabulary's single permitted overshoot (`ZenMotion.arrival`, ~103% at its peak) while its inner stroke draws itself up to `progress`. The overshoot is spent on the scale only; the drawn progress is clamped, so a reward curve can never overdraw. `HankoSealStamp` needed nothing — its `scale`/`opacity` are already driven by `SwipeableFlashcard`'s drag, which *is* the press-down.

### [2026-09-25] The Statistics Screen Now Says What It Measures
- **`[📊 DATA]` Reported:** *"look at the two statistics sheets that appear (one in the deck and one in the settings), what is actually shown as statistics and are there issues?"* — then *"fix all of six, make the design look consistent with what the rest of the app, make that the statistics in the setting screen show overall the app and the statistics in the deck specific screen show only for this deck"*. The two surfaces are one widget: the deck's *Statistics* tab and the profile's "My Progress" route both render `DeckStatsView`, differing only in `deckId` (null = whole library).
- **`[🔍 SCOPE]`** The deck tab is now titled with the deck's own name (`deckName:` passed from `deck_detail_screen`) instead of "Library Mastery" over numbers that were that deck's alone; the library entry keeps "Library Mastery" and its "My Progress" bar, and its empty state says "No cards available" rather than "in this deck". Nothing crosses the boundary either way — the provider filters it, and the tests now assert both directions.
- **`[🔧 FIX]` Six measurement defects:**
  - **Mastery was Reading-only.** The ring and legend bucketed with `card.isMastered(StudyMode.reading)` while the retention card below counted all five modes, so a word with forty calligraphy attempts read as "New Ink". Now word-level: mastered once ANY mode graduated it, learning once any mode has attempts, new only while nothing has been attempted.
  - **"Tricky" ranked by how often you *saw* a word.** Sorted by attempt count first, a word drilled thirty times at 100% led a word failed six times out of six. It now ranks by failed attempts, then by the shakier rate.
  - **"Reviews" counted words.** `dueToday` and the forecast counted each word once (by its earliest mode) while the card is titled *Upcoming Reviews* and the caption said "N Reviews". They now count **slots** — one per scheduled mode — and the headline is literally the first bar of the same forecast, so number and chart cannot disagree.
  - **The seven-day chart had no numbers** (`barTouchData: enabled: false`, rod titles off): each bar now prints its count under its weekday, FittedBox-guarded, with the axis reservation scaled for 2× text (a two-digit count does not fit the ~31px a bar owns at 2× on a 320px screen).
  - **"Average attempts per word" divided by every word**, so 150 characters with ten drilled reported 0.3; it is now the mean over the words actually practised.
  - **The filter missed the deckless cards.** `stats_controller` matched `deckId == deckId` only, while `deck_detail_screen` and `FlashcardController.getCardsForDeck` both also accept `deckId == ''` into the Main Library — a deck's Statistics tab could disagree with its own card list. The fallback is applied here too.
- **`[🧹 DEAD STATE]`** `reviewedThisWeek` and `longestIntervalDays` were computed on every pass and rendered nowhere (0 UI references): both dropped from `StatsState`, the controller and `props`.
- **`[🎨 UI]`** Kept on the app's own tokens rather than new ones — `AppTheme.accentOf`/`cardBgOf`, the book screen's ink well (radius 18 + Emperor's Gold hairline), `NotoSerifSC` card titles, the Cinnabar/Jade/Gold tone vocabulary and `MaterialLocalizations` weekdays honouring the locale's first day of the week.
- **`[🧪 TESTED]`** `deck_stats_insight_test.dart` gained a `statistics definitions` group (mastery across modes; one word with three tracks due ⇒ `[3, 1, 0…]`; failures-based tricky ranking; the Main Library's `deckId == ''` fallback asserting 2 / 1 / 3 for `default` / `hsk2` / library) and the changed expectation carries its reason (average 7.0 → 10.5 over the two practised words). `test/features/flashcards` = **115 passed**; analyzer clean on the touched files; `dart format` clean.

### [2026-09-25] Hero Flights - a New Shared-Element Pair, and One That Could Never Fly
- **`[🎨 UI]` Report:** only nine `Hero(` sites existed, and several card→detail chains had no shared-element flight at all.
- **`[✨ FEATURE]` New flight - the radical glyph** (`radical_library_screen` → `radical_detail_screen`). The deep-red radical in the shelf tile now flies into the 100px header box, on the frozen namespaced tag scheme: `HeroTransition.heroTag('radical_library', character)` on both sides. The scopes matter here: the radical character can also appear among its own matching characters on the detail screen, so the header's tag and any tile tag must not collide - which is exactly what the namespacing exists for.
- **`[🐛 FIXED]` The daily-story flight could never fly.** `story_library_screen` tagged its card `'daily_story_${dailyStory.hashCode}'` - an identity `hashCode`, which is not stable across runs, and which belongs to a *different* object than the `LibraryStory` the summary route receives. The two tags could therefore never match, and the `Hero` was dead code. It now uses `HeroTransition.heroTag('story_library', dailyStory.link)`, against a matching wrap around `StoryCoverArt` in `story_summary_screen`, so the cover genuinely travels.
- **`[🛡️ FROZEN]` Tier 1 respected:** no flight was re-timed or re-curved. Only tags were made to match, through the `HeroTransition` helper the standard mandates, and the frozen `'$scope::$id'` scheme is untouched.
- **`[⚠️ HAZARD FOUND, DELIBERATELY NOT "FIXED"]`** `dashboard_screen`'s quick-search bar carries `Hero(tag: 'dashboard_search_bar')` and pushes `DictionaryScreen`, which has **no** matching Hero - so it never flies. It must **not** be naively paired: `MainNavigationScreen` keeps all four tabs mounted simultaneously (a `Stack` of `_KeepAliveTab`s), so a `'dashboard_search_bar'` Hero inside `DictionaryScreen` would coexist with the dashboard tab's on the same route and Flutter would throw *"multiple heroes that share the same tag"* on every launch. Logged in `ISSUES.md` for a deliberate decision.
- **`[🧪 TESTED]`** `test/features/media` **90 passing** (only the 2 pre-existing `cultural_context_book_parity_test.dart` failures owned by another agent); `test/shared` + `test/core` **125/125** (including the Tier 1 frozen-tag assertion); flashcards `character_detail_localization` + `dictionary_expansion_panel` + `profile_screen_book_parity` **13/13**. ### [2026-09-25] Error Shake - the Last Unimplemented Motion Token, plus Tile Press Feedback
- **`[🎨 UI]` Report:** `docs/UI_UX_STANDARDS.md` § Motion listed an error shake as the **only** Tier 2 row with no implementation at all: `ZenMotion.shake` was defined and referenced **nowhere**, so a rejected answer or a refused action could only recolour - never move.
- **`[✨ FEATURE]` New `ZenShake`** (`lib/shared/widgets/zen_shake.dart`) - a damped horizontal rejection built on `ZenMotion.shake` and `ZenMotion.natural`, driven by a monotonically increasing `trigger` counter so a caller never owns a controller:
  - Only an **increase** plays, which is what makes the "the rejected tile is the selected one" pattern safe: a tile that stops being the rejected one drops its trigger back to `0` and stays silent instead of replaying.
  - The final keyframe is exactly `0`, so the rest state is byte-identical to not being wrapped.
  - Reduced motion hands the child straight back with no transform; the caller's `HapticsManager` call carries the rejection on its own.
- **`[✨ FEATURE]` Wired into the two rejections that already had the haptic but not the motion:** `quiz_step.dart`'s lesson quiz (where `HapticsManager.error()` and the wrong-SFX already fired) and `radical_lesson_screen.dart`'s find-the-radical tiles (`HapticsManager.heavy()`). Each shakes only the tile the learner picked.
- **`[🖐️ FEEDBACK]` Two tiles moved from a bare `GestureDetector` to `BouncingButton`** - `quiz_step`'s answer cards and `radical_library_screen`'s radical tiles - so a press carries `ZenMotion.tap` plus a light haptic. Its `onPressed` is `null` once answered, which removes the re-tap guard from the widget body.
- **`[🐛 FIXED]` A real trap, caught by the new test:** as a lazy `late final` field the controller was first constructed inside `dispose()` under reduce motion - because `build` returns early and never touches it - which tripped `SingleTickerProviderStateMixin.createTicker`'s `TickerMode` assertion while the tree was being finalised. It is now built in `initState`.
- **`[🧪 TESTED]`** new `test/shared/zen_shake_test.dart` **4/4** (travels on an increase, settles exactly back to rest, a dropped trigger is silent, reduced motion never moves); `test/shared` + `test/core` **125/125** (motion guards included); `test/features/course` + `character_detail_localization` **11/11**. Every file in `lib` parses. Ratchets safe: `ZenMotion.shake` 0 -> 1, `BouncingButton(` 23 -> 26, raw `Curves.` still 1 (the frozen canvas), `ZenMotion.` 255 >= 240.
- **File(s):** `lib/shared/widgets/zen_shake.dart` (new), `lib/features/course/presentation/widgets/lesson_steps/quiz_step.dart`, `lib/features/course/presentation/screens/radical_lesson_screen.dart`, `lib/features/flashcards/presentation/screens/radical_library_screen.dart`, `test/shared/zen_shake_test.dart` (new), `CHANGELOG.md`, `ISSUES.md`, `SESSION_STATE.md`.

### [2026-09-25] A Reopened Study Session Keeps Every Card It Had Reserved
- **`[🐛 FIXED]` Reported:** *"I think there's an issue with the anki card, card to review today (or new card), it stop after only one card"*. Reproduced end-to-end: start a session, grade **one** card, leave, reopen — the second session served exactly that one card and then ended.
- **`[🔍 CAUSE]`** `StudyQueueBuilder` skipped every card that was `isNew` in the mode **and** already listed in that mode's `modeIntroductionKeys`, and those two conditions could only ever match a card the learner had *not* studied. Reserving writes `introducedAt` plus the day's `introducedCardIds` / `modeIntroductionKeys`, never `attempts` — so a reserved-but-unstudied card is still `isNew`. Since starting a session reserves the whole queue, leaving one early made every unfinished card unreachable for the rest of the day: the queue collapsed to whatever had actually been graded. Its sibling path had already reasoned the right way for reviews — *"A queued review is not a completed review… otherwise abandoning a session consumes the daily allowance and makes Review appear empty when it is reopened"* — so this was the same principle left unapplied to new cards.
- **`[🔧 FIX]`** `lib/features/flashcards/domain/logic/study_queue_builder.dart`: a card that is `isNew` but was introduced today — in this mode or another — now stays in the queue as an `alreadyIntroducedNew` card instead of being dropped. Its quota was spent when it was reserved (`newlyReservedCardIds` unchanged), its `introducedAt` is already today so it is never introduced twice (`cardIdsToIntroduce` stays empty), and `alreadyIntroducedNew` deliberately sits outside `newAllowance`, so the daily limit still caps only *distinct* new cards.
- **`[🧪 TESTED]`** New `test/features/flashcards/deck_review_session_resume_test.dart` drives the real `DeckReviewSessionScreen` over an in-memory mirror of `StudyActivityRepositoryImpl`, so the production builder — not a stub — decides the queue: a fresh day of 2 due + 3 new is served in full, and a session abandoned after one card still offers the other two once reopened. `study_queue_builder_test.dart` gained the builder-level case, and `study_activity_repository_test.dart`'s concurrent-reservation test now asserts the invariant it guards (the shared limit caps *distinct* cards handed out; the re-offered ones are those already reserved). Measured before the fix — `resumed gradings = 1`; after — `resumed gradings = 5`. `flutter test test/features/flashcards` = **111 passed**; `flutter test test/unit_tests` = **160 passed, 3 pre-existing failures** (2 in `stroke_matcher_test.dart`, 1 in `poetry_cover_manifest_test.dart` — neither touches this path); `flutter analyze` over the three touched files = **No issues found!**

### [2026-09-25] Tier 2 Motion Sweep - The Twelve Static Screens
- **`[🎨 UI]` Report:** all 61 `*_screen.dart` files were audited against the motion surface (`ZenMotion`, `Animated*`, `AnimationController`, `TweenAnimationBuilder`, `Hero`, `flutter_animate`, `*Transition` and the reusable motion widgets). 20 had no bespoke interface motion, and **12 of those had none at all beyond the global page transition** - the post-session reward screen among them.
- **`[✨ FEATURE]` Every screen without interface motion now has it**, drawn entirely from the existing Tier 2 vocabulary (no new tokens, curves or durations):
  - `session_summary_screen` (the reward screen) - the accuracy **counts up on arrival** with a `TweenAnimationBuilder` on `ZenMotion.arrival` (the standard's reward token; the `easeOutBack` overshoot is clamped so the reading can never print 103%), and the three result cards plus the primary action arrive on `StaggeredListItem`.
  - `daily_study_dashboard_screen`, `flashcard_form_screen`, `paywall_screen`, `ai_data_privacy_screen` (per-card), `contact_screen`, `auth_screen` - content reveal via `ZenFadeIn`, so the result eases in instead of snapping.
  - `radical_library_screen`, `radical_detail_screen`, `deck_card_picker_screen`, `course_screen`, `profile_screen` - grids, lists and rails assemble with the list-entrance vocabulary (`StaggeredListItem`: 400 ms entrance + 50 ms stagger per row).
  - `lesson_screen` - advancing a lesson step is now an `AnimatedSwitcher` on `ZenMotion.swap` / `ZenMotion.natural` (the standard's in-place content swap) rather than a hard cut.
  - `story_summary_screen` - the story cover reveals with `ZenFadeIn`.
  - `qa_screen` was left on its original `ExpansionTile` (a paren-balance error during the change was reverted, restoring the file to HEAD); it already animates.
- **`[♿ A11Y]` Reduced motion holds for every addition.** The two one-shot widgets drive their duration through `ZenMotion.of(context, ...)`, so the OS "Reduce Motion" setting collapses them to their end state; `ZenFadeIn` and `StaggeredListItem` return their child unwrapped. The shrink-only ratchets are **unchanged** (raw curves 1, raw durations 123, `.ms` shorthand 10, `MaterialPageRoute` 23) because no new literals were introduced.

### [2026-09-25] The Deck's Speaking Exercise Now Reports the Rating and the Tone Analysis
- **`[✨ FEATURE]` Reported:** *"when we choose specifically the practice exercise, we never get the rating and the tones deep analysis, this rating and tones analysis is made everywhere else where we speak"*. The practice path is `deck_detail_screen.dart` → `StudyModeSelectionSheet` → `DeckReviewSessionScreen(mode: StudyMode.speaking)` → `SpeakingModeWidget`, and its result card knew only a score and a band sentence: the grader's per-character `wordScore` and its verdict were parsed and then thrown away.
- **`[🔁 PARITY]` The rating now reads the way the live call and the shadowing studio read it:** the panel opens with the same `graphic_eq` header (`azurePronunciationAssessment`, an existing 14-locale key) and reports the **same three readings** the live-call verdict averages — `overallScore`, `toneAccuracy`, `fluency` — as tinted pills beside the score, so one graded utterance is legible in both places. `completeness` left the trio so the two readings match exactly.
- **`[📊 DATA]` Each character now carries its own score and verdict:** the row prints the grader's `wordScore` and badges the character `Correct` / `Partial` / `Mispronounced` / `Omitted` (existing 14-locale keys) in the documented palette — Jade `#2E7D32`, Emperor's Gold, Cinnabar `#C62828`, muted ink for a character the grader never heard. The section header invites the deep analysis with `tapToReview`, and the row still taps through to `ToneComparisonSheet`: the same pitch-contour graph, per-tone audio audition and localized "tone 2 vs tone 4" diagnostic the live call opens.
- **`[🛡️ HONESTY]` No invented tones.** A row whose tone data is missing — or a character that was omitted entirely — keeps its score and its verdict and **drops** the `Expected · nán` / `You Said · nàn` comparison (showing a neutral glyph instead of the graph) rather than plotting a tone the learner never said. This is deliberate: `GeminiService.gradeAudio` already synthesises `actualTone` when Azure reports none (`gemini_service.dart:2520` and `:2568`, logged in `ISSUES.md`), so the UI must not present a guess as a measurement.
- **`[🐛 FIXED]` A failed grade is no longer a dead end.** In speaking mode the rating is a swipe and it used to be gated on `_isRevealed || _feedbackResult != null` — so when grading failed (silence, no network, AI consent refused) the learner was left holding a card they could not rate, with the swipe hint hidden. Both `isSwipeEnabled` and the hint now share one `canRate` that also includes `_error != null`, so revealing the card *or* failing a grade unlocks the same swipe-to-grade the other four modes always have.
- **`[🧪 TESTED]`** `speaking_tone_feedback_test.dart` grew from 11 to **15 tests**: the rating header and the live-call metric trio; each character's score and its `Mispronounced` badge; a landed character badged `Correct`; the `tapToReview` invitation; and a source guard that a failed grade unlocks the rating. One existing expectation was updated on purpose (`Accuracy 80` / `Completeness 100` → `Overall Score 84` / `Tone Accuracy 80`) with the parity reason written into the test. `flutter analyze` over the touched files = **No issues found!**; `speaking_tone_feedback_test.dart` = **15/15**, and the wider suite `flutter test test/features/flashcards` = **103 passing with 1 file failing to load** — that one is `profile_screen_book_parity_test.dart`, blocked by another agent's in-flight `qa_screen.dart` syntax error (`:23`, `:256`), not by this session.
- **`[📁 FILES]`** `lib/features/flashcards/presentation/widgets/speaking_feedback_panel.dart`, `lib/features/flashcards/presentation/widgets/modes/speaking_mode.dart`, `test/features/flashcards/speaking_tone_feedback_test.dart`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `ISSUES.md`, `SESSION_STATE.md`.
- **`[⚠️ NOTE]`** `live_call_summary_screen.dart` still carries its own private `_buildAcousticScoreBanner` / `_buildMetricItem` copy of the rating trio, and both it and `tone_comparison_sheet.dart` are owned by other agents right now (dirty in the working tree), so **neither was touched** — the deck re-states the same readings instead. Extracting one shared `PronunciationMetrics` widget and pointing all three consumers at it is logged in `ISSUES.md`.


### [2026-09-24] Speaking Practice Now Shows the Tones
- **`[🐛 FIXED]` `[🎨 UI]` Reported:** *"The speaking practice doesn't let us see the tones things etc…"*. It was a real hole, not a matter of taste: `SpeakingModeWidget` graded with **Azure Pronunciation Assessment at phoneme granularity**, and `GeminiService.gradeAudio` already returned per character — `pinyin`, `expectedTone`, `actualTone`, the phoneme scores and a word verdict — yet the screen rendered **only** a coloured hanzi, a score and a sentence. Every tone the learner actually produced was thrown away, and the mode showed nothing about the target tones before speaking either. The one place in the app that does tone analysis properly (Echo Hall's `ToneComparisonSheet`, with per-tone contours and "tone 2 vs tone 4" diagnostics) was never reachable from a flashcard session.
- **`[📊 DATA]` The result panel is now a tone report** — new `SpeakingFeedbackPanel` (`presentation/widgets/speaking_feedback_panel.dart`, standalone so it can be pumped with a canned grader result):
  - **Score** with the three Azure metrics as pills (`Accuracy 80`, `Completeness 100`, `Fluency 90`), tinted Jade ≥80 / Gold ≥60 / Cinnabar below — the deck statistics tab's reading.
  - **The verdict from the catalogue**, not the grader's English: the four band sentences were already 14-locale keys (`perfectPronunciationSoundsLikeANati`, `greatJobAFewMinorToneInaccuracies`, `notBadButYourTonesNeedSomeWork`, `keepPracticingListenToTheNativeAudi`, `goodEffortKeepPracticing`), so the band is now chosen from the score and the English `overallFeedback` field is no longer displayed.
  - **A tone row per character**, tappable through to `ToneComparisonSheet`: the hanzi, its pinyin, and two pills that name **the syllables rather than digits** — `Expected · nán` against `You Said · nàn` — written with `PinyinUtils.getAllTonesForSyllable` and tinted with `PinyinUtils.toneColors`, the app's canonical tone palette. Feeding the comparison the grader's `expectedTone`/`actualTone` is what opens the tone graph, the per-tone names and the localized "tone 2 vs tone 4" diagnostic.
  - A word with no tone data still renders its row (no crash, no empty panel).
- **Before speaking, the target tones are on the card.** Each syllable of the pinyin gets a pill with its tone number in the tone's colour (`hàn 4`, `zì 4`), under the pinyin and gated by the existing `showPinyinInSpeaking` setting — so the learner can aim instead of guessing. It is placed inside the card's existing scroll area (and inside a `FittedBox` in the pre-reveal branch) so 2× text cannot overflow it.
- **Same screen, palette and language.** The verdict colours on the graded hanzi moved from `Colors.green`/`orange`/`red` to Jade/Gold/Cinnabar (matching the shadowing session's per-character verdicts); the error banner is the Cinnabar alert tint at radius 14 with an icon; the bare `CircularProgressIndicator` became `ZenLoader`; and the microphone button is the book-screen primary action (Deep Carbon Ink in light mode, Emperor's Gold in dark) turning **Cinnabar while recording**, with the waveform in the accent. Five hardcoded English strings went with it: `microphonePermissionRequired`, `recordingErrorPleaseTryAgain`, `recordingFailedNoFile`, `couldNotProcessYourRecordingPleaseT`, `holdMicToRecordReleaseToGrade` and `listening` — **all of them already existed in the 14-locale catalogue**, so this entire task added **zero new keys**. Failures now log their detail through `debugPrint` instead of pasting the exception into the UI.
- **`[🧪 TESTED]`** new `test/features/flashcards/speaking_tone_feedback_test.dart` (**11 tests**): the panel shows the score and every metric; it names the two syllables (`Expected · nán` / `You Said · nàn`) and marks a tone that landed; it uses the catalogue verdict and never the grader's English; all four score bands; tapping a row opens the tone sheet; a word missing tone data still renders; the card shows one tone pill per syllable before speaking; a de/ru/th/hi/ja sweep of the panel; and two source guards (no surviving English literal, no Material hue, `ZenLoader` present). The suite caught one genuine defect while being written — the graded hanzi still used `Colors.green`/`orange`/`red` — and one wrong expectation of mine (`nán` in tone 4 is `nàn`, not `nà`). `flutter analyze lib` = **0 errors, 0 warnings, 0 infos**, `flutter test test/features/flashcards` = **105/105**.
- **File(s):** `lib/features/flashcards/presentation/widgets/speaking_feedback_panel.dart` (new), `lib/features/flashcards/presentation/widgets/modes/speaking_mode.dart`, `test/features/flashcards/speaking_tone_feedback_test.dart`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `ISSUES.md`, `SESSION_STATE.md`.

- **`[🎨 UI]` Reported:** *"for when we choose specifically what thing we want to study in the deck like 'calligraphy, reading, recall' etc... the screen to choose is not nice in terms of design, it doesn't follow the design ideas we have set up, it must look more like the book screen or other screens in terms of design."* `StudyModeSelectionSheet` was the last deck surface on a palette of its own: five rows in five Material hues (`Colors.blue`, `.green`, `.orange`, `.red`, `.purple`), each with a `shade50`/`shade900` fill, a `shade200`/`shade700` border at radius 16, a coloured circular icon badge and a grey `chevron_right` — while every other picker in the app is an ink well.
- **Now it is the app's vocabulary, exactly as the deck picker and the voice sheet are**: `ConstrainedBox(maxHeight: 0.8 × screen)` + a shrink-wrapped `ListView` (so a short list hugs its content and a long one scrolls), a title row (`howWouldYouLikeToStudy`, 19px bold ink) with the `practiceModes` purpose line, an Emperor's Gold hairline at 0.3, then one 18px ink well per mode (`AppTheme.cardBgOf`, gold hairline, accent glyph tile, `BouncingButton`-style press feedback with `HapticsManager.selection()`) closing on the accent `play_arrow` affordance instead of a grey chevron. The five icons are the **same five icons the new statistics tab uses** for the same modes, so a mode looks like itself everywhere.

Both are now read from the catalogue and — worth noting — the five descriptions and five names were **already present in all 14 locales**, so this fix needed **zero new keys**: `calligraphy`/`reading`/`recall`/`speaking`/`listening1` and `practiceStrokeOrderWithVisualGuides`, `seeTheCharacterRecallThePinyinAndMe`, `seeTheMeaningDrawTheCharacterFromMe`, `readOutLoudToTestYourPronunciationT`, `listenToTheAudioAndIdentifyTheChara`.
- **`[📊 DATA]` The choice is now informed.** Each row derives two chips from the deck's own cards: how many cards of that mode are **due now** (`"3 · Due now"`, accent tint) and the learner's **retention** in that mode (`"Accuracy 67%"`, Jade ≥80 / Gold ≥60 / Cinnabar below — the same reading as the statistics tab). A mode with nothing due and nothing practised explains nothing and shows no chip. Both call sites (`DeckDetailScreen` and the daily study dashboard) now pass their cards in; the parameter is optional, so the sheet still works with no data at all.

### [2026-09-24] Deck Session: Finishing a Card Is One Motion Again
- **`[🐛 FIXED]` `[🎨 UI]` Reported:** *"during the deck: the animation when we finish a card is weird."* The deck review session renders **one route per card** and graded a card by `Navigator.pop(context, grade)` — but the route underneath was the session's **"Ready to study" preview**, so every single finished card did this: the card swiped off (stamp 130ms → 90ms pause → slide 180ms), then the **whole page** ran a full `MaterialPageRoute` transition to the preview card (a big *Start session* button and *Not now*), then the next card's route ran another full page transition in. Between two words the learner saw a Start button, twice per card, over ~1s of churn — on iOS the Cupertino builder slid the preview in from the left instead.
- **Fix 1 — the screen behind a card is now the session's paper, never the preview.** `deck_review_session_screen.dart` tracks `_sessionStarted` (set when the queue is reserved) and renders a deliberately empty surface while a session runs, so the swap reveals nothing between cards. The Start-button preview exists only before the first card. A widget test asserts the preview and `study_session_start` are **absent from the tree** while a card is open and after a grade.
- **Fix 2 — a card route, not a page transition.** New private `_CardRoute extends PageRouteBuilder`: the incoming sheet settles over **`ZenMotion.quick` (300ms)** with a 6% rise + fade on `ZenMotion.enter`, the outgoing leg leaves on **`ZenMotion.swap` (180ms)**, and both collapse under reduced motion (`context.reduceMotion` → `Duration.zero`, no travel). Nothing new was invented: both durations come from the shared `ZenMotion` vocabulary, which is what the motion standard requires of interface chrome.
- **Fix 3 — no dead time between cards.** The SM-2 write and the activity record used to be awaited *between* the pop and the next push, which is exactly when the preview was on screen. `_applyGrade` now books the card synchronously (SRS result, tallies, learning-phase re-queue) and chains the persistence onto a `_writeChain` future that runs alongside the next card; `_drainPendingWrites()` flushes it before the summary and before leaving an aborted session, so a review is never lost. Writes are chained rather than parallel, so two cards can never interleave an update to the controller's state, and a failed write logs instead of stalling the queue.
- **Fix 4 — the swipe's feedback no longer gates its animation.** `SwipeableFlashcard` did `await HapticsManager.medium()` **mid-sequence**, between the stamp and the slide-off. Haptics ride `SystemChannels.platform`; a `TestWidgetsFlutterBinding` answers no handler for it, so that await **never completed** in a widget test (and a stalled platform channel is not a timer, so nothing failed loudly — the card simply never advanced). The haptic is now fired with `unawaited(...)`, matching the other 30 `unawaited` call sites and the app's own habit of not awaiting `HapticsManager`. This was found by driving the real swipe in a test and probing each beat.
- **`[🌍 L10N]` Bonus defect in the same flow:** all four mode widgets (`reading_mode`, `recall_mode`, `listening_mode`, `speaking_mode`) hardcoded `"Swipe to Grade:"` and `"⬅️ Again ➡️ Good ⬆️ Easy ⬇️ Hard"` — English in all 13 other locales. Both now use the existing 14-locale keys (`swipeToGrade`, `again`, `good`, `easy`, `hard`), so no new strings were needed.
- **`[🧪 TESTED]`** new `test/features/flashcards/deck_review_session_transition_test.dart` (**5 tests**): grading a card never reveals the preview (before, during or after), the next card is laid out while the swap is running, a graded card is still persisted once the write is backgrounded, plus two source guards (the card route and the drained write chain). The suite drives the real gesture — reveal, then an explicit `TestGesture` drag — against a Hive-free stack (stub deck repository, stub activity repository, fake card controller, mocked `SharedPreferences` and a mocked `SystemChannels.platform` so a haptic can never silently stall a test again). `flutter test test/features/flashcards test/shared` = **139/139**, `flutter analyze lib` = **0 errors, 0 warnings, 0 infos**.
- **File(s):** `lib/features/flashcards/presentation/screens/deck_review_session_screen.dart`, `lib/shared/widgets/swipeable_flashcard.dart`, `lib/features/flashcards/presentation/widgets/modes/{reading,recall,listening,speaking}_mode.dart`, `test/features/flashcards/deck_review_session_transition_test.dart`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `ISSUES.md`, `SESSION_STATE.md`.


### [2026-09-24] Deck Statistics: No More Stray Back Arrow, and Numbers Worth Reading
- **`[🎨 UI]` `[🐛 FIXED]` The deck's *Statistics* tab no longer renders a screen inside a screen** (`lib/features/flashcards/presentation/screens/stats_screen.dart`, `deck_detail_screen.dart`). The tab mounted `StatsScreen(deckId: …)`, which is a whole `Scaffold` **with its own `AppBar` and therefore its own back arrow** — so the Statistics tab carried a second title bar and a duplicate back button above a screen that already had both, inside a tab that is not a route of its own. The view is now split in two compositions over one dataset:
  - **`DeckStatsView`** — content only (no `Scaffold`, no `AppBar`). The deck screen's tab renders it directly, so the stray chrome is structurally impossible rather than cosmetically hidden.
  - **`StatsScreen`** — the routed wrapper (Scaffold + "My Progress") kept for the profile entry, which is a real push and legitimately owns a back button.
  - A test source-guard pins the composition (`deck_detail_screen.dart` may only mount `DeckStatsView`).
- **Palette and vocabulary brought in line with the app.** The 2×2 “blue / orange / green / purple” stat grid, the purple-green donut and the blue bar chart are gone, along with `Colors.grey[850]` / `Colors.white` cards, 16px radii and hard black shadows. Everything now sits in one ink well (`AppTheme.cardBg*`, Xuan paper / dark card, Emperor's Gold hairline at 0.28, radius 18, soft shadow) with a single `_Palette` resolving accents (Cinnabar `#8B0000` light · `AppTheme.accentDark` `#FFCA28` dark), Emperor's Gold, carbon ink, muted ink, Jade `#2E7D32` success and Cinnabar alert `#C62828`; card titles use the app's `NotoSerifSC` and every new string is localized.
- **`[📊 DATA]` Real statistics, from records the app already had.** The old screen showed four card counts and a forecast bar chart while `StatsState` already computed `accuracyByMode` and every `ReviewStats` already carried `attempts`, `successCount`, `interval`, `streak`, `lastAttemptDate` and `introducedAt` — all unused. `stats_state.dart` / `stats_controller.dart` now derive (one pass, no extra I/O):
  - **Mastery ring** — mastered share in the centre, with New / Learning / Mastered counts, numbers *and* percentages beside it.
  - **Accuracy by mode** — an overall retention figure plus a bar per practice mode (`Calligraphy`, `Reading`, `Recall`, `Speaking`, `Listening`), tinted Jade ≥80%, Gold ≥60%, Cinnabar below, and a muted "—" for a mode the deck has never been practised in, so *"I always fail speaking"* becomes a fact rather than a hunch.
  - **Seven-day workload** — `dueToday` (words whose earliest due date has passed) and the forecast bars labelled with the locale's own narrow weekdays, starting on the locale's first day of week.
  - **Momentum** — words first introduced in the last seven days, all-time graded attempts, and average attempts per word.
  - **Tricky characters / Strongest characters** — the five words with the most attempts and lowest accuracy, and the five that reached the longest intervals; each row shows the hanzi, pinyin, meaning and its metric and opens the same quick-look sheet as the deck's card list (haptics + `showQuickLook`). Words never practised never appear.
- **`[🌍 L10N]`** added `trickyCharacters`, `strongestCharacters`, `newThisWeek`, `averageAttemptsPerWord` and `noCardsYet` to all **14** locales and regenerated (`flutter gen-l10n`); mode names, `accuracy`, `accuracyByMode`, `attempts`, `reviews`, `days`, `dayStreakCount`, `dueToday`, `myProgress`, `libraryMastery`, `newInk`, `learningStatus` and `masteredStatus` reuse existing 14-locale keys. No string is lower-cased for presentation (German "Versuche" must not become "versuche").
- **`[🧪 TESTED]`** new `test/features/flashcards/deck_stats_insight_test.dart` (**2 tests**: deck scoping — another deck's cards must not leak into the totals — per-mode accuracy, `totalReviews`, this-week intake, longest interval, average attempts, tricky ordering by attempts and strongest limited to graduated words, plus an empty deck reporting zeros) and `test/features/flashcards/deck_stats_design_test.dart` (**6 tests**: no `AppBar` and no back-arrow icon anywhere in the tab, the composition source-guard, mastery/retention/workload/word-list content, the radius-18 ink-well assertion, the empty state, and a de/ru/th/hi/ja sweep over 390×844 and 320×568 at 1×/2×). The sweep caught three genuine defects that would have shipped: `CrossAxisAlignment.stretch` on the momentum tiles measuring against an unbounded `ListView` height (fixed with `IntrinsicHeight`), a cramped header trailing that overflowed in German at 2× (the overall accuracy moved into the body as its own row), and the mastery ring's centre caption overflowing inside its fixed 132px box in Thai (the ring now scales with the text scale and the caption is shrink-wrapped). `flutter analyze lib` = **0 errors, 0 warnings, 0 infos**.
- **File(s):** `lib/features/flashcards/presentation/screens/stats_screen.dart`, `deck_detail_screen.dart`, `lib/features/flashcards/presentation/providers/stats_state.dart`, `stats_controller.dart`, `lib/l10n/app_*.arb` (14 locales), `test/features/flashcards/deck_stats_insight_test.dart`, `test/features/flashcards/deck_stats_design_test.dart`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `ISSUES.md`, `SESSION_STATE.md`.

### [2026-09-23] Article Simplification Repair - "Simplify failed: Simplification returned an empty article."
- **`[🐛 FIXED]` The in-app browser's *Simplify Article* / *Auto Simplify* works on real pages again** (`lib/core/services/gemini_service.dart`). Three defects combined; reproduced live against `deepseek/deepseek-chat` (a 1,482-character Chinese article failed on one run, a 1,674-character one on the next):
  - **The page text was never checked for Chinese.** `WebBrowserScreen` passes whatever `innerText` the selector found; on an English page that is English, and the old prompt then stated *"the source has 0 Chinese characters and 0 sentences"* while asking for *"0 to 0 Chinese characters"* - an instruction to return nothing. A live English page came back as a **2-sentence, 31-character "article"**, and a model that follows that arithmetic literally answers `{"sentences": []}` - precisely the *"empty article"* the reader saw. `simplifyTextToHsk` now refuses a source with fewer than 20 Chinese characters (`minimumSourceChineseCharacters`) **before spending a call**, with the actionable message *"No Chinese article text was found on this page."*
  - **One malformed answer discarded everything.** Any payload that was not a `sentences` array of `chinese`-keyed objects was thrown away whole - including a response the 8,192-token budget had merely cut off mid-JSON. New `_parseSimplifiedStory` accepts a top-level array and a nested `article`/`simplified`/`result`/`data` wrapper; `salvageSentences` recovers every complete `"chinese": "..."` pair from JSON that will not parse; `AiSentence.fromJson` also reads `simplified`/`text` as the sentence text.
  - **The prompt asked for more than the model could deliver.** It demanded **2x the source length**, which blew both the completion budget (JSON truncated -> parse failure) and the 90-second timeout (measured `TimeoutException after 0:01:30`). The rewrite is now asked to *stay at least as long as the source* (never shorter), the numeric length rule is omitted for sections without measurable Chinese, `"chinese"` is stated to hold Chinese characters only, and the call timeout is 150s.
- **`[🔧 FIXED]` A shorter rewrite is no longer fatal.** 90% (`minimumRetryRatio`) still triggers the corrective retry, but the new `minimumAcceptableRatio` (**35%**, the value the app shipped before build 559) is the fatal floor: measured against the real API a compliant rewrite of dense news prose lands between 30% and 60% of the source, and dropping a whole section for being condensed costs the reader more than a shorter-but-complete rewrite. `_ContentLossFormatException` (a `FormatException` subclass, so the screens keep stripping the type prefix) marks genuine content loss, so the chunk runner does not spend a third call on it.
- **`[🔧 FIXED]` One lost chunk no longer costs the whole article.** New `_simplifyChunkResiliently` retries a failed chunk once and reports it as a missing chunk; `simplifyTextToHsk` keeps every chunk that succeeded and fails only when *nothing* survived, reporting the real cause instead of an unexplained empty article.
- **`[⚡ PERF]` Sections are 700 characters (was 1,800).** Measured: the JSON is roughly 30x the Chinese character count, because the reader renders the prose from the per-sentence word arrays - a 504-character section finished at ~16k characters of JSON while an 875-character section hit the cap and was cut off. Smaller sections finish (`stop`) with full coverage, and the model stops summarising long input (a 474-character section came back at 111% of its source, a 1,200-character one at 16%).
- **`[🐛 FIXED]` A sentence with no word breakdown no longer renders blank** (`simplified_article_reader_screen.dart`): the reader composes its prose from `sentence.words`, so a repaired or differently-keyed sentence would have shown an empty paragraph. It now falls back to `sentence.chinese`, and repaired sentences carry one word per character so the text still renders and stays tappable.
- **`[🧪 TESTED]` New `test/core/services/simplify_resilience_test.dart` (7 tests):** a non-Chinese page is refused with **zero** HTTP calls; an empty `{}` answer is retried then reported (never passed through); a truncated payload still yields its complete sentences, each carrying the words needed to render; a renamed `text` field and a nested `article` object are read; **one chunk failing still delivers the other chunks (12 of 12 sentences)**; and the prompt no longer asks for a longer-than-source rewrite. New `test/features/media/simplified_reader_word_fallback_test.dart` guards the blank-paragraph fallback. `gemini_service_simplification_test.dart` (3/3) and `simplify_length_preservation_test.dart` (3/3, including "retries once, then gives up loudly") still pass.
- **Evidence:** live repro against the real APIs (scratch harness, removed afterwards) - before the fix a 1,482-character article answered *"Simplification dropped too much of the article."* and then `TimeoutException after 0:01:30`, while an English page was accepted as a 2-sentence article; after the fix the same article returns 9 sentences and the English page is refused locally with no HTTP call at all. **Still open (tracked as P1):** upstream `429` from `deepseek/deepseek-chat` and `503 UNAVAILABLE` from the `gemini-3.6-flash` failover, with no retry/backoff yet - chunks lost to those are dropped and logged.
- **File(s):** `lib/core/services/gemini_service.dart`, `lib/features/media/presentation/screens/simplified_article_reader_screen.dart`, `test/core/services/simplify_resilience_test.dart`, `test/features/media/simplified_reader_word_fallback_test.dart`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `ISSUES.md`, `SESSION_STATE.md`.

### [2026-09-24] "Generate from Deck": the deck chooser is a designed picker, and generation finally says it is working
- **`[🎨 UI]` Reported:** *"generate from deck scenario screen (where we choose the deck) is really ugly and doesn't follow the overall design of the app"*.
- **What was there:** the entry card was already on-brand, but the moment you tapped it the app opened a bare `SimpleDialog` — a stock Material title with `SimpleDialogOption` rows of plain deck names. No card counts, no level, no icon, no accent, no hairlines, no empty state: the one step where you *choose* looked like a different product. Worse, the generation that follows (`_isGenerating`) was **never rendered**, so after choosing a deck the screen simply froze for the duration of an AI call, and a failure produced a hardcoded English `SnackBar` (*"We had trouble generating this scenario. Please try again."*).
- **New `DeckScenarioPickerSheet`** (`lib/features/echo_hall/presentation/widgets/deck_scenario_picker_sheet.dart`), presented through the app's standard `GlobalBlurredBottomSheet` like every other picker: title + purpose line, gold hairline, then one ink well per deck (card background, gold-hairline border, 18px radius) carrying its **localized name** (`deck.localizedName`), its **card count** (`cardsCount`) and an **HSK level chip** derived from the deck id, with an accent `auto_awesome` mark whose tooltip is the localized "Generate from Deck". Decks with **no cards cannot be chosen** (they stay visible but dimmed), because an empty word list cannot seed a scenario. An empty library now explains itself (`no_decks_found` + `downloadOfficialDecks`) instead of showing a blank dialog. It resolves to the chosen `Deck`, so the caller no longer round-trips through a deck *name* and re-finds it with `firstWhere`.
- **Feedback and failure paths:** a blocking overlay (scrim + card with `ZenLoader` + `generatingYourScenario`) now covers the screen while the scenario is written, and a failure surfaces through `ZenToast` with the new localized `failedToGenerateScenario` instead of an English `SnackBar`. The screen's other untranslated literal (`"No scenarios found."`) now uses the existing `noScenariosFound` key.
- **`[🌍 L10N]`** added `generatingYourScenario` and `failedToGenerateScenario` to all **14** locales (native wording: *Création de votre scénario…*, *Dein Szenario wird erstellt…*, *シナリオを作成しています…*, *جارٍ إنشاء السيناريو الخاص بك…*) and regenerated with `flutter gen-l10n`. The insertion script validates the fragment and the brace balance before writing — after an earlier attempt refused the whole batch because the `.arb` files already contain keys differing only by case (`linGdp6`/`lingdp6`), which case-insensitive parsers reject while `gen-l10n` accepts them.
- **`[🧪 TESTED]`** new `test/features/echo_hall/deck_scenario_picker_test.dart` (**5 tests**: name + level chip + card count, an empty deck cannot be chosen (sheet stays open, nothing returned), a real choice is returned and the sheet closes, the empty-library state, and a de/ru/th/hi/ja × 320×568 × 1.0/2.0 sweep with a 40-character Russian deck name). `test/features/echo_hall` = **42/42**; `flutter analyze lib test` clean except two unused-import warnings in `custom_scenario_dialog.dart`, which is another agent's in-flight file and deliberately left alone.

### [2026-09-24] Mandarin Bean stories: the first screen now opens on the story's own cover, in the book screen's composition
- **`[🎨 UI]` Reported:** *"redesign the first screen of the mandarin bean stories, it should look similar to the book screen, use the image of the mandarin bean cover"* — the header showed *"a generic monochrome ink-wash mountain landscape, which has no contextual connection to a story about dumplings and tangyuan (饺子和汤圆)"*.
- **Four defects, found in order:**
  1. **Every story showed the same landscape.** `story_summary_screen.dart` rendered a 300px `SliverAppBar` band of `assets/images/ai_hub_ink_mountains.png` whenever `imageUrl` was null — and in the bundled dataset `assets/data/mandarin_bean_stories.json` **all 150 entries have `"imageUrl": null`**.
  2. **The artwork was already in the app.** `assets/images/mandarin_bean/` ships **134 covers**, named exactly after the article slug (`dumplings-and-tangyuan.jpg`); the data simply never pointed at them.
  3. **Key words never loaded for these stories.** The screen sent Mandarin Bean links (`https://mandarinbean.com/…`) to `fetchAndParseFirebaseStory`, i.e. a Firestore lookup by URL, instead of `fetchAndParseLocalStory`, which is the path that resolves the bundled JSON by link.
  4. **A debug-build crash on every story.** `initState` called `_isPlaceholder` → `AppLocalizations.of(context)` → *"dependOnInheritedWidgetOfExactType … called before initState completed"*; the summary enrichment moved to `didChangeDependencies` (once, guarded).
- **New shared `StoryCoverArt`** (`lib/features/media/presentation/widgets/story_cover_art.dart`): a portrait cover card in the catalogue's composition (135×190, radius 12, spine shading, shadow, scrim) that resolves, in order, the story's `imageUrl` (network or asset) → the bundled Mandarin Bean cover derived from the link slug → a **topic-aware** calligraphic card (glyph + gradient by category: Food & Dining is 食 on warm cinnabar, History 史 on jade, Science 科 on ink-blue…). `resolveProvider()` exposes the same order to screens that frame the cover themselves. **No code path can fall back to the generic mountains again.**
- **`StorySummaryScreen` rebuilt in the book screen's composition:** transparent app bar with an ink back arrow, centred cover card, title plus its original line, a badge row (HSK level / Native · subject, tinted with the cover's own hue · Mandarin Bean), one full-width primary action at `minHeight: 52` in carbon ink (light) or gold (dark), then "Summary" and "Key Words" ink wells at radius 18 with the `NotoSerifSC` family and a `ZenLoader` while the summary is enriched. The floating overlay button and the 300px photo header are gone; behaviour (story loading, Tang-poetry enrichment, remote-link web reader vs. local reader) is unchanged.
- **Covers everywhere else too:** the library grid cards, the "story of the day" hero and the cultural-insight header now use the real cover (the grid's category tint dropped from 0.4 to 0.18 so the illustration reads), and `StoryFetcherService` resolves relative image paths (`/wp-content/…`) against the article URL — a relative cover previously failed to load silently.
- **`[🧪 TESTED]`** new `test/features/media/story_cover_art_test.dart` (6 tests: slug derivation incl. case, null for non-article links, precedence order, topic glyph/gradient, "the dumplings story resolves to its own cover and never the mountains", "no artwork → its own subject card") and `test/features/media/story_summary_book_parity_test.dart` (3 tests: the Mandarin Bean → `fetchAndParseLocalStory` regression, the cover/CTA/card design assertions, and a de/ru/th/ja × 320×568/390×844 × 1.0/2.0 text-scale sweep — which caught a real 232px German overflow and a duplicated source label, both fixed). `flutter analyze lib test` = **0 errors, 0 warnings, 0 infos**; reading + media + shared + core suites pass apart from the two pre-existing `cultural_context_book_parity_test` assertions owned by another agent.
- **`[🧹 CONCURRENCY]`** a directory-wide `dart format` accidentally reflowed four files owned by other in-flight agents (`smart_media_desk_screen.dart` — which then tripped a `curly_brace` lint — plus `curated_shows.dart`, `cultural_context_provider.dart`, `show_progress_provider.dart`); all four were reverted verbatim, and only the seven files this task owns are formatted.
- **`[📌 NOTE]`** 16 of the 150 bundled stories (the news items the feed parser already filters out of the library) have no cover on disk; they now fall back to their subject card rather than a landscape.

### [2026-09-23] One voice picker for the whole app — with auditioning — and Settings on the app palette
- **`[🎨 UI]` The "Choisir la voix du livre audio" dialog was three different screens.** Settings opened an `AlertDialog` whose subtitles were the provider's own voice identifiers (`zh-CN-XiaoxiaoNeural`, `zh-CN-YunxiNeural`, …) and whose title read `Kore — Femme, chaleureuse (Azure)`; the reader opened a bottom sheet with **hardcoded English** labels and an `orange` quota warning; the audiobook player showed six code-named chips whose tooltips said `Kore — Azure quota exhausted`. None of them let you **hear** a voice before choosing it.
- **New shared component — `lib/shared/widgets/audiobook_voice_sheet.dart`:** an `AudiobookVoiceSheet` plus one `audiobookVoiceOptions(l10n, hasStudioQuota:)` catalogue that every surface now renders, so a voice added once appears everywhere with the same human name. It is built from the app's vocabulary — card surfaces, gold hairline, 18px ink wells, Cinnabar (light) / amber (dark) accent — and **auditioning is the point of the redesign**: each row carries a 44px ▶ control that speaks a Mandarin sample in that voice, shows a `ZenLoader` during the cloud round-trip, turns into a stop control while speaking, and resets from the engine's own `onPlayerComplete` event rather than a guessed duration. Auditioning never changes the stored voice; a second tap stops it; closing the picker stops what the picker started, and **only** what it started (an audiobook already playing is left alone — the sheet tracks whether an audition happened at all).
- **Technical clutter removed, not restyled:** no neural-voice ids, no `(Azure)`, no `Azure quota exhausted` tooltips, no `zh-CN-*` anywhere in the UI; the metered voices carry a lock and the allowance is explained once in words (`weeklyAzureQuotaReachedSwitching`); the on-device row is marked with an offline icon instead of a second copy of its own label. `auditionSample` is a documented, deliberately untranslated Mandarin line — the voices speak Mandarin whichever language the app is in.
- **Settings itself uniformised (`settings_screen.dart`):** every row icon used to carry its own Material colour — `lightBlue`, `orange`, `amber`, `purple`, `indigo`, `teal`, `pink`, `blue` — so the screen read as a legend. All rows, switches and slider thumbs now use the accent via small `_accent/_card/_gold/_ink/_muted/_alert` helpers, the cards are `AppTheme.cardBg*` with a gold hairline at radius 18, the section header family is the app's `NotoSerifSC` (was the platform's generic `Serif`), the scaffold is the Xuan-paper/carbon token (was `#121212`), and the destructive row keeps the documented Cinnabar alert. The notification sheet moved onto `GlobalBlurredBottomSheet` with the standard handle and hairline instead of a hand-rolled container.
- **The language picker now matches** (`app_language_picker_sheet.dart`): teal accent → the app accent, and the same ink-well rows as the voice sheet, so the two pickers in Settings read as one component. Its pinned keys (`app-language-list`, `app-language-<code>`) and 17px name size were preserved.
- **Reader and player call sites rewired:** the reader's compact voice chip shows the listener's name for the voice (was `Azure Neural: Kore`); the player's six chips became one pill that opens the same sheet, auditioning with **the sentence the listener is already on**. Its `h`/`Local` badges moved to Jade Green / Cinnabar.
- **`[🌍 L10N]`** added `tapToHearVoiceSample`, `soundEffects` and `soundEffectsDesc` to all **14** locales (the two sound-effect strings were hardcoded English in Settings), reused the existing 14-locale keys for the voice names, and regenerated with `flutter gen-l10n`.
- **`[🧪 TESTED]`** new `test/shared/audiobook_voice_sheet_test.dart` (**9 tests**: no engine identifiers in the UI, audition + completion-driven reset, stop-on-second-tap, selection reports the id, auditioning never selects, metered rows locked while the on-device row stays usable, accent+radius assertion, "closing stops only what it started", and a locale sweep over de/ru/th/hi/ja at 320×568 with 2× text scale — which caught and fixed a real 1px overflow in Russian, by making the whole sheet scroll as one surface). `flutter analyze lib test` = **0 errors, 0 warnings, 0 infos**.

- **`[🎨 UI]` The live shadowing flow was the odd one out.** `shadowing_studio_screen.dart` ran on a `Colors.orange` accent (26 sites), Material `Colors.green`/`Colors.red` verdicts, an M3 `colorScheme.surface` background, a Material-indigo target-character highlight (`#4F46E5`) and ad-hoc amber pills — while the hub, the reader and the book screens all use the Xuan-paper/carbon palette. The file already declared book-screen token aliases (`_bookBg*`, `_bookCardBg*`, `_bookAccent*`) for the hub; the session never used them.

  - **Live session** — Scaffold background → the shared surface; header title/subtitle → ink + accent, with a gold hairline under the bar; mic button → accent, **Cinnabar while recording** (a live state, not a second accent); grading row → `ZenLoader` instead of a bare `CircularProgressIndicator` (the standard forbids a section-level spinner) with a `Flexible` label for long locales; play/next icons → ink/muted; hint → muted.
  - **Phrase card** — grade card → `_cardOf`, 18px radius, Jade/accent border (was `Colors.grey[900]`/`Colors.white`, radius 24, Material green/orange); per-character verdicts → Jade Green / accent / Cinnabar alert / muted; pinyin, feedback and translation → muted ink; the translation's `fontFamily: 'serif'` → `'NotoSerifSC'`.
  - **Target-word highlight** — Material indigo `#4F46E5`/`#818CF8` → the canonical accent, the way the reader inks the sentence you are on. `test/unit_tests/shadowing_studio_context_test.dart` asserted the indigo value and now asserts `#8B0000`, with the reason recorded in the test.
  - **Session summary, deck picker and create-deck dialogs** — checkboxes, switches, loaders, add icons, "create new deck" and the underline field use the accent; primary buttons follow the book-screen convention (Deep Carbon Ink light / Emperor's Gold dark, with matching foregrounds) instead of a white-on-orange fill; sheet/dialog surfaces use `_cardOf`.
- **`[🛡️ COMPAT]`** every `l10n?.x ?? "…"` fallback in the session paths was **kept**: `unit_tests/shadowing_studio_context_test.dart` renders this screen without localization delegates, so only colours and the `fontFamily` changed there.
- **`[🧪 TESTED]`** `flutter analyze lib` = **0 errors, 0 warnings, 0 infos**; `test/features/live_translate` (configuration localization ×14 locales, hub layout/scroll, voice+speed) and `test/unit_tests` pass apart from three **pre-existing, unrelated** failures in untouched files (see `ISSUES.md`).
